#2889. Nvidia warns chip buyers of legal risks tied to China AI access, sources tell CNBC

Nvidia warned its customers that new legislation on Chinese companies remotely accessing its artificial-intelligence chips could expose it and them to U.S. "legal action," sources told CNBC.

The chip giant is facing increasing scrutiny from U.S. lawmakers over Chinese companies' access to its advanced chips, which U.S. export controls restrict from going to China.

Nvidia messaged chip buyers in recent months to caution that the proposed Remote Access Security Act, or RASA, would give the U.S. government broader authority to control access to cloud services in the same way as physical hardware, potentially exposing both parties to legal risk, according to two people with direct knowledge of the matter. The people asked not to be named while discussing private communications.

It also said that, for now, it would not sell its chips to customers who rent more than 50% of the graphics processing units, or GPUs, to Chinese companies, the people said.

Separately, the Financial Times reported earlier this month that Nvidia verbally told data-center clients to direct at least 51% of their services to non-Chinese customers under future contracts.

Regulators have had limited powers to control companies' access to those chips through cloud services hosted overseas.

RASA passed the House of Representatives in January but has not yet passed the Senate. Remote access to the chips has provoked the ire of President Donald Trump's administration, which has sought to keep the chips out of China to maintain the U.S. lead in the AI race.

CNBC reported on Nov. 5 that Chinese lab Moonshot AI sought access to Nvidia chips through data centers in Europe. The Trump administration previously singled the company out, accusing it in July of accessing the chips through centers in East Asia.

The U.S. Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security, or BIS, announced on Monday an interim final rule designed to prevent Chinese companies' remote access to AI chips.

Nvidia and BIS did not respond to a request for comment.

On Sunday, U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent called for a "dramatic shakeup" at Nvidia after Jensen Huang's shock departure as CEO, amid reports of an extramarital affair with a woman U.S. authorities reportedly suspect of spying for China.

Bessent suggested other Nvidia executives should face scrutiny over the chip giant's compliance with export controls.

"I want to keep coming back to what was the culture at Nvidia, and were they pressing these seemingly illicit sales to goose their revenues and their earnings?" Bessent told Fox News.

In an August securities filing, Nvidia said it sought to "strictly comply with all applicable export control regulators" but did not have "physical control of our products after sale" and relied on its customers' and partners' compliance programs.

Huang has been asked to testify before the Senate Banking Committee on Dec. 3.

#2878. Aschenbrenner’s Situational Awareness made AI bets again before October rout

Leopold Aschenbrenner's artificial intelligence-themed hedge fund Situational Awareness, which nearly collapsed in July, loaded back up on AI stocks as it returned to the financial markets at the end of the third quarter. But all of its holdings are down big the last month and a half.

According to a new regulatory filing, as the fund got back on its feet Aschenbrenner took an equity position in Australian neocloud company SharonAI valued at more than $440 million, along with equity stakes in AI-centric plays Cleanspark, IREN, Bitdeer Technologies and Fermi. The Cleanspark stake is worth almost $39 million, while Iren and Bitdeer stakes are more than $21 million and more than $20 million, respectively. Fermi, meanwhile, has a value of more than $11 million.

The fund also owned call options at the end of September in neocloud Nebius, memory chip name Sandisk, the Roundhill Memory ETF (DRAM) and data center power play Bloom Energy. Sandisk and Nebius were key holdings that almost took out his fund over the summer.

All of these Situational Awareness holdings have been routed since Sept. 30. SharonAI is at the top of that list, seeing a 58% loss in October. It's also fallen 11% so far this month. IREN was down 41% in October, while Nebius declined 35%. Last week, Nebius plummeted more than 16% in a single day after the company issued a weak full-year forecast.

It's not known whether the hedge fund manager got out of those positions during the October AI selloff. The 13F filing is just a snapshot of what the hedge fund held at the end of the quarter.

Aschenbrenner — who launched the fund in 2024 after rising to fame that same year with his AI manifesto also entitled Situational Awareness — re-entered financial markets a bit in September, sources told CNBC at the time.

The Monday filing shows he picked up right where he left off.

The near-collapse of his hedge fund over the summer forced him to sell all of its public equity positions to Ken Griffin's Citadel. The fund had $45 billion in assets at its peak, but that tumbled to around $10 billion, consisting mostly of private stakes.

Stocked tethered to the AI trade took a hit in the most recent quarter as investors grew concerned about a slowdown in development of the technology. Those fears were spurred by comments from industry leaders such as Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei and OpenAI CEO Sam Altman back in September, with the latter saying that the company going public this year would be "ill-advised."

Since then, pressure on the trade has only continued.

In recent weeks, investors have worried about how Democratic wins in the midterm elections could hinder data center development, whether some technology companies are using too much debt to finance the AI buildout and what role Nvidia will play in that financing with Jensen Huang now out as CEO at the company.

Losses in Nvidia as well as other AI-related stocks dragged down the three leading U.S. indexes during Monday's session as investors continued to assess the fallout of Huang's departure.

#2869. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2866. Trump’s accounts traded up to $97.5 million in Nvidia as Huang gained White House access

President Donald Trump has had plenty of reason to cheer the rise of Nvidia.

The chipmaker helped propel the stock market that Trump is fond of touting. Its billionaire co-founder, Jensen Huang, became one of the president's favorite businessmen. And by the end of last year, Nvidia had become one of Trump's three largest individual stock holdings, worth between $12.5 million and $58 million, up from between $615,000 and $1.3 million a year earlier.

Since returning to the White House, Trump's investment accounts have bought or sold Nvidia shares at least 103 times — transactions worth a combined $27 million to $97.5 million, according to a new CNBC analysis of his annual and periodic financial disclosures, which were most recently updated through August.

By midday Monday, Trump's Nvidia stake, based on the value he reported holding in August, would be worth $4.5 million to $57.7 million with stock priced around $157. But the filings give broad dollar ranges for holdings rather than the number of shares, leaving his current stake unclear.

Now, Huang's resignation as Nvidia's CEO on Thursday, following revelations about his relationship with a woman suspected of spying for China and reports of affairs with company employees, has put those trades back in the spotlight.

Among them was a sale of $500,001 to $1 million on May 18, the first Monday after Trump returned from a state visit to China with Huang. Five days earlier, before Huang boarded Air Force One during a refueling stop in Alaska, Secretary of State Marco Rubio had briefed Trump on intelligence concerning Huang's relationship with the suspected spy, The New York Times reported.

Trump on Monday denied being briefed. Asked about the reported briefing from Rubio, Trump said, "I haven't heard that," adding that he did not know whether the woman, Alice Huang, was a Chinese spy.

The president's financial accounts also bought smaller amounts of Nvidia shares in the days immediately before and after the May 18 sale, part of a broader trading blitz. Such flurries have defined his second term: Trump disclosed more than 21,000 trades in 2025 alone, compared with roughly 500 during his entire first term, according to CNBC's analysis.

A White House spokesperson told CNBC independent advisors manage Trump's portfolio without his input. Trump Organization spokesperson Kimberly Benza said the arrangement is designed to "avoid even the appearance of a conflict of interest." The disclosures offer no evidence Trump directed the trades or that his advisors had access to classified intelligence.

Trump has long used the stock market as a scorecard for the economy, touting its record highs — sometimes when highs have not actually been reached — despite the turbulence caused by his tariffs and the Iran war.

"Look, we have the highest stock market we've ever had," Trump told ESPN commentator and political talk show host Stephen A. Smith on Thursday when pressed about Republicans' midterm losses.

Nvidia alone accounted for about 13.5% of the S&P 500's 2026 gains through September, according to an analysis by Choate Wealth that looked at how chip stocks have driven the year's performance.

Huang's rise in Trump's Washington has been nearly as striking as Nvidia's on Wall Street. The two have met or appeared together at least 15 times since Trump returned to office, according to a CNBC review of public records and news reports. Huang in June told The Associated Press that Trump even "calls me in the middle of the night."

At Trump's Jan. 20, 2025, inauguration, several Silicon Valley titans occupied prime seats, but Huang wasn't there. The two men had never met.

That changed on Jan. 31, 2025, when Huang visited the White House, capping a bruising week for Nvidia: Chinese AI startup DeepSeek had wiped $593 billion off the company's market value in a single day, according to LSEG data cited by Reuters.

Two days before the meeting, Trump's money managers bought between $1 million and $5 million in Nvidia stock.

For Huang, much of the relationship hinged on access to China. In April 2025, the administration effectively halted sales of Nvidia's H20 chips to China, prompting a $4.5 billion charge, according to an SEC filing. Huang argued that withholding U.S. chips would only accelerate China's development of its own.

The administration eventually relented. After another meeting with Trump in July 2025, Huang announced from Beijing that Nvidia expected to resume H20 sales. A week after Trump confirmed an August 2025 deal calling for 15% of the revenue from the company's chip sales to China to go to the U.S. government, his accounts also bought between $5 million and $25 million in Nvidia stock, one of several large tech investments that day.

Huang was also becoming a fixture of Trump's international diplomacy, joining him in Saudi Arabia and the U.K. Nvidia, already a $1 million donor to Trump's inaugural fund, would pledge another $25 million to his planned White House ballroom on condition of anonymity, The Washington Post first reported.

But behind the scenes, intelligence officials first briefed Trump last fall on Huang's relationship with the woman suspected of spying for China, The New York Times reported.

Trump nevertheless continued to ease restrictions on Nvidia's business in China, announcing Dec. 8 that the company could sell its more powerful H200 chips. His accounts had sold as much as $1 million in Nvidia stock four days earlier and bought more the day after.

In the first eight months of 2026, Trump's accounts made another 38 Nvidia trades worth a combined $9.4 million to $28.3 million, according to CNBC's analysis. A disclosure released Oct. 8 — the day Trump awarded Huang the National Medal of Science — showed purchases of up to $1.6 million and sales of up to $1 million in August alone.

Trump stood by Huang as Nvidia's board weighed his future last week, urging him to fight to keep his job, the Times reported.

With Huang out and Nvidia's shares tumbling, Trump described him Thursday as a "tremendous talent" who was "going through hell" — in the same interview in which he boasted of the market's record highs.

Trump is already floating a comeback for Huang, telling reporters Monday that reinstating him "certainly isn't a bad idea." But Nvidia's board is not considering his return, a person familiar with the matter told CNBC. The person asked not to be named because they were discussing internal matters."

#2863. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

#2861. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2859. Anthropic likely to price IPO above the current range of $70 to $78, sources tell CNBC

Anthropic is likely to price its IPO above the current range of $70 to $78, multiple sources told CNBC's David Faber on Tuesday.

The artificial intelligence company is planning to close the order books and lock in total demand at 4 p.m. ET on Tuesday, according to the people, who asked not to be named because they were not authorized to speak publicly. Anthropic is slated to list on the Nasdaq under the ticker symbol "ANT" and start trading on Nov. 19.

If Anthropic's IPO proceeds as planned, the company will debut roughly five months after SpaceX went public in a record offering that raised $85.7 billion. But Anthropic is facing a public market that's grown increasingly skeptical of the AI trade, as well as mounting and widespread antipathy toward the technology in the U.S.

A group of researchers, including Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei, co-founded the company in 2021 after defecting from OpenAI, now its chief rival. Anthropic is best known for developing a family of AI models called Claude, and it underwent a period of explosive growth in 2026, driven largely by the popularity of its AI coding tool, Claude Code.

For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Anthropic said it generated $33.24 billion in revenue, up roughly 1,200% from the same period last year, according to its prospectus. It reported a $104 billion net loss during the nine-month period, though most of that was due to the change in fair value of convertible notes and derivative liabilities. Anthropic lost $25.1 billion over the same stretch in 2025, the filing said.

Anthropic has long worked to establish a reputation as a safety-conscious AI company, and its prospectus included more than 90 pages of risk factors for investors to consider ahead of its listing.

"I think if we do this right, we can create an age of miracles where humanity is able to solve problems that have eluded us for millennia," Amodei said in Anthropic's roadshow video. "We have to make sure that all the risks, all the ways that things can go wrong with this technology, we very carefully address those risks as we bring the benefits and as we have a viable business at the same time."

"I invite you to join us on the next phase of this journey," Amodei said.

#2858. Bam Adebayo leaves win over Rockets early with hip injury, Heat don't expect long-term issue

Bam Adebayo left Monday night's game against the Houston Rockets early with an apparent hip injury.

The Miami Heat star did not take the court to start the second half of their 128-110 victory over the Rockets at the Kaseya Center on Monday, and was instead replaced by Andrew Wiggins to open the third quarter. It didn't appear as if anything happened to Adebayo in the first half, but he was not spotted on the bench as play resumed.

Eventually, the Heat said he was questionable to return with right hip tightness. They then ruled him out for good in the fourth quarter. Further specifics of the injury are not yet known.

Adebayo finished the night with just four points and two assists while shooting 1 of 3 from the field. He entered the night averaging 19.5 points and 8.5 rebounds per contest this season.

Heat coach Erik Spoelstra said after the game that the team was being "proactive" in holding Adebayo out, and that he didn't expect it to be a long-term issue, according to the Miami Herald's Anthony Chiang.

"I saw how he was grinding through it in the second quarter," Spoelstra said, via ESPN. "No matter what he says and no matter what anybody says, let's sit him down and evaluate him tomorrow."

While they don't have specifics yet, losing Adebayo for an extended amount of time would undoubtedly make a major impact on the Heat. Despite losing him, the Heat still managed to roll to a convincing win over the Rockets.

Miami led nearly the entire way on Monday night. The Heat carried a 16-point lead into the locker room at halftime and just cruised the rest of the way to secure the 18-point victory. Giannis Antetokounmpo led the way with 20 points, 16 rebounds and 6 assists. Klay Thompson added 17 points after going 5 of 10 from the 3-point line, and Wiggins had 17 points and five rebounds after stepping in for Adebayo in the second half. The Heat have now won five straight, and sit at 8-6 on the season.

Alperen Sengun led the Rockets with 23 points and seven rebounds. Kevin Durant finished with 22 points, and Jabari Smith Jr. had 12 points and 10 rebounds. The loss dropped them to 11-4 on the year.

#2856. Falcons snap counts from another dreary Sunday against the Chiefs

It's snap counts time, whether you like it or not.

Beaux Collins getting 25 snaps was not something I expected, but then, the Falcons were down by a lot late. Collins got open enough twice in the end zone for Penix to look his way, but didn't come down with either target; the Falcons clearly like what he brings to the table enough to get him more involved at the likely expense of Zaccheaus and Blair going forward. This team is still searching for a reliable third receiver and Zachariah Branch is on injured reserve, so I'd still stump for seeing more of the reserve tight ends.

Otherwise, nothing here is really a surprise. These are familiar faces who just need to play better.

I thought we'd see more Brown and perhaps Bowman at safety, but Hellams did indeed step into Jessie Bates' spot and play nearly every snap. What's more, he was good, leading the team in tackles with no misses and acquitting himself in coverage on a day where most of the secondary got torched. I've long been a Hellams fan but thought he had essentially fallen out of this team's plans on defense; Hellams can play his way into a more significant role in 2027 if he keeps this up.

Pearce is still getting up to speed, and the team's edge rushers are providing sporadic pressure at this point; I fully recognize the job is tougher against Mahomes in the first place. Getting Pearce ramped up and Abdullah back in the rotation should help with that somewhat, but when Snowden is your most productive guy off the edge, you might be in a little bit of trouble. Zero offense intended!

Hughes has been effectively parked after struggling throughout the season, but this is now back-to-back weeks where Terrell and Henderson have been in hell, Terrell in particular. It's not completely out of the question for Hughes to find his way back into playing time if that continues, but the grimness of possibly benching one of your starters for a starter who was previously benched is not doing wonders for my mood right now.

A pretty successful day on special teams as the rest of the team fell apart, with Abdullah getting a massive workload. Moody delivered an onside kick and made all his tries, Bailey had a fine day punting, the coverage teams didn't allow any significant returns, and Blair had the play of the day grabbing the onside kick. The last couple of weeks have given me hope that this group is settling in, and the kicker change gives me more hope. I just hope "special teams were good" isn't the nicest thing I can say about this team the rest of the way.

The only other note is that the Falcons keep talking about numbers as a reason for parking player like Bowman and Avieon Terrell, the latter of whom was inactive this week; I'm not sure getting a handful of snaps out of TJ Quinn on special teams is worth sitting an effective defender.

#2853. Jensen Huang slated to appear before House panels Dec. 1; Nvidia lawyer due to testify Dec. 2

Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang will appear before the House Foreign Affairs, Intelligence and China Select Committees on Dec. 1, the leaders of those congressional panels said on Tuesday.

Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter will also appear before the committees on Dec. 2, the lawmakers said in a statement.

"We look forward to hearing from Jensen Huang and Tim Teter on their roles in a potential breach of our national security," said Reps. Brian Mast, R-Fla., John Moolenaar, R-Mich., and Rick Crawford, R-Ark., the chairs of the Foreign Affairs, China Select and Intelligence Committees.

"The American people need answers on Mr. Huang and Nvidia's role in allowing dangerous PRC actors to access our most advanced chips, as well as the role of a potential Chinese intelligence asset in this scandal," they said.

Huang resigned from Nvidia last week after revelations that he had an extramarital affair with a woman suspected by U.S. intelligence to be a Chinese spy. Lawmakers have since called for investigations into Huang and Nvidia to determine the extent of damage to the U.S. lead in artificial intelligence and national security.

The woman, Alice Huang, was an executive at Singapore-based technology company Megaspeed that Nvidia sold chips to — raising concerns in Washington that at least some of the chips were subsequently illegally smuggled to China.

Though Brown was bleeding, he eventually returned to the game with a bandage on his head, and played a few more minutes of the 76ers' 135-123 win over the Nets.

The collision between Brown and Randle happened in the fourth quarter of Monday's matchup in Brooklyn, with Brown's forehead connecting with Randle's mouth. Both men were bleeding after the incident, though from different parts of the head.

Brown stayed down for a while, rolling onto his back and covering his face with his hands. The entire 76ers bench soon came out to surround him while he was tended to by trainers. Brown then received additional care on the bench, before returning to the game with a bandage on his head.

#2850. Future Hogs Week 11: College football players who could help the Commanders

The college football games from Week 11 are unlikely to change the College Football Playoff landscape much because 11 of the top 12 teams in the playoff rankings won. Georgia easily dispatched of Missouri, Utah barely beat Arizona in one of the year's most exciting games, Alabama and its defense smothered Vanderbilt, and UCLA remained undefeated by beating Illinois. LSU's playoff hopes are over after Texas scored a last-minute touchdown to escape Baton Rouge with a win. The top team in the ACC, Miami, needed the second half to pull away from Duke. The only team in the top 12 to lose was Kentucky. The Wildcats just couldn't make enough plays to beat Florida. It's likely Texas Tech will replace Kentucky in playoff rankings.

After having their playoff hopes dashed, LSU could play spoiler in Week 12 by knocking Tennessee out of contention. The top team, Georgia, will go on the road to face South Carolina. Utah will also be on the road to take on TCU. UCLA will leave sunny southern California for Ann Arbor to face Michigan. SMU, who got blown out in Week 11 by Wake Forest, will look to bounce back against Notre Dame.

Here are a few players who had a good week and might be able to help out the Commanders. Let me know what you think about these prospects and feel free to post players that stood out to you:

While Boise State has been able to rely on its run game much of the year, Broncos' quarterback Maddux Madsen had his best game of the season. While Maddux is short for an NFL QB at only 5'10", he weighs 210 pounds. His height doesn't appear to affect his vision as he is able to find receivers all over the field. Madsen has enough arm to push the ball down the field, but it looks like he has to put a lot of air under his deeper passes. His passes are accurate with good ball placement. While he's not a dual threat, Madsen can scramble effectively and gain positive yardage with well-timed QB runs. The Broncos won a shootout with Oregon State thanks to Madsen's 371 yards and four touchdowns on 23 of 32 passes.

If you long for the days when the Commanders had a big back that was tough to bring down, Micah Ford might interest you as a prospect. After watching a few plays where he bounced off defenders or ran through them, I had to look up his measurables. Ford is 6', 225 pounds. That's a large running back that can dole out damage, but he is also a big target. He could remedy that by running less upright. At that size, Ford has good burst. He is most effective when he can get downhill immediately as his lateral quickness appears to be average at best. While he's not a huge receiving threat, it is a part of his game as he has 27 receptions on the year. Ford helped Stanford beat Virginia Tech with 27 carries for 149 yards and three touchdowns.

It is possible that Aidan Mizell is the fastest wide receiver in college football. He's topped over 22 miles per hour this season according Reel Analytics. His only competition might be his younger brother, Arizona receiver Isaiah Mizell. Back to Aidan, he is slight at 6'1" but only weighs 177 pounds. You can't hit what you can't see, right? Mizell uses his speed to get open. He regularly beats defenders deep downfield and tracks the ball well. UCLA will also manufacture touches for him because once the ball is in Mizell's hands, he can take it the distance. He has to show that he's more than just a speed merchant by running a greater variety of routes and using his route running to get open. The Bruins beat Illinois, and Mizell contributed six catches for 176 yards and a touchdown.

After Ben Sinnott's performance against the New York Giants, I'm not sure we need another tight end. Heck, just trade John Bates and Chig Okonkwo in the offseason and use those roster spots for other positions. Okay, maybe that's going too far…maybe. Adding a player like Eli Finley to the tight end room would make it better. He's got good size at 6'4", 246 pounds. Finley is a good route runner and has good athleticism with that frame. That athleticism makes him effective on and off the line of scrimmage. Once he makes a catch, Finley is a good runner and can be hard to tackle. As a blocker, Finley latches onto defenders with strong hands and drives them off the ball. Louisiana Tech scored 63 points in a win over Southern Missouri, and Finley had six catches for 104 yards and a touchdown.

Arizona suffered a close loss to Utah despite putting up 39 points. Although there was a stretch of the game where Utah put a ton of pressure on Wildcats QB Noah Fifita, Arizona still had a ton of explosive plays. Zach Henning was a big reason Fifita had time to get the ball downfield. Henning is stout in the passing game at 6'4", 310 pounds. He plays with a strong base and strong core that allow him to anchor against power up the middle, something the Commanders need considering the defensive tackles in the NFC East. He's a little inconsistent in the run game. Arizona uses a lot of inside zone runs. What he does best is work to the second level to find blockers, but he can struggle to generate movement at the point of attack. Henning wears #58 in the clips below.

I wanted to highlight Myles Bradley earlier this season. In Week 4, he had a game with 4.5 sacks. It just so happens that Colin Simmons from Texas had five sacks that week. Well in Week 11, Bradley had five sacks, bringing his NCAA-leading total to 14.5. One thing that we have to get out of the way. Bradley is a small edge rusher at only 6', 230 pounds. That's so small that he may only be a pass-rush specialist in the NFL. The other thing is Bradley plays defensive end in a 4-3. Can he drop to play in space as required by a 3-4 OLB? What he definitely can do is use his speed to beat tackles around the corner and bring down the quarterback. He uses that speed to set up an effective spin move. While his hand usage is good, you won't see many power rushes. To go along with his five sacks, Bradley had nine total tackles, a forced fumble, and two deflections in Bowling Green's win over Kent State.

It's been a tough year for Virginia, but they were able to get a comfortable win against California. The Cavaliers' defense only allowed seven points with Kam Robinson leading the way. For the second year in a row, Robinson got a pick-six against the Golden Bears. It's because Robinson is a smart player that can quickly react to an offense. He uses that skill in coverage. In zone, Robinson drops to the right depth quickly and closes space on receivers to knock the ball away or make a pick. His quickness helps him beat blockers to make plays in the backfield. At 6'2", 239 pounds, it'd be nice to see Robinson play to his size by taking on blockers instead of running around them. To go with that pick-six, the fourth of his career which ties a school record, Robinson had 12 total tackles.

The matchup between Utah and Arizona was an exciting affair that went down to the wire. With the game in the balance on a key fourth down, it was Jackson Bennee who made the deciding play. Although Utah gave up their fair number of passes to Arizona and their QB Noah Fifita, Jackson Bennee stood out in a major way. Utah plays Bennee in the slot and he's superb playing man coverage from this position. That's because Bennee is incredibly quick and changes directions with ease. It is surprising to see him do this at 6'2", 190 pounds. The blend of quickness, agility, and length allows him to stay attached to receivers throughout their routes. Utah lists Bennee as a safety, but he looks like a slot cornerback at the next level. That's largely because Bennee lacks stopping power as a hitter and tackler. The effort and willingness is there, but he'll have to be stronger in this area to play the slot in the NFL. Bennee had three tackles and two PBUs in the Utes big win over the Wildcats.

Texas needed all 60 minutes to beat LSU, but there's no way they would've been in a position to win without Jelani McDonald. Last week, mistamoe2 commented that the Commanders could use a top-notch free safety. McDonald might be that player. He stands 6'2" and weighs 201 pounds. The Longhorns trust McDonald to play numerous alignments from single-high safety to split-safety looks, to man coverage. In zone coverage, McDonald is incredibly aware. He trusts his eyes to take him to where the ball is going and is rarely wrong. In man coverage, he moves fluidly to stay with receivers throughout their routes and will look back for the ball to avoid penalties. His run support and tackling is another attribute of his. Although he's not the biggest safety, he is physical. McDonald had 11 total tackles, a PBU, and three interceptions to help the Longhorns take down the Tigers.

Which college players caught your eye in Week 11 and could help the Washington Commanders? Let us know in the comments below:

Nvidia’s top lawyer, Tim Teter, also agreed to testify.

#2848. Trump has arrived in Asia. Here’s what he said about Iran and Deutsche Bank

U.S. President Donald Trump has arrived for his high-stakes trip to Asia, and took a range of questions on subjects from Iran to Deutsche Bank leaking details about his tax.

The president landed in Manila, Philippines, for the initial leg of his trip on Tuesday and answered rapid-fire questions from the press while meeting with other leaders.

Asked about a possible Iranian threat to Washington, D.C., he said that, if there were an attack, Iran would "pay a price the likes of which no country has ever paid before."

The question was about CNN reporting that U.S. intelligence suggested the country was planning to target senior members of Trump's administration with a dirty bomb or chemical weapon in the U.S. capital.

"We take it very seriously because they happen to be crazy," Trump said. CNBC has not independently verified CNN's report.

But the president also said he believed his administration was "very close to getting it done" when it came to the Iran war.

While Trump was airborne, the Financial Times reported Deutsche Bank had mistakenly leaked details of his taxes in March 2017, during his first presidential term. The mistake was kept from the White House at the time, per the FT.

Asked about this in Manila, the president said: "I paid a lot of tax."

"They shouldn't have done it. It's illegal for them to do it," he added.

Trump was also asked about Nvidia's announcement on Tuesday of a new National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee. The company, which reports earnings later on Tuesday, is reeling from the sudden departure of CEO Jensen Huang, who resigned last week after having an affair with a woman U.S. intelligence reportedly believes to be a Chinese spy.

"It's a great company. It's a great idea," Trump said.

Trump is due to fly to Shenzhen, China, where he is scheduled to meet Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin for a three-way meeting on Thursday, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit.

He is also due to hold a separate bilateral meeting with Xi. Asked by a reporter if he would have a separate meeting with only Putin, Trump said: "I don't know, but I would, if it's available, I would do that. He's coming. I mean, it's really with the guests. I am the guest of President Xi."

Trump met the leaders of Australia, India and Japan as part of the Quad grouping in the Philippines and the prime minister of Thailand. He is due to China on Wednesday.

Speaking at a dinner with Southeast Asian leaders as the event wrapped up for the day, Trump touted his record during his second term, citing investment into the U.S., trade deals made with countries represented by attendees at the dinner, energy infrastructure buildouts and America's AI industry.

Trump, who has led a campaign to rename artificial intelligence "super intelligence," or "SI," told leaders around the table that the U.S. is "leading the world" in the technology.

"We have the world's most powerful SI ecosystem," he said. "We're leading everybody by a lot, and we hope to keep it that way, and we will keep it that way."

He added that the U.S. has "the strongest and most capable military on the planet," pointing to investments in the armed forces and the country's defense sector.

"We make the greatest equipment. You saw that in Venezuela. You see it in Iran. You see it everywhere," Trump said.

#2846. Is there concern about Nick Bosa upon his return from injury?

The San Francisco 49ers defense struggled on Sunday against one of the best offenses in the league, as they gave up 40 points to the Dallas Cowboys.

Following the game, there were two main talking points about the defense: the cornerback struggles and the up-and-down pass rush. A lot of the pass rush focus centered on Nick Bosa, who has been a bit quieter statistically since returning from injury.

Bosa has yet to record a sack this season in four appearances, while having eight tackles. Is there any concern with the star pass rusher coming back from the nagging injury that kept him out for nearly two months?

"I see Nick working his way back," head coach Kyle Shanahan said on Monday. "I think that he has a number of plays that look just like Nick, and I think he's had a few plays that doesn't. I think Nick's ready to go, and I think he's definitely affecting the quarterback and doing some good things out there. But Nick's going to get better the more he gets back with us. He's taken a lot of time off with his injury.

"He had a lot of time off before that, going into Australia with his injury over the summer and everything like that, or from last year, so he hasn't gotten to play a lot of football. But I still see a guy out there who's a top pass rusher, who does a lot of good things. He's got to handle the majority of the chips, which doesn't give him as many opportunities on the edge. But I'm not worried about Nick. Nick's going to get better each week, and he'll eventually get there."

As a whole, the 49ers sacked Dak Prescott just once on Sunday after the Indianapolis Colts and Arizona Cardinals sacked him three and four times, respectively, over the two weeks prior. How did Shanahan see his unit as a whole?

"I thought we definitely rushed the passer better earlier on," Shanahan said. "Obviously, we didn't get any sacks, but I thought we affected the quarterback. I thought we hit him. I thought we wore down as the game went. I wouldn't have said it was a bad pass rush game by any means if I never watched the fourth quarter. But, by the time we got to the fourth quarter, I thought we had a chance, it was 38-19 with like nine-and-a-half minutes to go, and I thought we had a number of chances to get them off the field. And they stayed on for whatever the reason.

"Everyone had their moments in that, but then I thought we really died out and the pass rush disappeared. I thought all 11 did, but mainly our D-Line. A lot of them hold on it way too long. That was too much time with those wideouts running around. We've got to be able to finish better. We've got to clean some stuff up. I thought there were some times that we did rush them real well and Dak [Prescott] did a good job getting rid of it. Sometimes they had some chippers that we need to rush better on because we didn't get there at all. But for the whole part of the game, we've definitely got to do better."

When you look back on the film with Bosa's pass-rushing snaps, it's evident he's constantly getting double-teamed and chipped. That's more an indictment of the rest of the room than Bosa himself, as teams aren't fearing the pass rush elsewhere and instead are focusing game plans on San Francisco's star.

There have been a couple of seasons where Bosa's sack production hasn't told the full picture. Bosa had 69 pressures in 2024 with an elite 20 percent pass-rush win rate, but had only (I know, only!) nine sacks that season while playing 14 games.

In 2023, playing a full 17-game season, Bosa had 10.5 sacks, but an incredible 122 pressures with a 21.8 percent pass-rush win rate. Now, this season, in four games, Bosa has 13 pressures with a 16.7 percent pass-rush win rate despite the zero sacks. That's still a top-15 rate among edge rushers this year.

Bosa is still coming back from his injury, and the 49ers pass rush is still middling as a whole. Not one of their rotational defensive linemen has a pass-rush win rate over 12 percent outside of Bosa, which has allowed teams to chip him quite a bit.

Even with the doubles and the chips, Bosa still had five pressures on Sunday while getting his biggest workload of the year. Additionally, as I said in the three takeaways, yesterday's troubles felt more against the secondary than the pass rush as well.

There should be brighter days ahead for Bosa's sack production, but the underlying metrics and film are painting a more positive picture in his return from injury, at least from Sunday's game.

#2845. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2844. Homebuilder sentiment plunges to 14-year low, leading more builders to drop prices

Sentiment among U.S. homebuilders dropped to the lowest level in 14 years, as higher mortgage rates, higher costs for energy, materials and labor, as well as weaker consumer confidence, led to more builders cutting prices.

Builder confidence in the single-family housing market fell four points to 25, according to the National Association of Home Builders/Wells Fargo Housing Market Index, or HMI. That is the lowest since April 2012, when the market was just recovering from the housing crash and foreclosure crisis. Last November, the index stood at 38.

Mortgage rates pulled back slightly in October, but surged higher again last week. The average rate on the 30-year fixed hit 7.51% on Monday, according to Mortgage News Daily. Last year at this time it was 6.38%.

Of the HMI's three components, the index measuring current sales conditions fell six points to 28, and future sales expectations dropped two points to 30. Buyer traffic declined two points to 16 — its lowest level in 15 years, excluding the start of the pandemic in 2020.

"Builders are confronting multiple challenges in today's economy," Bill Owens, NAHB chairman and a home builder and remodeler from Worthington, Ohio, said in a release. "Stepped-up immigration enforcement is keeping legal workers off job sites, deepening labor shortages and driving up costs. Higher fuel prices are prompting subcontractors to raise their rates sharply, and with fewer buyers in the market, more builders are scaling back construction."

More builders are now cutting prices as they try to get buyers in the door. In November, 46% of them reduced prices, the highest share in the post-Covid period, according to the report. The share of builders using sales incentives also hit a post-Covid high of 68%. That was the 20th consecutive month in which at least 60% of builders used them.

"The housing market faces a range of supply-side challenges, including building material costs pushed higher by tariffs, an ongoing shortage of construction workers and excessive regulations that undermine housing affordability," Robert Dietz, NAHB's chief economist, said in the release. "Mortgage rates above 7%, softer consumer confidence and an uncertain economic outlook are holding buyers back."

In the three-month moving averages for regional HMI scores, the Northeast dropped four points to 34, the Midwest fell five points to 35, the South fell three points to 27, and the West posted a three-point decline to 23.

#2842. Embiid, Brown have big scoring nights as Sixers down Nets, sweep back-to-back

BROOKLYN — That second half is probably best forgotten, but that's another win stacked. The Sixers escaped with a 135-123 win over the Brooklyn Nets Monday night.

Joel Embiid put up a season-high 37 points shooting 13-of-22 from the floor along with five rebounds. Tyrese Maxey had a more reserved night, going for 22 on 7-of-12 shooting with nine assists.

Jaylen Brown hit some big shots again as he finished with 31 points for the second straight night, going 9-of-19 from the field and pulling down 13 rebounds as well. VJ Edgecombe once again hit a big three late, finishing with 14 points along with nine rebounds and four assists shooting 4-of-10 from the floor. Julius Randle led the Nets with 33.

LeBron James missed the second half of the back-to-back with lower back management, as well as Adem Bona and Dom Barlow.

The Sixers wasted no time trying to create fast breaks as Maxey picked off an errant pass on the first possession of the game for an easy dunk. Edgecombe ran quickly off the Nets' first miss of the night, missing his layup but Dean Wade was there to put it back. Embiid had a weird start, picking up his first basket of the game picking some cherries down the end of the court. He and Edgecombe picked up a silly backcourt violation as Embiid did not get down the court all the way, but Edgecome still threw it to him.

With that being said, Embiid made four of his first seven shots, all of them being pretty easy looks near the basket. He finally knocked down a three later in the quarter. In some very intriguing rotation news, Jabari Walker played some four next to Embiid, despite being the most reliable backup five option at the moment. Expectedly, Walker did slide down to the five when Embiid first subbed out of the game.

The Nets' shot diet was similar to start the game, scoring 10 of their first 15 points from the paint, and putting some size next to Embiid in Walker didn't help that effort all that much. Walker picked up a hustle bucket on the other end, but couldn't finish the quarter after picking up two fouls. Wade replaced him to finish the quarter at center.

After a quiet start to the game, Brown took over the offense with Maxey and Embiid on the bench, getting to the line a couple of times. Edgecombe also had a strong take to the basket as the quarter closed, helping the Sixers get out to a seven-point cushion.

Anfernee Simons continued his stretch of tough shotmaking with a contested midrange pull-up to start the second. The Sixers got down the floor quick on their next trip, and Brown was able to find Simons wide open in the corner for a three. Brown got Danny Wolf to foul him on a three-point attempt, then stopped Wolf on a post-up, helping Philadelphia build an extended lead for the first time of the night.

It was a bit of a relief to see the Sixers just look flat out better than their opponent, and, yes, it took one of the worst in the league to do so. Edgecombe hit Walker with a high-low pass, and that gave him easy driving access on the next possession. Brown hit a nice crossover to leave his defender in his tracks and get to the basket. Embiid once again tried the self lob off the backboard, and thankfully enough players got in the paint to prevent him from attempting a dunk.

Embiid settled into a mode of just trying to draw a foul every time down the floor, which he was able to do pretty easily. Brooklyn is tall at a lot of positions but center, ironically, isn't one of them. Embiid took 10 free throw attempts as he put 24 points in the first half.

If there was one complaint for the half, it was that Nick Nurse only played eight guys against this Nets team on the second leg of a back-to-back. They set themselves up well for the end of bench guys to get plenty of run late, but it would have been nice to see some Labaron Philon Jr. minutes. Brown came off a screen to drill a three off a side out of bounds as the half came to a close. But Nolan Traore was able to draw a foul just before time expired, cutting the Sixers' lead to 19.

Embiid picked up right where he left off, opening the second half with a turnaround fadeaway. Wade kept doing all the little things, fighting for an offensive rebound to keep a possession alive for a Brown three. Maxey hit a quick stepback three and found Brown for another corner threee. It was a great start to the half offensively that offset Brooklyn coming away with three points on their first four possessions of the third.

That streak did end for the Nets, but they were able to build the tiniest bit of momentum after Embiid got called for a transition take foul on Randle. The Sixers sandwiched that with a couple of turnovers. The bucket they came up with to stop it was Embiid's best look of the night, driving past his defender and spinning to the hoop for an easy layup.

On the very next possession, Embiid went up aggressively but got blocked by Michael Porter Jr. and hit the deck hard flat on his back. He remained on the ground for a bit, long enough for the Nets to get an open three and have Nick Nurse call a timeout. As bad as it looked, Embiid seemed more concerned arguing with the officials that he should have been fouled during the timeout, and did remain in the game.

Maxey hit a three coming out of that timeout, but the Sixers had started to cool down as Brooklyn continued to inch back in it. They came within six before Brown buried a much needed midrange pull-up. After a rare stop he was able to get to the line on the next possession, but split the pair.

To make matters even trickier, Walker picked up his fourth foul which looked pretty cheap, but the Sixers had already lost their challenge earlier in the quarter. Moe Wagner might have missed both free throws, but Kentavious Caldwell-Pope missed a three on the ensuing possession and Porter drilled one coming back down the floor. Walker made a great finish once again getting found by Edgecombe and also got to the line and split a pair. He pumpfaked a defender that left him all alone on the wing and he buried a three as time expired to keep the Sixers up by 10 after three.

After the quarter started with a Wolf three to bring it back down to single digits, Brown picked up a foul on each end to bring him to four on the night as well. Edgecombe had a really nice driving dunk, but the Nets kept getting closer as the Sixers couldn't stop Randle.

As Brown was turning around with the ball, he didn't see Randle right behind him and slammed into him pretty hard. A foul was called but Brown hit the deck pretty quickly and was down for quite a bit. He left the game for three minutes of gametime but was able to return.

Walker tried to slide over and take a charge, but got whistled for his fifth, bringing Embiid back perhaps a minute or two earlier than intended. The Sixers got very lucky on the ensuing free throws. After Wolf missed both he got his own offensive rebound and missed the putback layup right at the rim. Embiid knocked down a pair of midranges, but got called for his fourth foul trying to block the shot of Day'Ron Sharpe.

Sharpe ended up missing both of those free throws as well, and Embiid followed that up with another pretty driving layup. Brown seemed to be OK as he knocked down a midrange, but the Sixers just couldn't keep the Nets down. Randle got an and-1 and Traore got to the line to remain within striking distance.

After some good ball movement, Maxey found Edgecombe wide open on the perimeter for a big three, but Traore came right down the floor and hit a three of his own. Despite some second chances the Sixers came up empty on the following poessession and Egor Demin came flying right down an open lane for a dunk, prompting a timeout from Nurse with the Sixers up only seven.

Demin hit a three after another empty Sixers possession to cut it to four. Embiid got the ball in the corner and calmly pumpfaked his defender, took a dribble and buried a much needed three in response. Embiid made more threes in this game (two) than he had over the past three weeks. Wade made a great play to block Randle and after Edgecombe won a jump ball and got fouled, the Sixers were able to win this one from the free throw line.

#2837. Hornets vs. Lakers predictions: Odds, recent stats, trends and best bets for November 17

Preseason darlings of many prognosticators, the Charlotte Hornets (4-9) have struggled with injuries and a subsequent lack of offense. As a result, they head to Crypto.com Arena Tuesday night trying to halt a two-game skid. The challenge is a bit greater than simply looking to snap a modest losing streak. Their opponent is one of the NBA's best and hottest teams, the Los Angeles Lakers (12-2), who have won eight in a row. The Lakers are scoring a league-best 122.8 points per game and shooting 50.3% from the field, while the Hornets are producing just 99.1 points per game and shooting 40.0%.

Charlotte comes in off a 113-105 road loss to the Clippers on Sunday, its second consecutive defeat and eighth in 10 games. The Hornets actually attempted 18 more field goals than Los Angeles but shot only 38%, compared with 55% for the Clippers, while losing the assist battle 32-23. Coby White led Charlotte with 21 points on 7-of-13 shooting, and rookie guard Christian Anderson Jr. supplied a career-high 16 points. As mentioned earlier, injuries have complicated Charlotte's offensive problems, with Kon Knueppel (leg) and Dennis Schröder (soreness) listed out. Naz Reid, meanwhile, has been Charlotte's most productive scorer this season at 17.2 points per game while adding 8.2 rebounds, and Brandon Miller has averaged 15.3 points over his last 10 appearances.

The Lakers arrive in dramatically better form after extending their winning streak to eight with a 131-123 victory at Phoenix on Sunday. Luka Dončić erupted for 43 points in that victory and remains the centerpiece of an offense that has overwhelmed opponents throughout the opening month. Los Angeles has gone 9-1 over its last 10 while averaging 120.6 points, and Dončić has averaged 32.4 points in that span. Center Walker Kessler gives the Lakers a presence in the paint, averaging 14.5 points while shooting 68% from the floor, and Los Angeles also ranks among the NBA leaders in transition scoring. LA, though, has not been immune to the injury bug. Austin Reaves is out with a heel injury.

The teams split their two meetings last season, with each winning on the other's home floor. The most recent matchup came Jan. 15, 2026, when Charlotte stunned Los Angeles 135-117 at Crypto.com Arena, avenging the Lakers' 121-111 victory in Charlotte on Nov. 10, 2025.

#2836. UNC Basketball starter Neo Avdalas suffers wrist injury

The North Carolina Tar Heels faced their first big test last Friday, hosting Georgia in the Dean Dome. It didn't go as planned.

Unfortunately for the Tar Heels, they suffered their first loss of the season as the Bulldogs used a strong second-half push to earn the win. Now, the Tar Heels received news that they lost more than the game.

Starting wing Neo Avdalas suffered a wrist injury and is expected to miss an extended period of time, per Inside Carolina. Avdalas suffered the injury in the loss to Georgia and has been sidelined ever since. He had 14 points, three assists and two rebounds in that game.

Avdalas has been a starter in each of UNC's first four games this year, averaging 9 points, 3.5 rebounds and 3.8 assists. The Greek native was the first player to commit to Michael Malone in the transfer portal back in April and then earned his starting spot.

After four years in Greece playing professionally, he joined Virginia Tech last season and averaged 12.1 points, 3.1 rebounds, and 4.6 assists.

With Avdalas out for some time now, the Tar Heels will have to fill his role in the starting lineup. Freshman Maximo Adams has played really well in four games so far and could be a candidate to earn more playing time for the Tar Heels.

North Carolina is set to host Marshall on Friday night.

#2834. Miami Dolphins’ Most Valuable Player | Week Ten | Youth

The Miami Dolphins have been going through a bit of a youth-movement this season, in case you haven't heard. They're the youngest team in the National Football League by a wide margin — with an average age of just over 25 years at the start of the season. They've also had the most snaps taken by rookie players out of any NFL team this season — leading the Cleveland Browns by a comfortable amount.

That emphasis on developing young players is paying off for the Dolphins in a major way. While the team is currently struggling in the standings — winning just one game on the year so far — their rookies are taking their lumps and learning the ropes at an expedited pace. That should help the team progress much quicker than most anticipate, while keeping their future cap situation in the good (and cheap) place.

One young player who has been excelling in Jeff Halfey's defensive scheme is rookie first-rounder Chris Johnson. Miami's young cornerback has two interceptions on the season after nabbing one against Daniel Jones of the Colts on Sunday. He returned that interception 59-yards for a touchdown — Miami's first pick-6 on the year. In addition to his two interceptions, Johnson also has a forced fumble during his rookie campaign.

Following the game, Johnson spoke about how coaching helped him make his stellar play on Sunday.

"Definitely felt good. Really just reading my keys. Once the quarterback scrambles, he's looking to get the ball out of his hands. That's kind of something we talked about throughout the week. When we get him off-schedule, it's an opportunity. Really just making the most of my opportunity in that situation."

#2832. Nets can’t finish comeback vs LeBron-less Sixers, lose 135-123

I went to watch my New York Giants host the Washington Commanders at MetLife this past Thursday. They gave up a game-winning score with less than a minute to go. Last night, as a proud Syracuse alum, I went to watch the Orange face St. John's at Madison Square Garden. They ended up losing by 17 after leading by three at half.

Desperately hoping to see a winning performance, I knew the Barclays Center was the place for me to go on Monday evening!

Yes, I'm being sarcastic … a bit cynical, too. As expected, the Nets lost tonight to the Philadelphia 76ers. However, they did battle, so if you haven't soured on morale victories yet, read on.

With Michael Porter Jr. returning from a one-game absence due to personal reasons, the Nets first five in game No. 13 featured MPJ alongside Nolan Traoré, Egor Dëmin, Julius Randle, and Day'Ron Sharpe. The Sixers began with Jaylen Brown, Tyrese Maxey, VJ Edgecombe, Joel Embiid, and Dean Wade. LeBron James sat this one out to rest his back on the back-end of a back-to-back.

Like Joseph Quinn and Chris Evans, Michael Porter Jr. and Julius Randle had the hot hand at different times tonight, but both did simultaneously in the early going. The pair of vets combined for 13 of Brooklyn's first 19 points to keep the Nets in it as Joel Embiid mismatched his way to 13 opening quarter points. They were also helped by Keon Ellis, who returned to the rotation after playing just seven minutes of garbage time vs Memphis on Saturday. Eager to stay out of the dump, he went 2-of-2 from deep in the period with two blocks and a steal.

But even as Ellis helped the Nets post 46/43 splits in the first, they still trailed 33-26 after it. At the other end, whatever the Sixers weren't getting on their first try, they were on their second or third. Philly outscored the Nets in second chance points during the opening frame 10-3. Jaylen Brown, still haunting us just in a new rival's uniform, pulled down three offensive boards to help out on that front. He also finished the game with 31 points, 13 total rebounds, five assists, a steal, and a block.

Nolan Traoré chose to take the blame for the weak defensive start postgame.

"Don't be passive in the first minutes of the game," he said. "I've got to be the one, the one pushing, the one putting pressure on them, and not the inverse. So yeah, that's it."

The Nets did improve on the offensive glass in the second, not because they boxed out better or went with a bigger lineup, but because their opportunities to defend their own glass vanished. The 76ers began the second shooting 9-of-11 from the field, eventually going 14-of-21 by the end of the period and 4-of-7 from deep.

Brooklyn, who shot a less impressive 12-of-26 from the field and 0-of-6 from deep, looked out of position both on defense and just as Philly's opponent in a competitive setting. Whenever the Sixers weren't swinging the ball around to stretch Brooklyn out and get favorable looks, they were hitting off isolation takes that just put a sweltering and oppressive spotlight on Brooklyn's lack of comparable talent.

In fairness, these are the "super" Sixers, even without King James. Remember when the Nets had a team like that? That was fun.

Anyway, Philly led 78-57 at the break. Randle led the team by a wide margin at that point, pitching in 18 points on 7-11 shooting. With eight points in the second, Traoré worked his way to 10 points and two assists on 3-6 shooting. However, the other young guards struggled in what was arguably their worst collective game so far. Mikel Brown Jr. and Egor Dëmin combined for zero points on 0-6 shooting and two turnovers at half. Brown only made one basket the whole game, going 1-7 from the field, but did drop five assists with only one turnover.

Dëmin's first points of the night didn't come until the 6:25 point of the game, and although tardy, their timing was appreciated. After Randle kept Brooklyn hanging around with another eight points in the opening half of the third, Porter Jr. and Day'Ron Sharpe teamed up for a block on Embiid that sprung a transition opportunity, leading to a Dëmin three that cut the deficit to 10.

And after that, Michael Porter Jr. picked up the baton. He got 12 of his 25 points for the game in the third to bring Brooklyn even closer. A large percentage of his makes came via the dribble-handoff sequences Brooklyn often runs for him, but the biggest was a straight up heat check, which made it a six point game with about a minute to go…

After those six blanks in the second, the Nets had gone 5-7 from three in the third. They also bounced back on the glass to win by a 13-6 margin in the period.

"I think we just played harder," Traoré said. "We grabbed more rebounds, even if it wasn't enough, but it was just like a team effort to get stops together and then run."

Traoré enjoyed another solid night, contributing with a career-high 23 points alongside eight assists, two steals and just two turnovers while shooting 7-12 from the field and 2-4 from deep. Randle agreed with his assessment that the Nets played harder in the second half.

"Just punch first," he said. "Like in the second half, we punched first, and set the tone. I think we have to start every game like that punch first approach."

Randle was right about that and just about everything tonight. He had his best game as a Net, finishing with 33 points, six rebounds, an assists, two steals, and a block while shooting 13-20 from the field and 2-4 from deep.

The Sixers went two for one down the stretch of the third, with the latter offensive possession generating a Jabari Walker three at the buzzer to put them back up 10 entering the fourth. But the Nets were far from discouraged. Danny Wolf got the scoring started there with a drive-and-kick three from Noah Clowney. Julius Randle added on in transition shortly after that got it down to five with 9:54 to go.

But down the stretch, Philly started spamming the Joel Embiid x Tyrese Maxey pick-and-roll, which was as calming for the Sixers as it was effective in producing points. Bit by bit, Philly inched further ahead to lead 122-111 with 2:56 remaining.

But while quiet for most of the game, Egor Dëmin did have something to say before Philly could ice things. After the Sixers had slowly but surely rebuilt a double digit lead, Dëmin quickly and impulsively brought it back to two after sneaking inside for a finish at the rim before trotting down on the next possession and sinking a pull-up triple with 1:14 to play.

It felt a bit like the Orlando game last year, only this time, the visiting team's All-Star hit the big three to essentially end it a few seconds earlier.

The next time down the floor, Embiid splashed a corner three, which pushed the lead back up to five with 52 seconds remaining. In a monster game, the Philly big man finished with 37 points, five rebounds, an assists, a steal, and two blocks while shooting 13-22 from the field. Dëmin finished tonight with nine points, four assists, and three rebounds while shooting 3-10 from the field and committing five turnovers. He and Brown Jr. were still a combined of 12 points on 4-of-17 in 44 minutes.

"I'm just trying to do the best for the team, whatever's in front of me," Dëmin said. "I don't know. I'm just trying to enjoy the game, go out there and hoop with my friends, and some days are like that, some are different, and hopefully I can get more of the good days then bad days, so kind of trying to approach it with this kind of attitutde."

To stave off the comeback, Philly only need to take care of business at the line. Edgecombe and Maxey handled all the paperwork, going 7-8 from the line as the clock ran out.

It wasn't just finisher move from Philly, but another lesson for the Nets in what they need to patch up. With Brooklyn's offensive renaissance in the second half, they ended up shooting better from deep for the game by nearly five percentage points. They shot worse from the field by just over one. However, they left 12 points at the line, shooting 22-34 for the game.

"I don't want to go to the players and go, 'yeah, make free throws,' and then you go there and you're like nervous," Fernández said. "You know, obviously, I don't want to shoot 64%. If we shoot better, we probably have a bigger chance, but I know our guys are good shooters, and they're going to make free throws. We're going to concentrate and make sure we believe that all of them are gonna go in."

Final: Philadelphia 76ers 135, Brooklyn Nets 123

Julius Randle collided with Jaylen Brown in the fourth quarter took a shot to the face. He stayed in the game, but didn't mince words about it postgame. Nevertheless, we highly doubt he'll miss any time.

"It feels terrible, to be honest," Randle said. "But I'm fine. It just went to track on me, he turned, and didn't see me. Just kind of collided, but it's fine."

Julius Randle tied his season high with 33 points. It was his second 30-point game this season.

Day'Ron Sharpe recorded five assists in his sixth straight game, extending the longest such streak in Nets history.

Nolan Traore tied his career high with 23 points. It was also his high for the season thus far.

The Nets play the Chicago Bulls Wednesday night at Barclays Center, starting at 7:30 p.m. EST. It will be Nic Claxton's first return to Brooklyn after the three team June trade that sent him and a second rounder (Isaiah Evans) to Chi-Town and Julius Randle and a first (Joshua Jefferson.)

#2831. “Aaron Rodgers is not good enough’: Analyst has harsh message for Steelers quarterback

The Pittsburgh Steelers lost 38-18 to the Cincinnati Bengals on Sunday Night Football in a game that was even more lopsided than the final score would indicate. Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow completely carved through what had been a well-respected Steelers defense coming into the game, finishing with 409 yards passing for four touchdowns.

As poorly as Pittsburgh's defense played, things were hardly any better on offense. Veteran quarterback Aaron Rodgers ended the game with 143 yards and two touchdowns, but nearly all of his production, including both touchdown drives, came in the fourth quarter with the game well out of reach.

On his 4th and 1 podcast, former NFL MVP Cam Newton did not mince words about the sort of quarterback he believes Rodgers is at this point in his career.

"That was embarrassing, to say the least. The Bengals destroying the Steelers," Newton said. "I just feel like Aaron Rodgers is not good enough to carry a team anymore. And when you look at DK Metcalf, it's extremely disappointing. And just looking around the board, going especially versus a Tee Higgins, a Ja'Marr Chase, a Joe Burrow, it's like you pick your poison with those guys.

"The Steelers on the other hand, it's only but so much a defense can do. It's only but so much Aaron Rodgers can try to create. I'm watching this game in sheer disappointment. Not that it messed up my picks, because it did. They could have done whatever they wanted to do it."

Newton went on to point out just how poor a job the Steelers did with possessing the ball with Rodgers at the helm.

"You get out-possessioned as far as for the time of the possession because you cannot sustain drives anytime you're going against a high potent offense," Newton went on. "You see the Bengals come down, move the ball effectively, efficiently, and all of a sudden they get points. To combat that, they at least move the ball and get, and they stall out in the red zone. The Steelers go three-and-out. They get the ball again, and then they go down there and score. Now, it's 10 to nothing while y'all still over here saying, 'Oh man, it's too cold out here.'

"That's how you get ransacked, slapped upside your head, punched in your mouth, kicked in your gut. Just a poor display of football. I wouldn't have expected this from a bad team. This was for first place in the AFC North. So, it wasn't like it wasn't a meaningful game. This was an extremely meaningful game that both teams needed to be at their best. One team showed up while the other was still in the parking lot talking about, 'Hey man, where my keys at?'"

As harsh as Newton's assessment may be, it's almost impossible to argue that he's off the mark after Pittsburgh was outgained by over 250 yards to lose first place in the AFC North. At 6-3, the Steelers and Rodgers are still in the playoff picture, but Rodgers and co. will need to find answers fast to have nay hope of ending the Steel City's drought of playoff wins.
