#2830. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2828. Lions' Aidan Hutchinson confirmed to have torn ACL, ending his 2026 season

Fears that Detroit Lions edge rusher Aidan Hutchinson suffered a torn ACL in his right knee were confirmed after additional exams, according to The Ringer's Tom Pelissero. Though tests reportedly revealed no other significant damage to Hutchinson's knee, he is expected to miss the remainder of the 2026 season with the injury.

On Tuesday, Lions head coach Dan Campbell confirmed that injury, saying "it stinks" and that Hutchinson will come out of it better on the other side.

Hutchinson injured his knee on the Lions' first defensive series versus the New England Patriots on Sunday in Munich. After sacking New England's Drake Maye, Detroit's fifth-year star jumped in celebration and landed awkwardly on his right leg. Hutchinson immediately went to the turf. As teammates and players approached, he gestured to the front of his right knee.

Campbell added Tuesday that he will not speak with Hutchinson about celebrating plays. Campbell said he didn't want to take emotion out of the game.

Hutchinson reportedly underwent an MRI after Detroit's 35-10 win that showed a torn ACL. Lions head coach Dan Campbell said the defensive end would undergo further testing when the team returned to the U.S. before making an official announcement.

"We'll know more when we get back in the States," head coach Dan Campbell said postgame, via the Detroit News' Nolan Bianchi. "Anytime you lose a player of his caliber, the type of teammate he is, he's a captain of ours, that doesn't make things easy."

Meanwhile, a fan captured video of Hutchinson in a wheelchair at the airport prior to the Lions traveling back to Michigan on Sunday.

Maye sustained an injury to his ankle on the same play and the Patriots fear the QB may have suffered a fracture that would end his season. The third-year star stayed in Germany for additional testing that could determine if he needs surgery, Patriots head coach Mike Vrabel confirmed during a radio interview Monday.

In nine games this season, Hutchinson recorded 6 sacks and 24 total tackles in addition to 1 pass defended and 1 forced fumble.

Hutchinson's torn ACL is the second serious, season-ending injury that he has sustained during his NFL career. During the 2024 season, he suffered a broken fibula and tibia in his left leg during a Week 6 win over the Dallas Cowboys. Hutchinson recovered to play in all 17 games last year, compiling 14.5 sacks.

The Lions' 2022 first-round draft pick (No. 2 overall) out of Michigan, Hutchinson has been named to two Pro Bowls and earned second-team All-Pro honors during his career. He's compiled 49 sacks in 65 games with 200 tackles and 5 interceptions.

Last year, Hutchinson signed a four-year, $180 million contract extension (including $141 million in guaranteed money). That extension kicks in next season following the fifth-year option the Lions picked up this year.

#2827. Diving deeper into Cavs 126-115 win over Bulls: Cavaliers are discovering hidden depth

CLEVELAND — It wasn't the prettiest game, but the Cleveland Cavaliers did enough to get past a struggling Chicago Bulls team 126-115 thanks to a 39-point performance from Donovan Mitchell.

39 points on 16-of-25 shooting, 4 rebounds, 3 assists

Mitchell showed once again why he's one of the best scorers in the league. He repeatedly got into the teeth of the defense to create high-quality looks, going 11-for-14 on shots in the paint.

That was all the Cavs needed to get past the Bulls.

27 points on 10-of-18 shooting, 6 assists, 2 rebounds, 1 steal

Powell has a difficult task. His teammates are all slotted one or two roles higher than they would be on a more functional team. This often forces Powell to create in a cramped half-court and playmake for teammates that the defense is mostly fine with leaving open.

Despite those limitations, Powell has been a useful player this season, and was again on Monday. He went 7-for-9 on shots in the paint. A 0-for-6 shooting performance from three kept this from being an even better night.

17 points on 6-of-13 shooting, 3 rebounds, 2 assists

It's incredibly difficult to shift between roles as seamlessly as Proctor has done in the last week. He's gone from being the primary on-ball creator in the win over the Dallas Mavericks to being a mostly off-ball shooter and second-side facilitator alongside the veteran backcourt. Vastly different things are being asked of him depending on the role.

On Monday, Proctor was a good off-ball player. He initiated actions when the ball swung to him and wasn't afraid to shoot when he had openings. The Cavs need tertiary players who can attack a defense that's overloaded to stop dribble penetration from Mitchell and James Harden. Proctor has shown over the past few weeks that he can do that. He'll be a mainstay in the rotation as long as that continues.

Afterward, head coach Kenny Atkinson said that he's still figuring out what Proctor's role is when the team is fully healthy.

"It's starting to reveal itself," Atkinson said. "We all know when the playoffs come and all that they're going to take Don and James away, and you need that other ball handler to get in the paint and make plays."

Mitchell echoed that point when asked about Proctor's game. Someone who can ball-handle like that can help in the postseason

"If he's going to continue to score, then we'll take the buckets," Mitchell said. "And then if he makes the right plays, which he has been, it's just a plus for us."

This was the fourth straight game Proctor has scored 15 or more points.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Browns throwback shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cleveland gear HERE.

4 points, 8 assists, 4 rebounds, 6 turnovers

The Bulls desperately needed someone who could create off the dribble. Giddey is one of the few players who has the talent to actually do that. However, he wasn't able to do that here, resulting in the Bulls benching him down the stretch.

4 points, 6 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 1 block

Omier plays basketball like the linebacker at your college that you run into at open gym. Nobody wants to try to stay in front of a guy who's that physical and single-mindedly focused on running through whoever is standing in their way. And they're stronger than everyone else they're playing with, which makes it even more unpleasant.

Unlike this annoying guy you run into at open gym, Omier can actually play basketball.

It's incredibly difficult to make it in the league as a 6'5" forward — especially if you don't have superb on-ball skills. Omier can shoot some, but nobody would say he's a stretch big. He is, however, a tank that is willing to bulldoze his opponent to get to his spots.

Omier did that here. The Cavs were sleepwalking through the first quarter with some pretty uninspiring basketball, then Omier entered the game and changed that by simply moving people out of the way.

"He's got a magnet for hands, strength, quickness to the ball," Atkinson said. "I know it's just two games, but it's pretty impressive."

Guys like this are incredibly important over an 82-game season. Sometimes you just need someone to come in and light a fire under your team.

"I'm just glad we don't have to box him out in camp anymore," Mitchell joked.

9 points, 10 assists, 4 rebounds, 1 steal

This was a good outing from an offensive process standpoint. Harden wasn't an efficient scorer (3-for-10), but he wasn't forcing the issue. More importantly, he kept the offense moving. He wasn't dominating the ball, was looking to hit outlet passes when they were available, and limited his turnovers (two). This led to him leading the team in plus/minus with a +15.

"This is starting to be similar to last year where we're playing a lot of different guys. … Last year there's a real silver lining to that. We've learned a lot about our guys. … You want to win games while you're discovering these guys, and that's a good thing about these past two games that we found a way to win the game, but now we're discovering different players and different lineups."

– Kenny Atkinson on what he's learned about the team's depth

It's been an uneven start to the season. However, learning more about guys like Meleek Thomas, Proctor, and others could be a blessing. The Cavs have gotten a good look at how they handle actually meaningful minutes. These reps can be invaluable for guys trying to find their place in the league, and you can't replicate them anywhere else.

Mitchell went 8-for-10 on shots in the paint.

"I love when he gets downhill like that and in the paint," Atkinson said. "I know he's a great pull-up three-point shooter. Just when he gets in the paint for us, it just causes so much havoc."

This wasn't a remarkable game for Cleveland. The Bulls are simply a bad team. They don't have the talent to compete against skilled teams on a night-to-night basis — especially when they aren't hitting their threes. Putting them away with a relatively stress-free victory is exactly what you want.

There wasn't a whole lot from this game that you'd feel comfortable extrapolating further, but that's okay. Sometimes you just need to pick up wins when you play games like this, and that's what the Cavs did.

#2825. Readers Vote | Mohawk Valley's top Week 11 football performer

Welcome to the Observer-Dispatch reader poll for the Mohawk Valley's 11th Football Player of the Week for the 2026 season, sponsored by Carbone's at The Fitness Mill.

Week 11 of the 2026 high school football season - the championship weekend for Section III's playoffs - is in the books. Who performed best from the teams on the eastern side of the section?

Caleb Herringshaw joined Dolgeville teammate Wyatt Eggleston as a two-time winner last week and both are nominated again for their roles in the Blue Devils' Class D championship game victory.

Whitesboro senior Connor McDonald, Rome Free Academy quarterback Mark Barone, Vernon-Verona-Sherrill running back Gabe Wheeler, Camden Blue Devil Brayden Fuller, Notre Dame Juggler D'Marco Santiago and Dolgeville's Logan Comstock have also won reader voting this season. Eggleston became the first repeat winner in Week 8.

Listed below are this week's 13 nominees, and the polls will be open at uticaod.com and timestelegram.com until 10 a.m. on Friday, Nov. 20.

WESLEY D'IMPERIO, Morrisville-Eaton: Junior ran for 139 yards and three touchdowns, ran the second half kickoff back for a fourth touchdown, and narrowly missed a fifth touchdown with

WYATT EGGLESTON, Dolgeville: Senior ran for career-high 229 yards and two touchdowns on 30 carries, passing 1,000 yards in the process, as Blue Devils won their 22nd Section III championship and fifth in six years.

JAYDEN EMERSON, Vernon-Verona-Sherrill: Senior caught two touchdown passes, including the go-ahead score as the Red Devils beat New Hartford for the second time this season and won their first sectional football title in more than three decades.

CALEB HERRINGSHAW, Dolgeville: Senior ran for the first three touchdowns in Blue Devils 53-8 victory in section's Class D championship game, ran for 132 yards on 13 carries and went over 1,000 yards for the season, and contributed a sack and an interception on defense.

JAKE JONES, Morrisville-Eaton: Junior scored two touchdowns, one on a 36-yard pass from Noah Mudge that gave Mavericks a first half lead and the other on a five-yard run in the second overtime that proved to be the game-winner in Section III's eight-player championship game.

LEWIS JOSLYN, Morrisville-Eaton: Senior contributed seven tackles, one sack, one fumble forced and recovered on defense as the Mavericks repeated as eight-player sectional champs, and made a key stop in the backfield on a South Lewis two-point conversion attempt in the first of two overtimes.\

NOAH MUDGE, Morrisville-Eaton: Junior quarterback passed for 156 yards and one touchdown, ran for 146 yards, two touchdowns and a two-point conversion, and added 10 tackles and an interception on defense in 54-46 double overtime victory in Section III's eight-player championship game.

ADAM MUNZ, Vernon-Verona-Sherrill: Senior receiver caught four passes and the Red Devils' most valuable player award at Section III's Class B championship game.

SIDNEY ROCHE, Dolgeville: Sophomore lineman received Blue Devils' most valuable player award for Section III's Class D championship game.

EVAN TANNER, Dolgeville: Sophomore ran for 111 yards and two touchdowns on 12 carries, giving the Blue Devils a third 100-yard rusher in championship game victory over Cato-Meridian.

GABE WHEELER, Vernon-Verona-Sherrill: Senior ran for more than 100 yards for the eighth time this season and 16th time in his varsity career, and threw his first varsity pass for the go-ahead touchdown in Red Devils' win in sectional championship game.

DEAGAN WILLIAMS, Vernon-Verona-Sherrill: Sophomore ran for one touchdown and helped seal the victory for Red Devils' first sectional championship since 1992 by intercepting a New Hartford pass and running it back for a second touchdown in the final minute of the fourth quarter.

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.

#2823. Kayvon Thibodeaux could return to NY Giants, says NFL insider

"There is a scenario" in which free-agent-to-be edge defender Kayvon Thibodeaux returns to the New York Giants next season, according to NFL insider Jordan Schultz. Schultz made that statement during an appearance Monday on the 'Valentine's Views' podcast.

"I would have said no a month ago. I would have said no six weeks ago," Schultz said. "There is a real sentiment inside that building that Kayvon Thibodeaux has turned the corner. He's turned the corner emotionally, maturity."

Trade rumors have swirled around the 25-year-old Thibodeaux since the Giants made edge defender Abdul Carter the No. 3 overall pick in the 2025 NFL Draft. With Carter and Brian Burns, who is now out for the season with a torn ACL, it has seemed likely that Thibodeaux would leave the Giants for a bigger role and a bigger paycheck at the end of the season.

"I think Brian Burns has helped him from the outside looking in, and I've asked people what does that mean when a guy turns his corner? Does that mean he's playing better and there is a sense of that but there's also just more of a sense of urgency," Schultz said. "This was another really high pick. I think it was a top five pick and obviously has been someone that Giants fans have wanted more out of.

"You're starting to see it now and I do believe for the first time over the last let's call it year and a half, two years that there is a scenario in which Kayvon Thibodeaux is there, and I never would have thought that previously."

Statistically, Thibodeaux is not having a great season. He does not have a sack, has just three quarterback hits, per Pro Football Focus is averaging a career-low two pressures per game, and his PFF pass rush win percentage of 9.3 is the second-lowest of his career.

Yet, there is a sense that Thibodeaux does a lot of the dirty, unnoticed work that has helped the Giants' defense rank fifth in the league in yards and 11th in point allowed.

Will that be enough to keep him in New York? Maybe. If the Giants can find the money.

"It's really hard to pay three different dudes [at] essentially the same position the same type of money," Schultz said. "The Giants are not going to be able to pay three dudes $25-30 million dollars [at] essentially the same spot."

#2821. Nets’ comeback bid falls short against LeBron James-less 76ers

Few NBA teams can rival the 76ers in terms of star power these days, even when LeBron James is out of their lineup.

The Nets have a few intriguing present and future pieces on their roster, but they definitely are not among that select group of teams.

James was out of uniform for the tail end of back-to-back games, but the Nets comeback bid from a 24-point hole and an ugly first-half defensive performance ultimately fell short in a 135-123 loss Monday night to the reloaded Sixers at Barclays Center.

Philadelphia 76ers center Joel Embiid (21) fights for the ball with Brooklyn Nets forward Julius Randle (30) in New York, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. AP Photo/Yuki Iwamura

"We found the energy, we made the run, but we fell short," head coach Jordi Fernández said. "At the end of the day, especially against a team like that, you want to end up playing like this from the beginning.

"So I can say that I'm proud of the second half that we played. Now, our challenge is trying to put four quarters together."

Joel Embiid recorded a season-high 37 points, Jaylen Brown finished with 31 points and 13 rebounds, and Tyrese Maxey added 24 as the Sixers improved to 12-3. They are now 4-0 in games played without James, the four-time league MVP and the NBA's all-time leading scorer.

Julius Randle matched his season-high 33 points, Michael Porter Jr. contributed 25, and Nolan Traore tied his career best with 23 for the Nets (3-10).

Jordi Fernández ditched the supersize lineup he used in recent games, going back to Traore at the point while shifting Egor Dëmin back to shooting guard as Danny Wolf returned to a reserve role.

Porter was back among the starting unit after sitting out Sunday's loss in Memphis for personal reasons, replacing Noah Clowney.

Dëmin and Mikel Brown Jr., the team's past two lottery picks, remained on separate units. The linchpins of the organization's rebuilding plan came into the game having totaled just 18 minutes on the court together this season, with Dëmin missing six games due to a bone bruise and Brown subjected to a minutes restriction early in his rookie campaign.

Anfernee Simons buried the first five points of the second quarter to stretch a 33-26 lead to 12.

Philadelphia 76ers center Joel Embiid (21) shoots over Brooklyn Nets center Day'Ron Sharpe (20) during an NBA game in New York, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. AP Photo/Yuki Iwamura

The Sixers dominated boards early, building a 19-8 edge as the lead ballooned to 46-28 on a three-point play by Dean Wade barely two minutes into the period.

The Nets trailed by as many as 24 before halftime following a wide-open Maxey trey with a little over five minutes remaining.

The Sixers led 78-59 at intermission, just three off the Nets franchise record for points allowed in the first half set by the Knicks with 81 in 2023.

"We were getting scored on every single possession," Dëmin said. "That's not something we're proud of."

The Nets' 13-2 spurt helped them climb back to within 10 thanks to an and-one by Traore plus a Dëmin 3-pointer midway through the third quarter.

Brooklyn Nets forward Julius Randle drives past Philadelphia 76ers center Joel Embiid and guard Jaylen Brown on Monday, Nov. 16, 2026, in Brooklyn, N.Y. JASON SZENES FOR NEW YORK POST

Traore's weaving layup, a 3-pointer by Clowney and a bucket by Porter cut the lead to six with under three minutes left in the period. But Jabari Walker rattled in a 3-pointer at the buzzer for a 10-point Philly cushion, 105-95, entering the final quarter.

Two more long-range buckets by Wolf and Randle cut the Nets deficit back to five in the opening minutes of the fourth.

Dëmin knocked down another triple to eventually pull them within four with 1:14 to play, but Embiid responded with his second 3-pointer as the Sixers closed out the game.

"We were a more connected group in the second half on both ends of the floor," Randle said. "Maybe we should punch first. I think in the second half, we punched first and set the tone.

"I think we have to start every game like that, punching first."

#2819. Where Oregon's defense, special teams rank after Week 11 win

Facing a subpar Michigan Wolverines offense on Saturday, the standard for the Oregon Ducks was high defensively. If the Wolverines, who were held scoreless for the first 58:30 against Michigan State the previous week, scored more than a touchdown it would likely be considered some sort of disappoitment.

Well, good thing that wasn't the case. The Ducks allowed just seven points in the 31-7 victory, a fluky 70-yard rushing touchdown in which Wolverines running back Savion Hiter broke multiple tackles on his way to the end zone. But other than that, the Oregon defense locked down Michigan, both in the passing game and on the ground.

Considered to have above-average special teams units, Oregon performed well in that aspect of the game as well. Punter Bailey Ettridge, who a day earlier had been added to the Ray Guy Award Watchlist, did his thing on five punts. However, kicker Gage Hurych missed short from 46 yards out and isn't as reliable as he seemed earlier in the year, though it didn't take away from a strong special teams outing overall.

Let's take a look at where Oregon's defense and special teams rank nationally in various statistical categories after Week 11.

#2818. Detroit Lions plan to activate, play Kerby Joseph this week vs. Buccaneers

Detroit Lions safety Kerby Joseph has not played in a football game in 401 days, but it appears that counter will drop to zero this upcoming weekend.

On his weekly radio appearance on 97.1 The Ticket, Lions coach Dan Campbell said the expectations—barring any setback—is for Joseph to be activated from the Physically Unable to Perform list this week and play in Sunday's game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers.

"Barring anything else here negatively—which there hasn't been, he's progressed nicely here—the plan would be to get him going this week. Get him going and get him into Tampa Bay and let him play," Campbell said.

Joseph has been dealing with a knee injury that limited his play early in 2025 and was eventually painful enough to cause him to miss the final 11 games of the season. Since then, Joseph has been working tirelessly to build enough strength and stability in his knee for the Lions to be comfortable enough to put him back on the field. The Lions returned him to practice three weeks ago, and they've been encouraged by his progress.

"He looked a lot better than what I anticipated him looking (like)," Lions safety coach Jim O'Neil said a couple weeks ago. "So it was very encouraging. So now he's gotta stack multiple days. He's got to stack them, so today will be a big day for him, but he looked awesome yesterday at practice."

It's unclear how much of a role Joseph will have upon his return, as the Lions have a pretty deep bench at safety. Not only did Brian Branch return to the lineup a few weeks ago, but veteran Chuck Clark has started to play better, and Detroit did just trade for former Jets safety Andre Cisco—who saw the field on defense last week in Germany. Regardless, it sounds like Detroit will have their All-Pro safety back in some capacity.

On top of providing an update on Joseph, Campbell noted that center Cade Mays (ankle) is unlikely to play this week, but he's still very encouraged by how backup Juice Scruggs is playing.

"I don't see Cade being ready this week. There's a chance, but I'm not putting all my eggs in that basket," Campbell said. "And Juice has been doing a nice job for us. He's playing really good football. That whole o-line played dang good football the other day."

#2816. Sixers Bell Ringer: Sixers back in East’s top spot on back of big performances from Embiid, Brown

Sixers Bell Ringer Season Standings:Jaylen Brown – 3.5Tyrese Maxey – 3.5VJ Edgecombe – 3Joel Embiid – 1LeBron James – 1Anfernee Simons – 1Dean Wade – 1

The Sixers traveled to Brooklyn on Monday night for their second night of their back-to-back after defeating the Indiana Pacers Sunday. LeBron James sat out this one for rest, but the Sixers had the services of Joel Embiid. Embiid wasted no time making his presence felt, pouring in 13 first-quarter points to lead the Sixers, who led 33-26 after one.

The Sixers exploded in quarter No. 2 for 45 points to stretch their lead after two to 78-59. Embiid led all scorers with 24 points at the halftime break. Jaylen Brown posted a dominant half of his own with 19 points and 10 rebounds.

The Nets countered with a big quarter of their own cutting the lead to 105-95 after three quarters. A big three at the buzzer from Jabari Walker helped the Sixers stall the Nets' run.

Brown left the game early in the fourth quarter after sustaining a blow to the head from Julius Randle but would later return. The Nets got the Sixers' lead down to just four points with under a minute to go when Embiid knocked down a pull-up corner three with 52 seconds to go to give the Sixers the needed cushion. The Nets fought hard and made the Sixers play all 48 minutes to close out this game. The Sixers, who led by as many as 24 points, would go onto win by a final score of 135-123. The Sixers moved back to first in the Eastern Conference.

Joel Embiid: 37 points, 5 rebounds, 3 blocks, 13-for-22 from the field, 9-for-10 from the line

Joel Embiid put together his best scoring game of the young season thus far. He looks to have battled through the foot injury that has limited him over the last few weeks and is back moving as good as he has all season. Embiid found his touch in the midrange where he dominated the game, catching a rhythm in an area of the floor where he has made a living throughout his career. Embiid, who has really struggled from three this season, also knocked down a pair from beyond the arc, including a pull-up dagger with under a minute to go. A healthy Joel Embiid is a dominant Joel Embiid and he appears to be trending in the right direction physically — and the touch has returned as well.

Jaylen Brown: 31 points, 13 rebounds, 4 assists, 9-for-19 from the field, 10-for-13 from the line

Jaylen Brown put up a 19-point and 10-rebound double-double in the first half alone in this one. He continues to stack good performances and show why he is the All-NBA and All-Star performer that he has been for so many years. Brown poured it in from all three levels as he often does putting on a clinical scoring performance. It was nice to see him return late in the game after suffering the blow to the head earlier in the fourth quarter. Brown's 13 rebounds are a season high for him. Defensive rebounding is another area himself and the Sixers continue to excel, which was a large concern for the team overall coming into the season. The Sixers' unselfish brand of basketball continues to lead to wins and standout performances for all different cast members.

#2815. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2813. Tiger Woods to make long-awaited golf return at Hero World Challenge

Tiger Woods is ready to return to professional golf, and he's now set a date for when his comeback officially begins.

After multiple injuries and setbacks, Woods has committed to play in next month's Hero World Challenge with some of the PGA Tour's top players. This will be the first time Woods competes in a professional golf tournament in more than two years.

Woods hosts the annual event in the Bahamas, which is slated for Dec. 3-6. He was added to the event through one of three remaining wild-card spots in the 20-golfer field, according to tournament organizers on Tuesday, Nov. 17. The 50-year-old has been away from competition since The Open Championship in July 2024.

His comeback attempt ahead of last year's Masters was derailed by a DUI arrest and rehab stint stemming from a car crash in March that led to a plea deal and the suspension of his driver's license.

Tiger Woods said in a statement Tuesday night he is "stepping away" to "seek treatment and focus on my health." Woods' social media message marked his first extensive public comments in the wake of his rollover car crash and subsequent arrest for suspicion of DUI. Woods' prior dominance on the links cannot be understated. Take a look back at the 10 best moments of his career. Keep in mind, this is a subjective list.

10. "Better than Most" 2001 Players Championship Ask any golfer and they can tell you the exact putt that the words "Better than Most" come from. The iconic putt was great in and of itself, but it was made legendary by analyst Gary Koch's commentary, repeating "Better than Most" over and over with increasing enthusiasm as it got closer and closer to the hole.

9. 2000 U.S. Open Woods' dominance is well-documented, but it wasn't always. The 2000 U.S. Open was one of the first tournaments where fans started to realize just how much damage Woods could do. After leading by just one shot after the first round, Woods then kicked his game to another gear, leading by six after 36, 10 after 54, and 15 when all was said and done. Woods was the only player in the entire tournament to finish under par. He finished at 12-under.

8. Woods aces the 16th at 1997 Phoenix Open Often referred to as the "loudest hole in golf," the 16th hole at TPC Scottsdale is known for producing legendary moments. Of course, Woods had to add himself to that mix. Woods' ace in 1997 sent the crowd into an absolute frenzy, the kind of eruption that would make Shooter McGavin lose his mind.

7. Just short? 2005 Masters This is arguably the shot that Woods is most known for. Any golf fan can close their eyes and see the ball stop at the lip of the cup, flashing that iconic Nike logo, and leaving the crowd disappointed just moments before it takes one final turn into the cup and sending everyone into hysterics. The fact it happened at Augusta just added to the legend.

6. 2022 Masters Woods did not win this tournament, but Woods' career isn't all about the wins, it's also about the resilience, the ability to get back up when you get knocked down. Woods was involved in a pretty serious car crash just 14 months prior to this tournament. Somehow though, Woods still managed to make the cut at the Masters, extending his streak of consecutive Masters cuts made to 22, tying him with Fred Couples and Gary Player for the longest such streaks in history.

5. "Hello, world" 1996 Fresh off winning the U.S. Amateur for the third time in his young career, the golfing world was waiting in anticipation wondering if Woods would remain an amateur. However, it was clear that there was something in the works. After all, Woods' dad, Earl, had secured a five-year, $40 million deal with Nike, which in all likelihood, wasn't a spur of the moment deal. Woods' iconic "I guess, hello world" marked the beginning of what would become the most iconic professional career in golf history.

4. The "Tiger Slam" 2000-2001 Not many golfers can say they've won a career Grand Slam. Tiger Woods won all four tournaments in a year. Though some skeptics will say that it doesn't count since they didn't all happen in the same calendar year, those people don't have friends, so who cares what they have to say. Woods won all four major tournaments in a row, making him the defending champ at each of the biggest events in golf. That's like being an undisputed heavyweight champion in boxing, but probably a thousand times less likely. It's very likely that we never see another golfer win all four majors in a row. That's how insane this feat truly was.

3. 2006 Open Championship Though this wasn't the first or even the second time Woods would win the Open Championship, Woods' victory in 2006 was iconic because of what Woods was dealing with at the time. Woods lost his father, Earl, earlier that year. You can see the emotion on Woods' face after the winning putt as he shares an embrace with his caddie.

2. 1997 Masters Woods was under a lot of pressure prior to this tournament. He was the young up-and-comer that would bring in a new era of golf. But he'd never won a major, he'd yet to make a major impact on the sport. Then the 1997 Masters happened. He won by 12 strokes, and set a then-record four-round score of 270 (-18). Even more iconic was Woods' resilience in the tournament. Through nine holes, Woods carded a 4-over and was struggling immensely. He then shot four birdies and an eagle on the back nine, finishing the first round at 2-under. That was just a taste of all that was to come.

1. 2019 Masters By 2019, Woods was well past his prime. He wasn't the same dominant force that shook the Earth when he walked by. Woods hadn't won a major in over a decade. Fans had written him off, but Woods wasn't done. It was a flash from the past that had fans giddy with enjoyment. He didn't dominate like fans were accustomed to years prior, but he managed the win anyway. The best part was definitely him embracing his children behind the 18th green, the same place he hugged his parents after his win in 1997. It was really a full circle moment and an exclamation point on one of the greatest careers ever.

Tiger Woods said in a statement Tuesday night he is "stepping away" to "seek treatment and focus on my health." Woods' social media message marked his first extensive public comments in the wake of his rollover car crash and subsequent arrest for suspicion of DUI. But the 15-time major winner told SiriusXM PGA Tour Radio recently that he was "feeling better. No doubt about that. I've been training quite a bit."

Woods is also expected to play in TGL's 2027 season as a member of Jupiter Links Golf Club beginning next month. The PNC Championship, slated for Dec. 19-20 in Orlando, Florida, is the PGA Tour's annual father-son event that Tiger Woods and Charlie Woods have also been regulars at in recent years. They did not play in last year's event as Tiger Woods recovered from back surgery. Golfweek previously reported that PNC Championship organizers are holding a spot in the 2026 field in case Woods is healthy enough to compete.

Woods announced in October 2025 that he underwent a disc replacement surgery on his lumbar spine. It was the seventh back surgery of his career, and the second in about a year's time after he underwent microdecompression surgery in September 2024. Woods also needed surgery when he ruptured his Achilles in March 2025 while training and practicing at home.

Before the March 2026 incident that led to his arrest and decision to go to rehab, Woods had been working his way back with an eye toward playing in the Masters in 2026. He played in the TGL Finals on March 24 earlier this year.

The Hero World Challenge is a 20-player, limited-field event with no cut. Scottie Scheffler, Justin Thomas and Cameron Young are among the top golfers scheduled to play. Rickie Fowler and Gary Woodland also received special tournament invites along with Woods on Tuesday.

#2812. How the Bears’ defense grades after their bye week

The Chicago Bears had their bye week this week, using the weekend to recover and regroup heading into their final eight games of the 2026 season. All told, their 7-2 record is one to be proud of, and though their offense gets a lot of the national praise, their defense has certainly surpassed expectations.

In particular, the way the Bears' defense has played in the two games after they traded for Jeffery Simmons has been remarkable. Even before then, though, their defense was better than many anticipated, thanks largely in part to creative pass-rush scheming by defensive coordinator Dennis Allen and strong play from this offseason's additions. This defensive improvement has seen the Bears project as a likely playoff team by Cloudbet review and a potential winner of the NFC North for the second year in a row.

All year, I've been recapping how the Bears have done analytically based on PFN Impact grading. Coming out of the bye week, I wanted to recap how each of their defensive and special teams players have ranked at their respective positions throughout the course of the season.

Neville Gallimore: 68.8 (108th of 120 DTs)

Tyrique Stevenson: 63.3 (96th of 102 CBs)

As a team, the Bears rank No. 11 in the NFL with a 76.8 PFN Defense Impact grade after Week 10. They rank in the top ten in the league in percentage of drives allowing a score, lowest EPA allowed per play, and lowest success rate allowed.

The obvious standout here is Jeffery Simmons, though only two of his nine games have been played with the Bears this season. He only comes in second narrowly behind Quinnen Williams not just among NFL defensive tackles, but among all NFL defensive players, regardless of position. He has established himself as a genuine contender for Defensive Player of the Year between his short time with Chicago and his previous seven games with the Titans.

None of the Bears' current top three performers by PFN Impact grading were on the team last year. Dillon Thieneman has been fantastic as a rookie, allowing a targeted passer rating of just 67.3 this season with a well-rounded analytical profile that grades above average among safeties in coverage, run support, and overall efficiency. Cam Lewis had a shaky start to the year but has improved his standing in recent weeks.

In addition to Simmons and Thieneman, the third of those top three defensive players this year has been Devin Bush. His 84.3 run defense grade ranks seventh among all NFL linebackers going into Week 11, and he's tied for fifth at his position with seven tackles for loss. Neither of the Bears' other two primary linebackers have been fantastic in run support or in rushing the passer, but D'Marco Jackson ranks third among all linebackers with an 88.6 coverage grade, giving him the slight edge over T.J. Edwards in the grading scale.

Along the defensive line, Austin Booker has grown into a quality starting edge rusher with 5.0 sacks through his first nine games. He and Simmons are the only defensive linemen with an above-average grade at either defensive end or defensive tackle, though it's worth noting that Montez Sweat has been on an upward trajectory since the Bears acquired Simmons from Tennessee.

In the secondary, fourth-round rookie Malik Muhammad has surprised as an average starter, which is a very impressive start to an NFL career for a Day 3 draft pick. Kyler Gordon hasn't played in enough games to qualify for a PFN CB Impact grade, though his presence in the nickel has been a welcome one. Chicago remains shaky at cornerback along the perimeter, though Jaylon Johnson boosted his stock with a 94.9 grade in Week 9 against the Buccaneers amidst an otherwise disappointing season.

Cairo Santos ranks No. 21 in the NFL with a completed field goal percentage of 83.3% going into Week 11. He's gone 3-for-4 from beyond 50 yards, which is a better conversion rate than many kickers, but he doesn't have as large of a sample size kicking from deep, and the blocked field goal he had from 23 yards out in Week 2 against the Vikings looms large. From a punting standpoint, Tory Taylor doesn't grade as elite in a particular aspect of his game, but he ranks No. 13 in the NFL in both net yards per punt and gross punting average, making him slightly above average for his position.

#2809. Tiger Woods to return to competition at Bahamas tournament

Tiger Woods is returning to competition for the first time in two years, announcing Tuesday he will be playing in the Hero World Challenge at the unofficial event he hosts in the Bahamas.

His decision was posted on Instagram, with Woods joining the 20-man field along with Rickie Fowler and 2019 U.S. Open champion Gary Woodland.

The Hero World Challenge in New Providence, Bahamas, in Dec. 3-6.

Woods last competed in the 2024 British Open at Royal Troon, where he missed the cut in his last year playing all four majors. He also played the 36-hole PNC Championship with his son, where he could ride in a cart.

Woods ruptured his Achilles tendon in February 2025 and missed the entire year. As he was starting to practice this March, he was arrested and jailed on a DUI charge.

The 20-man field at Albany Golf Club is for players from the top 50 in the world ranking, though it reserves a spot for the tournament host.

#2808. Game Recap: Seven Suns score in double figures in balanced victory over Denver, 117-105

The Phoenix Suns, on the second night of a back -to-back, made the journey to the Mile High City to open up their five-game road trip. The result? A 117-105 win.

It was a solid team victory for the Phoenix Suns, who once again watched their starting unit struggle early before the bench provided a highly effective and efficient first-half performance. In the second half, however, we saw a much more engaged version of the Suns' starting five, helping Phoenix build a lead that grew as large as 19 points.

The Suns shot 44.4% from beyond the arc, knocking down 16 three-pointers while winning the rebounding battle by a convincing 51-35 margin. And while Denver owned the interior early in the game, scoring 38 points in the paint in the first half, Phoenix surrendered only 12 in the second half. The Suns also converted 18 Denver turnovers into 31 points, reminding us how effective this basketball team can be when it's playing with defensive aggression and purpose.

Nikola Jokic and Jamal Murray each finished with 28 points, but Phoenix countered with a balanced offensive attack throughout the evening. Devin Booker led all Suns scorers with 20 points despite shooting 5-of-14 from the field, and seven Phoenix players finished in double figures.

On the second night of a back-to-back, on the road, against a quality opponent, the Suns received meaningful contributions throughout their rotation. They defended, they rebounded, they knocked down their shots, and they looked increasingly connected as the game progressed. That's the version of Phoenix Suns basketball we've been waiting to see with greater consistency.

The win is the Suns' first on the road and moves the team to 6-7 on the year.

If you were hoping to see some adjustments to the starting five, prepare to be disappointed. The Phoenix Suns rolled out the same starting lineup they've deployed for the majority of the season.

The starting unit played Denver even through the first 5:37 of the game. That said, the Suns were uncharacteristically careless with the ball, committing four early turnovers that led to four easy points for the Nuggets.

Denver was making its living in the paint, scoring 22 of its 24 first-quarter points there. Neither team could find much success from beyond the arc, combining to go 1-of-11 from deep, with the lone triple coming courtesy of Jordan Goodwin.

Nikola Jokic and Cam Johnson each scored six points to lead the Nuggets, while Goodwin paced the Suns with five. It was a relatively uninspiring quarter of offensive basketball, with Phoenix trailing 24-20 heading into the second period.

The second quarter began with an interesting adjustment: Dillon Brooks shared the floor with Luke Kennard, Khaman Maluach, Jordan Goodwin, and Collin Gillespie. You could sense that Brooks felt more empowered to be the alpha offensively within that unit, taking the majority of the shots and asserting himself as the primary scoring option. The problem? He wasn't hitting them, going 1-of-4 from the field through the first three minutes of the quarter.

Khaman Maluach checked out, and in came Koa Peat. You could immediately feel his impact on the group as a whole. There was more physicality, more activity on the glass, and a greater sense of balance.

With Collin Gillespie, Luke Kennard, Dillon Brooks, Miles Bridges, and Peat on the floor, the Suns deployed a small ball lineup despite Denver having Marvin Bagley III out there. It was an effective blend of physicality, rebounding, defense, and perimeter shooting, and Phoenix put together a 13-3 run as a result. Peat was only out there for 1:16, but he was a +5.

Nikola Jokic returned, as did Oso Ighodaro, and the Joker went right back to abusing Phoenix down low. The starting unit eventually returned, with Luke Kennard in place of Dillon Brooks. And with them came the turnovers.

Jordan Ott opted to utilize both of his challenges within a three-second span. He was successful with the first, as the basketball went out of bounds off Nikola Jokic, but unsuccessful with the second, as the foul called on Collin Gillespie was upheld. So with 2:03 remaining in the second quarter and the score tied at 51–51, the Suns were already out of challenges.

It was a miserable shooting half for Devin Booker, who made only one field goal and finished with five points, courtesy of going 3-of-4 from the free-throw line. He did distribute the basketball effectively, recording seven assists, although he also committed two turnovers. But hey, he collected his 3,000th career rebound. There's something to celebrate.

Nikola Jokić led all scorers with 16 points at the half, shooting 7-of-14 from the field. Denver continued to make its living down low, with 38 of its 53 points coming in the paint. The Suns, however, held a 12-6 advantage in second-chance points and a commanding 28-9 advantage in bench scoring.

Perhaps the most encouraging statistic of the first half? The Suns were winning the battle on the glass, outrebounding the Nuggets 27-19. Given what we've witnessed through the first 12 games of the season, that's a refreshing development. Like a Cherry 7UP.

The Suns' bench matched the production of the starting five, with both units scoring 28 points in the first half. The difference was in how efficiently they got there. The starters shot 37% from the field and 14.3% from beyond the arc while committing eight turnovers. Meanwhile, the second unit shot a blistering 73.3% from the field, going 11-of-15, while connecting on 60% of its three-point attempts and committing only two turnovers.

Once again, the bench was responsible for keeping Phoenix afloat. And despite Devin Booker's rough shooting performance in the first half, the Suns entered halftime with a 56-53 advantage.

The Denver Nuggets, a team not particularly known for its perimeter shooting, came out of halftime and promptly knocked down three triples. Take Jamal Murray out of the equation, and this is a team that shoots 30% from beyond the arc.

Denver lost its challenge with 9:12 remaining in the third quarter after a foul called on Murray against Jalen Green was upheld. What made the decision more significant was that it was Murray's fourth personal foul, presumably why Nuggets head coach David Adelman elected to challenge the play.

Green followed it up with an impressive transition dunk that tied the game at 62. On Denver's ensuing possession, Green came away with another steal and drew a foul while aggressively attacking the basket.

And wouldn't you know it? Dillon Brooks picked up a technical foul after knocking down a three-pointer. His crime? Yelling at the opposing bench, something we've obviously never seen another NBA player do after hitting a three-pointer. But his reputation precedes him, and the officials assessed him his third technical foul of the season.

The starting five looked considerably scrappier to open the third quarter than we've grown accustomed to seeing this season. Through the first six minutes of the period, the unit was a +3, forcing five Denver turnovers and converting them into 11 points. There was a welcomed sense of defensive aggression from a group that has struggled to consistently establish it.

The Suns extended their lead to 10 points following Devin Booker's first three-pointer of the evening. Phoenix allowed Denver to get within five points before putting together a 9-0 run that helped extend its lead to 13. The Nuggets responded with a 5-0 run to close the quarter, trimming the deficit to single digits.

Overall, the Suns outscored the Nuggets 34-29 in the third quarter, including a 31-5 run, surrendering only six points in the paint after allowing 38 in the first half. Their defensive aggression continued to pay dividends as well, converting six Denver turnovers into 14 points.

The Suns carried a 90-82 advantage into the fourth quarter.

Phoenix opened the fourth quarter with a 7-0 run, prompting a David Adelman timeout as Phoenix extended its lead to a game-high 15 points.

With Nikola Jokic on the bench, you knew Jamal Murray was going to take it upon himself to get the Nuggets going offensively, and he scored all six of Denver's points through the first five minutes of the quarter. But the Suns' effort on the glass was magnificent early in the fourth.

Meanwhile, the Suns were playing collective basketball and relentlessly attacking the offensive glass. They secured six offensive rebounds through those first five minutes, generating six second-chance points. Those extra possessions were vital in preventing Murray from generating any significant momentum for Denver and allowing Phoenix to maintain control of the game.

The starting unit returned, and Denver promptly knocked down a couple of three-pointers. It also felt as though the whistle tightened up, as Jalen Green continued to aggressively attack the basket without being rewarded with trips to the free-throw line.

With four minutes remaining, Denver deployed the Hack-an-Oso strategy, and Jordan Ott was having none of it. He immediately pulled Oso Ighodaro from the game, opting to deploy a small-ball lineup with Miles Bridges at center and the Suns protecting a 14-point advantage.

Jamal Murray was feeling it, knocking down a three-pointer and following it up with a jumper. He then drew a foul on a three-point attempt, helping the Nuggets pull within eight points with 1:46 remaining. Ighodaro subsequently returned to the floor.

Devin Booker responded by burying a big 17-footer to push the lead back to 10, and that one felt like the dagger. Murray scored again, bringing his fourth-quarter scoring total to 18 points, but with 34 seconds remaining, it essentially became a free-throw shooting contest the rest of the way.

It's off to Sac Town for the Suns' first game against the Kings this season.

#2806. Browns offense effective at the goal line and perimeter against Texans

Down 14-0, down 17-0, it doesn't seem to phase this Cleveland Browns team in games against the Titans and Texans. Maybe it's just something with bad AFC South teams, who knows. But Cleveland only had one cold quarter of offense this week, and then they put it together for the remaining three quarters, showing a great deal of consistency by stretching the field with the long ball, working the perimeter, and having success at the goal line.

Below, we analyze the snap counts and stats on offense for the Cleveland Browns' Week 10 game against the Houston Texans.

25-of-40 (62.5%) for 312 yards, 1 INT. 8 carries, 49 yards (6.1 YPC), 1 TD.

There were a couple miscues by Deshaun Watson, with the biggest being a missed touchdown pass to Jerry Jeudy at the end of the first half that saw the Texans playing a bizarre defense. It's unclear if that was a mix-up between the two or an inaccurate throw. Watson also had a little trouble hitting some receivers in the right spot then they were crossing the field, as evidenced by passes behind KC Concepcion and ahead of Blake Whiteheart, leading to an interception.

Besides that, though, it was a solid game for Watson. The biggest key was his ability to push the ball downfield. With the Browns down big, he got the long ball to Jeudy. On the drive before that, he had a perfect one dropped into Concepcion too, but he couldn't hang on. Watson also got away from defenders against the Texans to gain 49 yards on the ground, including a touchdown run. He graded out to a 77.7 by PFF on offense, fourth highest on the team.

4 carries, 6 yards (1.5 YPC). 4 catches, 31 yards (6 targets).

10 carries, 38 yards (3.8 YPC), 1 TD. 3 catches, 33 yards (5 targets).

There still wasn't a ton of production in the ground game, although Quinshon Judkins did get the all-important go-ahead touchdown in the final minute. Both running backs were assets as receiving targets, though, taking advantage of the Texans' defense along the perimeter.

3 catches, 21 yards (8 targets). 1 carry, 6 yards (6.0 YPC), 1 TD.

Finally, the Browns actually got a completion to Malachi Corley. And then they did it a couple more times. It's about time they used him, because if he's playing snaps as a receiver, he can't just block 100% of the time. Some may say that you do that to catch a team by surprise like they did this week, but they should've done that sooner. Now, that play will be an option moving forward, and if teams collapse on it, you might have someone leaking out behind Corley. He was the team's highest-graded player on offense by PFF (89.8).

This was a little bit of a rougher day for KC Concepcion. I wouldn't say he had point blank dropped passes, but there were about three contested balls that I think he'd tell you that he wishes he could've held on to. Nonetheless, he still had a touchdown run as part of the Browns' goal line success.

Jerry Jeudy is getting open against these down-the-depth-chart defenders. As mentioned earlier, he also got open before the half, but Watson missed him.

Only one catch for Denzel Boston, who had a toe injury that bothered him again late. Thankfully, the bye is coming up. Funny enough, they used him heavily on punts this week.

Harold Fannin is just a beast, he refuses to go down. He now leads the team in catches (44) and yards (544) heading into the bye.

Blake Whiteheart was the team's lowest-graded offensive player. I'm not saying he should be replaced this year, but during the bye, seeing if the team can find a better contributing second tight end might be on their radar.

The offensive line was quite good in pass protection this week, as the sacks were minimal. Penalties weren't a thing either!

Tytus Howard was the team's second-highest graded player on offense (82.1), per PFF. Zion Johnson, Spencer Fano, and Austin Barber also did a solid job as far as grading goes. Elgton Jenkins is the only player who had a rough grade.

#2805. ESPN's ranking for Florida's Thomas Haugh in Player of the Year race

As the college basketball season enters its third week, ESPN recently released its rankings of the nation's top 10 players and early contenders for National Player of the Year honors, placing Haugh at No. 2 overall.

The race for college basketball's most prestigious individual award looks more competitive than in recent seasons, with an influx of returning stars, talented first-year students, and international prospects strengthening the national talent pool.

According to ESPN's Myron Medcalf, Haugh has emerged as one of the country's premier players after electing to return to Gainesville for another season rather than pursue a professional career. The decision came after Florida's disappointing second-round exit against Iowa in the 2026 NCAA tournament, a loss Haugh previously acknowledged played a significant role in his decision to return.

In the first two weeks of the season, Haugh has elevated his production across several statistical categories, especially from beyond the arc. The veteran forward is shooting an impressive 45% from distance while attempting five shots per game, showing significant improvement as a perimeter scorer.

One of his most notable performances came on the road against in-state rival Florida State on Nov. 10, when Haugh recorded 21 points and 10 rebounds while making three of his six three-point attempts.

So far this year, Haugh is averaging 17.3 points, 8.3 rebounds, and 2.5 assists per game, while shooting 52.6% from the field. His scoring versatility, rebounding, and improved perimeter shooting this season have helped reiterate his standing among the nation's elite players while strengthening Florida's championship aspirations.

With the Gators looking to make another deep postseason run, Haugh's continued development could be instrumental in determining whether Florida can return to the national championship conversation.

#2803. Dan Campbell: I'm not telling players to stop celebrating because of Aidan Hutchinson's injury

Lions head coach Dan Campbell isn't going to tell his players to stop celebrating in the wake of defensive end Aidan Hutchinson's torn ACL.

Hutchinson will miss the rest of the season after injuring his right knee while celebrating a sack of Drake Maye in Sunday's game. Hutchinson jumped in the air and immediately grabbed his right knee after suffering the injury when he landed back on the turf in Munich.

During an appearance on 97.1 The Ticket on Tuesday, Campbell called the injury "one of those things that happened" and that "this game is built off of emotion" that he doesn't want to take away from his players.

"I hate it, I know he hates it. But I mean, the number of moves that I've seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one leg lateral movements, lateral balances, I'm just like, it was one of those things, man," Campbell said. "I hate it. I think we all understand what it is, and let's be smart, but no, I'm not gonna say, 'Nobody celebrates anymore.'"

Campbell recounted a story from his own playing days when he tore his biceps while celebrating a play and that didn't lead him to make celebration avoidance a rule for the Lions when he got the job. That doesn't make Hutchinson's injury any easier for the Lions to deal with, but it makes his response to questions about the advice he'll give in the future easier to understand.

#2801. Steve Kerr: ‘Lineup combinations are going to change’

It was somewhat understandable — albeit, still a bitter pill to swallow — that the Golden State Warriors were shellacked by the league's best team in the San Antonio Spurs without Steph Curry being there to save the day. Scoring 14 points in the first quarter against a defense anchored by perhaps the best defender in the world in Victor Wembanyama doesn't need much explanation, especially when Curry — a proven hard counter to Wembanyama and his defensive abilities — is back in the Bay Area rehabbing a torn right oblique muscle.

However, scoring fewer than 14 points in the first quarter against the New Orleans Pelicans — heading into tonight, the fourth-worst defense in terms of points allowed per 100 possessions — is great cause for concern. The Warriors managed a paltry 11 points against the Pelicans in the first quarter, while giving up 35 in turn. It was a hole they never managed to climb out of, too insurmountable for even one of their patented fake comebacks to occur.

It's clear that without Curry — and to a certain extent, backup guard Brandon Williams — lineups have become wonky. Combinations that normally wouldn't see the light of day are being deployed. Such is the nature of a team that is without their best player, their second-best player, and a couple of key role players that connect everything together (not to mention, a center that virtually just up and left the team) — all combining for hundreds of millions of dollars in salary missing.

But that is the reality right now for the Warriors, who will have to find ways to tread water. One of those ways is finding combinations that do work — a task for Steve Kerr and his coaching staff to figure out.

The Warriors had perhaps their most winnable game of the road trip tonight — and did things that were far from winning basketball. The schedule isn't going to get any easier, with Dallas up next on Thursday, followed by Memphis the following night to complete a tough back-to-back slate. Three days after, they will thankfully return home — but against the Eastern Conference's best team in the Boston Celtics.

The NBA is a league without mercy — and the Warriors will just have to find ways to cope with that fact.

#2795. Nations Championship schedule, results, kick-off times and how to watch

Rugby's Nations Championship has reached its last weekend of pool play with the finals weekend fate of the world's top teams becoming clear.

The new competition pits the Six Nations sides against the four Rugby Championship competitors, plus Japan and Fiji, across six matches split evenly across the July and November Test windows.

The results of those games will determine each of the 12 teams' seedings for the finals weekend at Twickenham's Allianz Stadium, with six matches contested over two days and both team and hemisphere silverware up for grabs.

Each entrant will be matched against the equivalent qualifier from the other pool to decide overall placings at the conclusion of a busy year of international action. South Africa and New Zealand are assured of a top-two finish in their group – but both were beaten in a stunning penultimate round of pool action.

Here's the full competition schedule, and how the tables stand ahead of finals weekend.

Announced during the 2023 Rugby World Cup, the new competition will be held every other year and replaces the traditional summer tours and Autumn Nations Series. It is designed to add context to the international calendar, with Fiji and Japan invited to join the Six Nations and Rugby Championship sides to form a top 12. Promotion and relegation is planned for the future with the second-tier Nations Cup, though will not take place in 2026.

Northern Hemisphere: France, Ireland, Scotland, Italy, England, Wales.

Southern Hemisphere: South Africa, New Zealand, Argentina, Australia, Fiji, Japan.

The first finals weekend will be held at Twickenham on the last weekend (27 to 29) of November. Three days of double-headers will be held at the ground, with the first-placed finisher of the Six Nations sides meeting whoever tops the other pool, and so on and so forth down the rankings.

The winner of that marquee match will lift the first Nations Championship trophy, while whichever hemisphere – a term used loosely with Japan included in the "southern" section and Fiji hosting their games in the United Kingdom – wins more final matches will also be crowned. The 1st vs 1st match is worth two points, with all other matches in the finals weekend worth one.

Viewers in the United Kingdom will be able to watch every game on ITV after the free-to-air broadcaster struck a deal to show the competition.

England vs New Zealand (2.10pm, Twickenham)

Eleventh place final (4.40pm): Northern 6 vs Southern 6

Fifth place final (8.10pm): Northern 3 vs Southern 3

Ninth place final (1.10pm): Northern 5 vs Southern 5

Third place final (4.40pm): Northern 2 vs Southern 2

Seventh place final (1.10pm): Northern 4 vs Southern 4

Nations Championship final (4.40pm): Northern 1 vs Southern 1

The absence of Stephen Curry is being felt across the Warriors roster.

On Monday night, the Warriors suffered their second consecutive blowout loss, dropping 115-94 to the lowly Pelicans, and Steve Kerr was well aware that his "starting lineup didn't seem to have a lot of juice."

"We're gonna have to change some combinations and find some things that work for us," Kerr said. "We got some good looks early and it was one of those nights where shots didn't go, and it felt like that really deflated us."

Steve Kerr says Warriors had no juice in Monday's loss to Pelicans. AP Photo/Darren Abate

The Warriors already found themselves staring at a gaping hole from the moment the game started, being outscored 35-11 by the end of the first quarter. New Orleans nearly doubled their lead by halftime as Golden State trailed 61-36 by the end of the first half.

Yaxel Lendeborg showing his frustrations during Monday's loss to the Pelicans. Getty Images

Near the end of the game, Lendeborg was seen sitting on Golden State's bench, looking dejected after putting together a performance where he did everything he could to help the team win, finishing with 28 points, eight rebounds and three assists.

"He fought his way back. The second half, he did a much better job attacking," Kerr said. "I thought the game, at the start of the third quarter, it felt like how it should've started."

Golden State scored 29 points in the third and fourth quarters, and Kerr said he felt like he saw the energy that the team was missing when the game started.

"The ball was moving. We were diving on the pick and roll, pulling the defense in," Kerr said. "First half, we were just on the perimeter, launching."

Kerr went on to say that as the team adjusts to playing without Curry, they will need to find some lineup creations that will work.

"I'm looking for a force of cutting. Playing with a sense of urgency. The connection that starts when the ball starts to move off of those cuts," Kerr said.

#2791. Will Rams keep using two kickers?

The Los Angeles Rams have been using Brandon McManus for field goals and Josh Karty for kickoffs, and how much longer will Sean McVay keep doing this? The Rams have the bye week to make changes, or keep rolling with the two kickers. Using two kickers is uncommon, but whatever works.

McManus has been perfect on field goals and extra points since joining the Rams, while Karty has been solid when his "dirty kicks" work, but Karty has also had some penalties on kickoffs giving opponents excellent starting position. Karty has been inconsistent throughout his career.

It's been interesting to see the Rams use two kickers, but is it something they'll keep doing or will the experiment be shutdown after the bye week? It's a storyline worth watching.

"After losing Blake Grupe to injury, the Rams didn't stop by adding Brandon McManus, their third kicker this season. They continued by re-signing Joshua Karty. Karty was released last season when his 'knuckle kickoff' attempts tarnished his field-goal accuracy.His return was meant to optimize LA's defensive field position on kickoffs, with McManus used exclusively for field goals and extra points. Unfortunately, the results of Karty's kickoffs over three games are less than encouraging.

The risk was worth taking. Deploying Karty exclusively for his touchback-inducing knuckleball, thus spotting opposing offenses at the 20-yard line, is certainly innovative thinking"

"Even their 6-4 record suggests they're a good team, even if they're no longer the on-paper superteam they were once heralded as in September. The Rams enter their Week 11 bye tied for the same record as the Packers and Seahawks, who are seventh and eighth in the NFC.

Through 10 games in 2023, they were 4-6 and still made the playoffs. In 2024, they were 5-5 and once again made the postseason. So technically, they're in better shape now than they were in those two seasons."
