#2789. Rockets flounder in Miami 128-110

Sometimes a team has the energy to take a (private, comfortable) flight after a game, and play another game the next day, and play very well. After a big win in Washington, where pretty much everything from the early third quarter onward was garbage time, one might think the team would be more rested ready to compete in its next game. That's a great plan, and morally sound, but probably easier said than done if that post game flight is to Miami. The Rockets announcers called it a long flight, but it really isn't that long, a bit over two hours. Still, given all the gear, and to-ing and fro-ing you'd expect the Rockets to hit their likely downtown, luxury hotel, after midnight. You may be old, and I may be old, but the Rockets? They're not old. They might want to enjoy some nightlife in Miami.

Could they? Yes, absolutely. Why? Because although last call is typically 2AM in the Miami area, venues in the "Entertainment District" can serve until 5am. Bars and restaurants with a seating capacity under 100 people? They can stay open until 5am. And in certain areas liquor sales go on 24/7. Add in the fact that while you and I might not have access to posh, or wild after parties in Miami, (I can't speak for you) very wealthy young basketball stars probably do. The Rockets might discover a lot more entertainment, amusement and diversion on offer late night in Miami than you or I might find. Again, I can't speak for you.

This is possibly why the Rockets were flat, like a fish, a flat fish, a flounder. And flounder they did. At no point did this game look like a win for the Rockets. It started badly, with Miami shooting 3s like the peak Golden State Warrios, and that great shooting did not not only include a less-washed looking Klay Thompson (Heat Culture Injection!) and Andrew Wiggins, but everyone else, too. It didn't help that Miami came out playing like the U of Miami Hurricanes football team, and this was fine, apparently. They love the Hurricanes down there. Of the football variety, anyway.

The Rockets looked slower, less forceful, and generally not ready to do much. The, unique in the NBA Noah Laroche "No Screen" system, seemed to baffle the Rockets, and their switching and produced about 900 open corner 3pt shots for Miami. The novelty of a system they're not likely to see more than once was too much for the Rockets and they were breaded and fried like fish for 128 points tonight. Whereas Miami's coach, Erik Spoelstra just played the hits from last season, and packed the paint, and dared the Rockets to score inside while being, ah, "vigorously defended and possibly knifed" or make outside shots.

Make outside shots, they did not. The Rockets shot 28% from three, on fairly low volume as well. Miami put up 50 3pt shots, and made 18. That's a stark difference from the Rockets 36 3pt attempts. Why would you want to put up more 3s if you're not making them at a good rate? Because the 2pt shots weren't falling either. At 41% on twos, it's better to shoot more threes.

Would more energy have won this one? I'm not sure. They Rockets typically lose at least one game a season to Miami. The Heat bet on tired legs shooting from range, and swarming defense in close. It wasn't some sort of utter shut down, the Rockets scored about NBA average at 110, but the flat, floundering, start for 15pts in the first quarter couldn't be overcome. A better shooting Rockets team might have won this, as they had a 21 to 13 offensive rebound advantage, but if you don't cash those boards with makes it can look like this game. Maybe, though, more energy would have helped. They deep bench, particularly Isaiah Crawford looked decent in their minutes.

Another point to consider is that the Rockets truly do give up a fair number of games available by their better players by not playing various players on a back to back. Adams might have helped control the paint. It turned out he wasn't really needed against the Tall But Bad Wizards. It wasn't a bad decision to play him in DC, but when you're guessing about FVV and Adams (if available) on every back to back, you're going to roll snakeyes sometimes.

Of concern also, Amen Thompson sat out a game in a return to his native land of South Florida with left knee tendonitis. He seemed to land badly on a play in DC, and the seems to be result of that, despite him playing on. Hopefully this is nothing long lasting, or serious, and more of a precaution.

This might have been a better outcome if the Rockets started in Miami and went to The District, but we'll never know. Best to forget it and move on.

#2788. Andy Reid defends Xavier Worthy, who had no catches in 57 snaps

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes completed 22 passes to eight different receivers and threw five touchdowns in Sunday’s 35-13 win over the Falcons. None of his completions went to Xavier Worthy.

Worthy has 33 catches for 278 yards and a touchdown in nine games this season. The Chiefs have not used him in the run game.

On Sunday, he led all Kansas City wide receivers with 57 snaps and drew three targets. He also made a special teams mistake, allowing the Falcons to recover the first successful onside kick of the season in the NFL.

Coach Andy Reid was asked about Worthy’s lack of production, which had to be at least part of the reason the Chiefs traded for Josh Downs last week. Downs had four catches for 53 yards and a touchdown in his Kansas City debut.

“He just needs to keep doing his thing and working, and he’ll be fine,” Reid said Monday, via Charles Goldman of AtoZ Sports. “Some days will be his, and some days it goes to other guys. I mean, that’s just how this thing works. We’re spreading it around. Pat loves spreading it around and does a great job with it. Our coaches love doing that. It’s tough on defenses. Everybody’s kind of getting a little piece of the pie. You saw when he was only the guy against the Chargers and his production there was great. So, it’s not that he’s not capable of that.”

Reid further explained that the Falcons’ game plan was to take Worthy out of the game, which “allowed other guys to be available.”

Worthy, a first-round pick in 2024, had his best season as a rookie when he made 59 catches for 638 yards and six touchdowns, while also running for 104 yards and three touchdowns on 20 carries.

In the 23 games he’s played since, Worthy has 75 catches for 810 yards and two touchdowns, with 11 carries for 87 yards and no touchdowns.

#2785. How much Inter paid for Spence, Tottenham pocket €30.75m plus bonus

The financial details behind Inter's deal with Tottenham Hotspur for Djed Spence have been revealed, with the cost reaching €30.75m plus a series of add-ons.

The 26-year-old made the move from Spurs to Inter in mid-August, after an excellent performance for England at the 2026 World Cup.

He was their second choice for the role of right wing-back, after being out-bid by Chelsea for Marco Palestra, and will eventually cost around half that fee.

MILAN, ITALY – OCTOBER 10: Djed Spence of FC Internazionale Milano reacts during the Serie A match between FC Internazionale and Parma Calcio at Stadio Giuseppe Meazza on October 10, 2026 in Milan, Italy. (Photo by Claudio Villa/Getty Images)

Calcio e Finanza looked through the Inter accounts and found the precise details of the agreement to buy Spence from Tottenham Hotspur.

The basic fee is worth €30.75m split into three instalments in the summer of 2026, 2027 and 2028 of €10.25m each.

There are also potential add-ons worth up to a total of €5.125m tied to various achievements for the club and the player.

MILAN, ITALY – OCTOBER 10: Djed Spence of FC Internazionale Milano warms up as a substitute during the Serie A match between FC Internazionale and Parma Calcio at Stadio Giuseppe Meazza on October 10, 2026 in Milan, Italy. (Photo by Claudio Villa/Getty Images)

This is €512,500 for each Serie A title won by Inter, as long as Spence played at least 50 per cent of those matches, but can only be used at most twice for a total of €1.025m.

In order for it to count, Spence must've played a minimum of 45 minutes per match.

MILAN, ITALY – OCTOBER 10: Djed Spence of FC Internazionale Milano warms up as a substitute during the Serie A match between FC Internazionale and Parma Calcio at Stadio Giuseppe Meazza on October 10, 2026 in Milan, Italy. (Photo by Claudio Villa/Getty Images)

The same fee and system are used for reaching the Champions League semi-final, again playing 50 per cent of games, and at most in two seasons for a maximum of €1.025m.

Simply qualifying for the Champions League is worth €512,500 when playing 50 per cent of Serie A matches, but this bonus can be activated up to three times for a peak of €1.5375m.

Winning the Champions League is a one-off bonus worth €1.025m, again with Spence participating in half those games.

ATLANTA, GEORGIA – JULY 15: Djed Spence #25 of England reacts after making a stop against Giuliano Simeone #17 of Argentina during the FIFA World Cup 2026 Semi Final match between England and Argentina at Atlanta Stadium on July 15, 2026 in Atlanta, Georgia. (Photo by Shaun Botterill/Getty Images)

If the player reaches 30 goal contributions (between goals and assists) in all competitive games, there is another bonus going to Tottenham of €512,500.

This is not necessarily in one season, but once the target is reached, it cannot be repeated.

If all these bonuses are activated, Spence will have cost €35,875m.

LIVERPOOL, ENGLAND – OCTOBER 26: Djed Spence of Tottenham Hotspur during the Premier League match between Everton and Tottenham Hotspur at Hill Dickinson Stadium on October 26, 2025 in Liverpool, England. (Photo by Carl Recine/Getty Images)

That is not all, because Tottenham also have a percentage of his future sale fee worth 10.25 per cent of the total, but it also applies in case of a loan with option to buy.

If Spence is released as part of an exchange of players, then Inter must give Spurs 10.25 per cent of the calculated value.

#2780. Warriors vs. Pelicans player grades: Yaxel Lendeborg leads the way in a rough game

Well, that was a dud. The Golden State Warriors have looked so impressive when Steph Curry is off the court this year … but only in the games where he starts on the court. In their second game since Curry's abdominal injury, the Warriors once again looked completely lost … and once again needed more than six minutes to score their first field goal.

The end result was a 115-94 loss to the New Orleans Pelicans but, if we're being honest, the game wasn't nearly that close. So let's run through the grades, because nobody wants to spend much time on this one. As always, grades are based on my expectations for each player, with a "B" grade representing the average performance for that player.

Note: True-shooting percentage (TS) is a scoring efficiency metric that accounts for threes and free throws. Entering Monday's games, league-average TS was 57.4%.

33 minutes, 28 points, 8 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 2 blocks, 4 turnovers, 2 fouls, 9-for-23 shooting, 2-for-9 threes, 8-for-8 free throws, 52.8% TS, -30

Lendeborg had the worst game of his young career on Friday, in his first taste of being The Man at the NBA level. He responded by calling himself out and cutting his hair.

For a while it seemed like this game might be a similar outcome, but as time wore on, he really started to figure things out. Lendeborg, like all of his teammates, couldn't find the bottom of the hoop in the first half, but he poured in 20 second-half points, including 10 in the first few minutes of the third quarter as he almost single-handedly tried (albeit unsuccessfully) to get the Dubs back in it.

The resilience was noteworthy. As was the defense which, while far from perfect, featured some really impressive hands. Lendeborg has a bit of Andre Iguodala's ability to swipe at the ball while moving backwards and still avoid fouling, and it's very impressive. It disrupted Zion Williamson on a few occasions.

There's still a lot of work to do if he wants to lead the team to victory in Curry's absence, but he showed some signs in this one.

Grade: B–Post-game bonus: Led the team in points; tied for the team lead in rebounds; worst plus/minus on the team.

Podziemski has a nice jumper, and his three ball has been good this year, but his game works best when it's working inside out, rather than outside in. The lefty is at his best when he's penetrating the defense, getting looks in the lane, and then parlaying that threat into outside looks, and that wasn't the case today.

The bigger issue, though, is that with Curry out, the Warriors need Podziemski to function as a facilitator of sorts, and it's hard to do that when you turn the ball over five times. It's funny: sometimes Santos seems to be bothered more than most of the team by length and athleticism, and sometimes he seems to handle it better than the rest of the team. This game was squarely the latter, as he combatted New Orleans' size and speed by being extra aggressive, and couldn't be kept off the glass.

23 minutes, 11 points, 3 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals, 1 turnover, 4 fouls, 4-for-11 shooting, 2-for-6 threes, 1-for-1 free throws, 48.1% TS, -8

First and foremost, Richard's toughness needs constant praising. It seems quite possible that the plan for Richard is to play through the pain until the team gets healthier, and then forgo the second half of the season to have surgery for the torn UCL in his thumb. It could end up costing Richard the chance to play during a stretch run, and he deserves to be commended for toughing out the pain, and for putting the team first in just his second NBA season.

He's on the court for defense primarily, but he was one of the team's most aggressive scorers in this game. It didn't always work, as the shooting efficiency points to, but it was very notable, and it helped keep the Pelicans' defense honest. I liked seeing him in attack mode, and hopefully we get more of that going forward.

8 minutes, 2 points, 1-for-1 shooting, 100.0% TS, +6

Conventional wisdom might make it seem like Payton should be getting more minutes with Curry and Brandon Williams sidelined, but in reality, it seems to push GPII further down the depth chart, since the Dubs really need some offense to replace that high-octane duo, and Payton is a defense first (and second) guard. Still, he did some good things in his limited time in this game.

8 minutes, 3 points, 5 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 foul, 1-for-1 shooting, 1-for-2 free throws, 79.8% TS, +6

I don't like that it usually comes under such unfortunate circumstances, but I really am enjoying every chance we get to watch Ike. The undrafted rookie, who is on a two-way contract, really filled out the box score in his limited time in this game.

6 minutes, 2 points, 1 rebound, 1-for-3 shooting, 33.3% TS, +8

Even though he was on the team last year, Leons is still a bit more raw than the other two-way contracts on the team, Ike and Cryer. But he's still good for a few eye-opening plays when he gets on the court. He definitely has a lot of talent.

#2779. Which was MS Dhoni's final match for CSK?

Which was MS Dhoni's final match for CSK? originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

MS Dhoni's last match for CSK was against Rajasthan Royals at Delhi on May 20, 2025.

His first came at Mohali on April 19, 2008, when he was dismissed for 1 and CSK still won by 33 runs.

He retired on the IPL 2027 retention deadline and takes over as CSK's mentor.

November 17, 2026, was the day all 10 franchises had to send the BCCI their lists of retained and released players before the IPL 2027 mini-auction. CSK's list went in with Dhoni's name on the released side, at a fee of ₹4 crore, and with it came the announcement that he was retiring as a player and taking over as the franchise's mentor.

The decision was made on CSK's own video call. Rupa Gurunath, the franchise's whole-time director, said in a video posted on the CSK account that chief executive Kasi Viswanathan had been reading out the release list on the Saturday before the deadline when Dhoni interrupted him. She said Dhoni very quietly suggested his own name should be added to it and that the call ended there.

He had not played a competitive match in 18 months. Dhoni did not feature in any of CSK's 14 games in IPL 2026, which makes the last match he played a defeat in Delhi in May 2025.

It also means the farewell game at Chepauk he had spoken about in the past will not happen. N Srinivasan, in the statement confirming the move to mentor, said there is no CSK without MS Dhoni.

MORE:How MS Dhoni informed CSK about his decision to retire ahead of IPL retention deadline

The 62nd match of IPL 2025 at the Arun Jaitley Stadium on May 20 was a dead rubber between the two teams at the bottom of the table. Dhoni was captaining because Ruturaj Gaikwad had fractured his elbow against the same opponents in March, and CSK had handed the job back to him on April 10 with four defeats from their first five games already on the board.

Rajasthan bowled first, and CSK lost five wickets inside 7.4 overs, with Yudhvir Singh doing most of the damage on the way to 3 for 47. Ayush Mhatre counter-attacked with 43 off 20 balls and added 56 with R Ashwin, then Dewald Brevis made 42 off 25 and Shivam Dube 39 in a stand of 59. Dhoni came in at the back end and was still there at 151 for 6 in the 16th over when he hit the last six of his IPL career.

He finished on 16 off 17 and was caught by Tushar Deshpande off Akash Madhwal in the final over, with CSK managing only 17 runs in the last three to reach 187 for 8. Madhwal took 3 for 29 and the Player of the Match award.

Rajasthan knocked the runs off in 17.1 overs. Yashasvi Jaiswal made 36 off 19, and Vaibhav Suryavanshi made 57 off 33 to take the chase away early, and Sanju Samson added 41, and although Ashwin took two wickets in the 14th over to finish with 2 for 41, Dhruv Jurel hit 31 not out off 12 balls to end it.

CSK finished the season bottom of the table for the first time in their history, with eight points from 14 matches and a net run rate of minus 0.647. That's not a much different picture than how his IPL debut was nearly 18 years ago.

MORE:Will CSK retire MS Dhoni's No.7 jersey? List of retired jerseys in cricket after a player's retirement

Dhoni arrived at CSK as the most expensive player in the inaugural auction in February 2008, bought for 1.5 million dollars four months after he had captained India to the World T20 title in Johannesburg. He was named captain immediately, a job he would hold until 2024.

His first IPL match was the second the competition ever staged, at Mohali on April 19, 2008, the day after Brendon McCullum's 158 not out opened the tournament in Bengaluru. Dhoni won the toss against Yuvraj Singh's Kings XI Punjab and chose to bat, and his own contribution lasted six deliveries.

He made 1 and was given out, with replays suggesting the ball had come off an inside edge onto his pad rather than the bat. What followed made the dismissal irrelevant. Michael Hussey took CSK to 240 for 5 with an unbeaten 116, the highest total of the tournament at that stage.

Punjab's reply was better than the margin suggests. James Hopes made 71 off 33 balls, including a 24-ball fifty that was the fastest of the tournament to that point, and Kumar Sangakkara scored 54 before Muttiah Muralitharan removed him in the 18th over. Yuvraj made 23, and Punjab closed on 207 for 4, leaving CSK winners by 33 runs.

CSK reached the final that season and lost it to Rajasthan Royals. It was a quiet start for a player who would carve himself as one of the absolute legends in the league's history.

Dhoni played 278 IPL matches across 19 seasons, 272 of them for CSK and 14 for Rising Pune Supergiant in 2016 and 2017, when CSK was serving a two-year suspension. He batted 242 times and was unbeaten in 100 of those innings, a figure that explains both the average and the role he played for most of the second half of his career.

The 205 dismissals are the most by any wicketkeeper in the competition, and he is the only one to have passed 200. The highest score of 84 not out came against Royal Challengers Bengaluru at the Chinnaswamy in 2019, and he never made an IPL hundred in 19 seasons.

The captaincy record is where the gap over the rest of the field is widest. Dhoni led in 235 matches, more than anyone in IPL history, and won 136 of them, also a record. His five titles tie Rohit Sharma for the most by a captain, and CSK reached 10 finals and 12 playoffs under him.

He was also the first IPL captain to defend a title, winning in 2010 and 2011, and added two Champions League T20 trophies with CSK in 2010 and 2014. From IPL 2027, those numbers stop moving, with Dhoni working alongside new head coach Zaheer Khan as mentor.

#2778. State of the NFC South: Week 10

The gap between first and second place is widening, and Week 10 featured one of the biggest NFC South matchups of the year.

The Bucs were on their bye week, and after not playing, they are in second place in the division. Next week, the Bucs will be facing the Lions, but QB Baker Mayfield's availability is up in the air.

To our only winner of the week, the Panthers are now the only team with four wins in the division and sit atop the NFC South. With a mixture of a very inexperienced referee group, some bad penalties, and horrible pass blocking, this team should have lost. But the Saints couldn't take advantage of anything, and the Panthers were just the better team on Sunday.

Their next game will be against the Ravens, who are without Lamar Jackson. Eventhough Carolina's defense did well against the Saints' RBs, this is still a group that ranks very low in the NFL in run defense, and that's a worrying stat while facing Derrick Henry.

Atlanta had a really bad week. They were forced to go away from Bijan Robinson in this matchup against the Kansas City Chiefs, and eventhough Michael Penix finished with 286 passing yards, the passing game couldn't score a touchdown. The defense is the real issue in this game, allowing Patrick Mahomes to throw for 348 yards and 5 touchdowns. Allow is probably not the correct word here because we are talking about Mahomes, but this was his best game of the season so far.

The Falcons will be on their bye week next.

And to the Saints. Ugh is the best word I can use to describe the Saints right now. Two things couldn't happen this season: The team being as bad and Tyler Shough regressing. Well, both are true right now. Kellen Moore, even after firing T.J. Paganetti, still hasn't figured out the run game, even against the Panthers' run defense. If it wasn't for Brandon Staley and this pass rush, the Saints would have lost this game by 30, even with all the positive calls they got from the refs.

As for Shough, he is playing with a broken hand and is probably playing as bad as we have seen from him. He is missing wide-open throws, not seeing wide-open receivers, and is playing scared in the pocket. This is why it was so important for Moore to figure out the run game and offensive line this past offseason. Keeping Shough a bit more comfortable in the pocket while also freeing up the passing game would cause Shough to improve. We have seen what Shough can do, and that's good enough for him to be a starter, but he needs a new head coach.

#2775. Fantasy Football D/ST Rankings: Week 11 2026

Fantasy Football D/ST Rankings: Week 11 2026 appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

As the fantasy football season gets closer to playoff time, it's important to make good decisions. And who knows if you might score a big defensive effort from a surprise team like the Vikings, who soared despite a tough matchup. Here are the fantasy defense and special teams rankings for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season.

With a horrific six-team bye week, the good options may be few and far between. The Packers, Patriots, Rams, Falcons, Browns, and Seahawks are all on a bye. Fantasy managers may have to take a defense facing an elite offense.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

The best bet is a team you can't really depend upon this year. The Texans were expected to be awesome like they were in 2025. It just hasn't happened.

And last week, with a relatively good matchup, the Texans pretty much flopped like a world-class soccer player. However, they get the trade-depleted Colts. And the Texans are at home. So they should have a shot at scoring some points.

Standout edge rusher Will Anderson said the Texans need to be much better, according to texanswire.com.

"We have to do a better job of being more disciplined," Anderson said. "I think that's the biggest thing. We had way too many penalties (against the Browns), myself included. We're ball key. I have to watch the ball. Just cutting down on the penalties, looking yourself in the mirror and taking ownership of the things you didn't do right in this game. But also noticing the things you did do right in this game and taking that and using that momentum going into Thursday night."

Anderson said the Texans don't have a big puzzle to solve. He said the team can be better simply by doing the little things.

"I think it's just the execution," Anderson said. "I think they do a good job of presenting what we have to go out and do. (And) I think for us, it's just being in those live moments and being able to take a deep breath and do your job. I think that's just been the biggest thing right now is, get the call, play the call no matter how it may look.

"It's the same thing every week. And it's things just not going our way, like we want it to. We just have to continue to rally, stand 10 toes down, continue to play ball, continue to have fun doing it, continue to bring the energy into the building and keep everybody uplifted and keep this thing rolling."

They will invite the Raiders and their rookie quarterback to Mile High Stadium. This is a chance for the Broncos to make splash plays.

Still, head coach Sean Payton wants his team to do the little things better. They've been good at harassing opposing quarterbacks, but Payton wants them to be better situationally, according to Sports Illustrated.

"Then there were some third-down, fourth-down-and-longs defensively that was an Achilles heel for us a year ago, and the other day became one," Payton said. "Those are just a few things."

A defense on the rise is the Bills. And this week they get the unproductive Dolphins at home. A case can be made that the Bills could end up as the No. 1 fantasy defense this week.

It's all about working together, Bills head coach Joe Brady said, according to a post on the Bills' YouTube page via AOL.com.

"I think our communication is at a high level," Brady said. "They're understanding the scheme, how they're trying to be attacked. We knew that this was part of the process of going through it all and us getting the feel of 11 guys playing together and who we wanted to be. I thought coming out of the bye week was important for us.

"I think our defense is playing at a high level right now. We've got to just keep going. We've got to get better."

Also a safe bet is the Jaguars defense. They handled the Titans as needed in Week 10. But it wasn't dominant, according to MiamiHerald.com.

"I don't think it was our best," Jaguars head coach Liam Coen said. "I'm not sure we swarmed and ran to the ball quite as much as I would like to see us do at times. Kind of at times, looked like we were trying to punch the ball out instead of getting the guy on the ground a few times.

"But I thought for the most part guys played well. It wasn't perfect. We can play better, but our defense played really well. I think the last few weeks we have held teams under 20 points a lot … and we should win games if they play that way."

The Giants have some offensive threats. But the Jaguars can still thrive.

Coming off an absolute embarrassment against Joe Burrow and the Bengals, the Steelers aren't exactly the top defensive choice in Week 11 for fantasy managers. However, the Eagles are inept on offense. So this is a chance for the Steelers to bounce back.

Plus, the Steelers have a proud team and organization. Mike McCarthy said the poor showing bothered him, according to NBC Sports.

"That's clearly not to the standard of the Pittsburgh Steelers. It's not to any of our expectations," McCarthy said. "I was very disappointed. We're all disappointed."

It might be a surprise to see the Vikings in this spot. But they're in the same boat as last week, going up with an offense that just put up 46 points. And they're on the road against those 49ers. Some folks might avoid the Vikings. But they are, at the very least, a sleeper.

#2773. Here's who coaches tabbed for the All-MVL boys soccer teams

The Muskingum Valley League's top soccer players were honored by league coaches with their All-MVL squads recently.

Sheridan's Xavior Weisbarth-Rodriguez and West Muskingum's Aiden Metz were named Players of the Year in the Big and Small School Divisions, respectively.

Weisbarth-Rodriguez also earned Big School Offensive Player of the Year honors for Sheridan, which shared the league championship with River View with 18 points. The senior forward finished with 28 goals and eight assists, as the Generals set program records for wins and won their first league title in program history.

River View's Kasen Summers was selected Defensive Player of the Year, Tri-Valley's Corbin Ryan was named Midfielder of the Year and John Glenn's Bennett Mueller earned Goalkeeper of the Year honors. Vince Andrews, of River View, and Chris McKinney, of Sheridan, shared Coach of the Year honors.

In the Small Division, Metz led a dominant West Muskingum squad that captured the league title and won its first regional title in program history. Teammates Brooks McCune (senior), Austin Fink (junior) and Bryce Stant (senior) were named Midfielder, Defensive Player and Goalkeeper of the Year, respectively, while Ryan Barks was chosen Coach of the Year.

Metz finished with 16 goals and a team-high 16 assists. His goals trailed only junior Dyllan Farmer, who had 24 and led the team with 55 points. Stant finished with a 1.13 goals against average and made 74 saves in 21 games.

The Big School first team included Summers and teammates Cameron Murphy and Blaze Klein, Weisbarth-Rodriguez and teammate Kasen Miller; John Glenn's Rocco Sabino, Mueller and Angel Reyes, Maysville's Keaton Meredith and Carson Shockley, Tri-Valley's Ryan and Jake Pratt and Philo's Parker Johnson.

West Muskingum placed five players on the Small Division first team in Fink, Stant, Metz, McCune and Farmer. Coshocton's Clay Watson and Colton Mathias, New Lexington's Nate Elekes and Morgan's Aaron Baker also earned first-team honors.

River View's Cooper Alverson and Easton Wesney, Sheridan's Luke Close, Logan Keaton, Zach Hamilton and Zander Weisbarth-Rodriguez, John Glenn's Karson Wood and Keegan Halsey, Maysville's Colton McPeek and Tri-Valley's Brady Aker and Henry Boyd were second-team Big School picks. Honorable mention honors went to River View's Levi Fox and Maysville's Jackson Factor.

The Small School second team consisted of West Muskingum's Landan Frick, Coshocton's Jorge Hernandez and Leo Jasper, New Lexington's Addie Spicer and Owen Mildenstein and Morgan's Brock Roop and Staton Cunningham.

Coshocton's Camrin Talbott, New Lexington's Maddox Carson and Billy Tollet, and Morgan's Ray Hale were honorable mention in the Small.

#2772. Warriors get blown out faster than the British in 1815 in disaster trip to New Orleans

In the Battle of New Orleans, Sir Edward Pakenham took the British army on a road trip to Louisiana to face Andrew Jackson's American troops. In less than 30 minutes, the British army had suffered over 2,000 casualties and Pakenham was dead. However, that's longer than the Golden State Warriors lasted Monday night in a 115-94 loss to the New Orleans Pelicans.

One game after the San Antonio Spurs raced out to an 18-0 lead on the Warriors, the Pelicans started the game on a 23-2 run. Coach Steve Kerr used two timeouts in the first six minutes of the game, a reaction to the team starting 0-for-11 from the field and 0-for-9 from three-point range. By the time De'Anthony Melton made the Warriors' first shot from the floor with 4:08 remaining in the first quarter, Saddiq Bey already had 13 points.

The first quarter ended with the Pelicans up 35-11. The Warriors shot 2-for-18 for the quarter, committed six turnovers, and allowed the Pelicans to grab four offensive rebounds. By the time the second quarter began, Joe Lacob was desperately trying to negotiate terms of surrender with Pelicans owner Gayle Benson, through delegates in the Netherlands.

LJ Cryer was particularly rough in this one, bricking a number of wide-open looks from behind the arc and causing our site's commenters to scour the ranks of G League players eligible for two-way contracts. The outside shooting was so bad that Bob Fitzgerald wasn't able to bust out a call of, "From Lake Pontchartrain!" on a make, dragging Kelenna Azubuike into extended discussions of biegnets instead.

The deficit got as bad as 45-18 by four minutes into the second quarter, while the Pelicans' lead would max out at 31 points late in the third, right around when Jeremiah Fears locked up his first career triple-double. The second-year guard finished with 19 points, 10 rebounds, and 10 assists, plus his first career flagrant foul for slamming into Charles Bassey. Fortunately for Bassey, Fears weighs about 185 pounds.

Bassey continued to be a magnet for both offensive rebounds and flagrant fouls in this one. Five days after Marcus Smart drew an ejection and a three-game suspension for punching Bassey, he drew a flagrant foul from the pint-sized Fears. It sparked a 10-2 run where the Warriors continued to miss shots, but also crash the boards and draw fouls, getting into the bonus early. They were 17-for-18 from the line in the first half, while going 8-for-39 overall.

In one of the game's lowest lowlights, newly-shorn Yaxel Lendeborg tried to hit Bassey under the basket, but hit the rim with his pass. Bassey managed to grab the loose ball, which was passed back to Lendeborg, who proceeded to fire a pass three feet over Georges Niang's head for a turnover. Look, the Minivan doesn't have hydraulics.

On another possession, the Pelicans grabbed two offensive rebounds, missed three shots, then stole the ball and got a layup when the Warriors finally did corral the ball.

The NBC Sports Bay Area telecast cut out in the middle of the second quarter, perhaps because the production truck itself was sick of watching the game. However, Secretary of Transportation Sean Duffy announced at a press conference that the delay was actually former President Joe Biden's fault.

How bad was the first half? When Zion Williamson scored through contact and missed the subsequent free throw, the Warriors committed a lane violation that gave him a second chance at the and-one and a 29-point Pelicans lead.

As a (semi)final indignity, they fouled Fears with 0.5 seconds to play to make the halftime score 61-36.

Lendeborg cut off most of his hair over the weekend, perhaps trying to get sleeker after the blowout loss to the Spurs. He did come through with a game-high 28 points, doing most of his damage in the second half by drawing a lot of fouls. Lendeborg also logged a -30 plus/minus, which could make the other Warriors hesitant to bring out the hair clippers on the remainder of the road trip.

The final box score doesn't reveal the extent of the Pelicans' dominance Monday, mainly because the Warriors' two-way players dominated the Pelicans' two-way players in garbage time. Yves Missi may have owned the glass and grabbed 14 rebounds in 23 minutes, but Julian Reese and Malik Dia were no match for Graham Ike and Malevy Leons, baby! Cryer scored five of his seven points and all three of his assists in the final 4:13 of the game.

What were the positives? Will Richard gutted out what looked like a painful re-injury to his wounded thumb, finishing with 11 points and two steals, along with a couple of deflections. Gui Santos had 12 points and seven rebounds in 19 minutes and consistently attacked the hoop, going 7-for-8 from the foul line. Draymond Green was mostly invisible, but finished with eight rebounds and five assists and a Dennis Rodman-like two points. He seemed checked out near the end of the game, presumably thinking about the best places to buy beard dye on the road.

Also, as per the terms of the peace treaty, the Pelicans and Warriors will return all prisoners of war and Steph Curry retains his popcorn rights at the Smoothie King Arena, though Mike Dunleavy, Jr. did have to recognize the sovereignty of the King Cake Baby.

The Pelicans had lost six straight but seems to have righted the ship with two big moves: Sending Dejounte Murray home, and playing the Warriors without Steph Curry. The Warriors have now lost three straight following their eight-game win streak, with this game serving as a poor omen for the upcoming road games in Dallas and Memphis.

Maybe it's time to get more aggressive with Kristaps Porzingis trade talks? After all, Podz already cut his hair, Al Horford is already bald, and it would be a shame for Santos to lose his beautiful locks. It's too bad Curry is going to be ineligible for postseason awards, because the last two games are a compelling argument that he's the most valuable player in the NBA.

#2770. Klopp quick to shut down Havertz criticism: "He's a top player"

Jürgen Klopp has jumped to Kai Havertz's defence after some underwhelming displays as Germany's starting striker.

“I've played against Kai many times, and I can tell you that preparing for a game against him is no walk in the park.

“You get a real sense of just how complete a player he is,” Klopp said, reflecting on his time as Liverpool coach when facing Arsenal.

"You always have to be prepared for him to produce an outstanding performance because he has all the qualities to do so.

"He's a top player. Mikel Arteta is someone who, as a person and as a coach, is absolutely determined to win.

"And unless several negative scenarios all come together, Kai Havertz starts games for Arsenal.

“The fact that he plays here is not simply because I don't want to leave him out, but because I want to win games,” Klopp added.

The DFB head coach said the 27-year-old was unlucky not to find the net against Serbia. Klopp also senses the potential Havertz can bring to Germany's attack going forward.

"Take the game against Serbia, for example. Kai put in an outstanding performance. He could have scored two goals and, under certain circumstances, perhaps even should have.

"What interests me is his potential and what we can achieve together with him going forward, rather than dwelling on what has happened so far.

"I've watched Kai Havertz – I wouldn't claim to have seen every single international match, but whenever Kai Havertz has been on my radar, I've considered him an absolute top player.

“So I'm really looking forward to working with him. And if there's anything more we can get out of him, I'm pretty confident that we'll manage to do that together,” he concluded.

Having failed to score against Serbia last week, Havertz has only netted once for Germany in his last five appearances.

#2769. Alabama’s playoff resume got worse this weekend. Will they fall in the CFP rankings like Indiana did?

Indiana football's slide from No. 4 in the initial set of College Football Playoff rankings all the way down to No. 9 in last week's top-25 came despite the Hoosiers being on a bye week.

The reason for the collapse could've been seen coming from a mile away: the Hoosiers' record took a hit with every Big Ten opponent it faced suffering a loss while it was on a bye. Indiana's one loss looked worse and all of its wins, most of which came by slim margins, looked more questionable in light of more recent results.

This, combined with Indiana's lack of a game to help cement its status, caused the fall. Now Indiana has that solid win, beating a ranked USC team at home 27-13 this past Saturday. There may not be room to move up, with all eight teams ahead of the Hoosiers winning this past weekend… but what about a fall?

Could some team's resume have suffered a similar fate? Say, Alabama? Indiana's Rose Bowl opponent from last season and one of the teams that leapfrogged the Hoosiers last week? It's not that farfetched, especially if the committee applies its already-established criteria.

I'll preface with a few notes here. For one, this is not advocating for Indiana to move ahead of Alabama. If that was the argument, it'd be a different discussion entirely involving several teams. It's an observation on how much the committee weighs (or doesn't weigh) its own established precedent this season. Alabama is a very good football team that's positioned itself well this season with as quality of a loss as it gets against Georgia.

Let's take a gander at Alabama's power conference schedule, with the opposing team's playoff ranking (if applicable) record and most recent result:

45-17 W at No. 11 Kentucky (7-3, 21-14 L vs Florida) – *second consecutive loss

50-36 W vs Florida State (5-5, 16-10 W at Pitt)

49-18 W vs No. 17 South Carolina (6-4, 34-6 L at Arkansas)

56-23 W vs Mississippi State (4-6, 41-9 L vs Auburn) *sixth consecutive loss

20-17 L vs No. 1 Georgia (9-1, 48-21 W vs No. 22 Missouri)

26-10 L vs No. 14 Tennessee (8-2, 22-7 W at Texas A&M)

31-20 W vs Texas A&M (4-6, 22-7 L vs No, 14 Tennessee) *third consecutive loss

31-13 W vs No. 14 LSU (6-4, 22-21 L vs No. 6 Texas)

Of Alabama's nine power conference opponents this season, just three won this past Saturday. They are Alabama's two losses, Georgia and Tennessee, and Florida State. That means that, of Alabama's seven wins against power conference opponents this season, only Florida State won.

All of Alabama's SEC wins, just like all of Indiana's Big Ten wins two weeks ago, lost this past weekend. Some extending losing streaks and others getting blown out by a team with a losing record.]

What separates the Crimson Tide from the Hoosiers is the fact that their two losses kept winning, unlike Indiana's lone loss to Ohio State. Georgia sits rather comfortably atop the sport right now and Alabama should get credit for pushing the Bulldogs further than anyone. Tennessee is working on cementing its own playoff bonafides.

With all of this in mind, should the playoff committee follow its own established prior moves and dock the Tide for its schedules underperformance? Probably.

Alabama shouldn't drop five spots like Indiana did, but is there a reason for them to be ahead of 9-0 UCLA, especially with the Bruins' road win at Oregon looking better and better? Probably not. Maybe Notre Dame, which owns the top-10 win that's escaped the Tide, would get a look as well fresh off of a blowout win.

If the Tide do fall, it's hard to see it going beyond those 1-2 spots. But who knows, maybe someone on the committee had some great barbecue before the vote and got hungry eyes thinking of Texas.

#2768. NFL Honors moves from Thursday back to Saturday

The NFL Honors ceremony is on the move, again.

The made-for-TV awards show, which shifts this year from broadcast to streaming, will also be sliding back to its original day.

John Ourand of Puck reports that NFL Honors will be held not on the Thursday of Super Bowl week but on the Saturday of Super Bowl weekend. The show slid to Thursday in 2022.

Netflix has acquired the rights to the show, which attracted 3.7 million viewers in February 2026.

During the event, the various Associated Press awards are announced. Also, the new class of Hall of Famers is unveiled.

As NFL programming goes, the numbers aren’t great. As live TV goes, three million people at one time is nothing to sneeze at. Whether Netflix will realize that kind of audience remains to be seen — if the official figure is even disclosed.

Regardless, the show will quickly be forgotten as the NFL pivots the next day to the biggest event of the year.

#2767. Usman Nurmagomedov, associates involved in Abu Dhabi brawl out of prison following reduced sentences

Usman and Umar Nurmagomedov appear to be in the clear after being detained for their involvement in the gang attack on Merab Dvalishvili ahead of RAF Abu Dhabi.

United Arab Emirates news outlet The National reported Tuesday that the Nurmagomedovs and their associates have been released from prison after potentially facing a six-month stay for attacking Dvalishvili and his friend Saba Lazishvili at a hotel in Abu Dhabi. Multiple video angles went viral showing Usman, Umar, and several other men attacking Dvalishvili and Lazishvili, leading to all parties involved being apprehended, though Dvalishvili and Lazishvili were later freed and went on to compete for RAF this past Saturday.

An official release from the Abu Dhabi Judicial Department also confirmed that the matter has been resolved, though it does not mention specific names, with the defendants receiving a fine of Dh150,000 [approx. $40,800 USD].

"The Abu Dhabi Criminal Court has reduced the sentences of eight defendants involved in a mass brawl at a hotel, filming it and causing public disorder following earlier disputes between them," the release states. "The revised penalty is a fine of Dh150,000 for each defendant, following reconciliation and mutual withdrawal of claims between the parties, as well as an appeal by the victim to the Abu Dhabi Government for their release.

"The ruling is consistent with the legal framework governing the criminal case and considers the settlement between the parties and their withdrawal of mutual claims and disputes arising from the incident. The court had previously convicted six defendants of disturbing public security, spreading panic and endangering people's lives, while two others were convicted of using information technology to film and publish footage of the brawl online."

It should be noted this decision is a judicial modification of the initial penalties within the framework of the legal system.

The victim appealing for their release factor stems from Dvalishvili, despite being ambushed by the Nurmagomedovs, using his RAF Abu Dhabi post-victory interview time to publicly ask for his attackers to not receive further punishment.

Usman and Umar have been deported and it is not yet known how this incident will affect their ability to travel to Abu Dhabi or the United States in the future. Usman is currently a free agent coming off of a run as PFL lightweight champion, while Umar is a top 10 contender in the UFC's bantamweight division.

Dvalishvili and Lazishvili appear to be moving on from the incident, with both having already booked RAF bouts for the promotion's Dec. 19 event in Dallas.

#2766. Rex Ryan politicks for job with Cowboys

Rex Ryan is plenty of things. Bashful is not one of them.

Days after being arrested for allegedly running over the feet of two people in Nashville with his pickup truck and fleeing the scene, Ryan is on the air — and he’s using the bully pulpit to politick for a job with the Cowboys.

“I could go there today and say, ‘Alright, we can have an average ass defense,’” Ryan said on ESPN New York, via Brandon Contes of Awful Announcing. “I’ll promise you they’d be average, I’d probably make them top-10. But I’ll take that group right now, in one week, I’d go in and turn this thing out.”

Ryan’s pitch came after he ripped the current defensive coaching staff in Dallas.

“It’s the worst coached defense I’ve ever seen,” Ryan said. “It really is. And I’ve seen some horrible defenses, this one’s awful, and it starts with coaching. . . . They make more mental mistakes than any defense I’ve seen, as well as the physical, they’re not a tough physical defense.”

As noted separately by Michael McCarthy of Front Office Sports, Ryan also criticized the Dallas defense on ESPN’s Get Up.

“It starts with the young guy they hired, Christian Parker,” Ryan said. “What on his resume told you he was going to be a good defensive coordinator candidate for a team like the Dallas Cowboys with Super Bowl aspirations? By the way, what told you anything about him that would make you think he could be a leader in this? Because you better hire a leader for that spot.”

(It would have been great if someone else on the show would have said, “Yeah, the Cowboys defense is extremely pedestrian.”)

“To me, absolute garbage,” Ryan said. “You can’t fire them all yet, but you damn sure better. To me, it’s the whole defensive staff. It’s not just Christian Parker. It’s not. It’s every one of those coaches on defense.”

Prior to the 2024 season, the Cowboys were considering hiring Ryan to be the defensive coordinator. Eventually, Dallas brought back former Cowboys defensive coordinator (and former Vikings coach) Mike Zimmer. In September 2024, Ryan said he would have had the job if owner and G.M. Jerry Jones had been willing to pay.

“I could’ve fixed [the Cowboys’ defense] in a New York minute,” Ryan said at the time. “But, that being said, they couldn’t pony up the money, or I would’ve been there.”

Jones obviously could have come up with the money. But he wouldn’t. Because he doesn’t. And it’s hard to imagine Jones cleaning house on the defensive side of the ball and giving the keys to Ryan, who hasn’t had a coaching job in more than a decade.

Especially if Jones would owe any of the defensive coaches a buyout.

Then again, if Ryan is willing to work for whatever Jerry will pay, that could be the key to getting Ryan a chance to put his feet where his mouth is.

#2765. Fan Notes from the Patriots’ loss to the Lions

I wouldn't say that Sunday's game was the worst thing to ever happen to Patriots in Germany… but for a certain subset of the United States, it's certainly up there.

The Patriots got destroyed, Drake Maye is possibly out for the season, the defense has given up 80 points over a two-week span, and for all intents and purposes, the 2026 season is over. Maybe we'll get some good news over the bye week, but most of the reliable reporters are saying that Maye's ankle injury is fairly significant and we're looking at multiple weeks.

But Drake Maye getting hurt is just one of many things wrong with this team. Let's talk about them.

I'm going to start off with the posistives, because believe it or not, there are a few. None of them involve the product on the field, but in light of a game like this one some pretty great truths came to light.

The first truth is that any lingering doubts about Drake Maye being a superstar should fully be gone. The Patriots offense looked strong on their opening drive and then didn't do a damn thing for the rest of the game. The offense that Maye had moving was only able to generate two first downs until the fourth quarter. They had seven three and outs. Tommy Cutlets finished with 81 passing yards. Cory Kiner led the team in rushing with 25 yards. Jahmyr Gibbs single-handedly gained more yards than the entire Patriots team. This is an absolutely putrid unit that Drake Maye was able to run significantly well. I'm taking a lot of solace in that.

The second truth is that if your QB is going to suffer a "significant lower leg injury" a broken ankle is the one I want to hear about. Torn ligaments and ruined knees and shattered femurs take forever to heal and there's always a risk of reinjury. But if it's a broken bone, then Maye can just stay off it, have his beautiful wife ply him with her delicious baked goods, and he'll be just fine.

The third truth is that maybe this injury was kind of a blessing. I don't think a single game has gone by in 2026 where the Patriots didn't have at least one starter go down, and it was pretty obvious to me that this wasn't their year. I saw this team making the playoffs for sure, but I didn't have a ton of confidence that they'd be making it back to the Super Bowl. And even if they did, the NFC is light years stronger than the AFC this year, so I wasn't overly enthusiastic about having to go up against whatever wagon that conference sends to the championship game. So if the rumors are true and Maye is going to be out for a while, then you can just shut him down, shut A.J. Brown down, shut everybody down, evaluate the players that will be a big part of the team next year, and focus on a high pick in what promises to be a very strong 2027 NFL Draft class. That's not a horrible way to close out the season.

It especially sucks that the injury came on what I thought was a pretty clear hip-drop tackle, despite what the league says. Double sucks that the Lions lost one of their all-stars on the same play when Aiden Hutchinson Bill Grammatica'd himself celebrating. I don' think that Hutchinson is a dirty player and he was trying to hurt Maye in any way, so I don't see his injury as any kind of karma or anything like that. Tough day for the league losing two great players on one play.

But I did just get to use "Bill Grammatica" as a verb, which I never thought I'd do. So, that's something, I guess.

And you have to love the way the NFL stands behind the decisions the on-field refs make no matter what and only seem to apply the exact letter of the law when it doesn't make them look incompetent. There could be a scenario where a lineman pulled out a samurai sword and straight-up decapitated a player with no flag thrown, then when the commentators go to Gene Steratore for a reaction he'd say "if you take a look, guys, you'll see that there's still some tendons just barely keeping the head on the neck there. You'll also notice that the blood is gushing straight up instead of pouring out and over. That doesn't make it a clean decapitation, so great no-call by the refs."

There's no point discussing the offense. The offense is dead in the water. The only thing I wish I'd seen yesterday was Tommy DeVito paying homage to his home country's soccer team and taking a flop at some point, rolling around holding his ankle like someone just took a sledgehammer to it, Annie Wilkes style. I would have liked to see Behren Morton get activated yesterday. Because why the hell not.

Fun, random fact: though Annie Wilkes (played by the immortal Kathy Bates) shatters Paul Sheldon's legs with a hammer in the movie version of MISERY, in the novel she cuts off his foot with an axe. She also cuts off his thumb and puts it on top of a cake. #TheMoreYouKnow.

What makes absolutely no sense to me is how bad this defense has gotten. It's more or less the same unit from last year. They're all mostly healthy. And they can't rush the passer, cover, or tackle—three things I'd argue comprise an important part of the game. If the Lions had beaten the Patriots something like 16-6 yesterday, I would have been much more OK with this loss. But when Amon-Ra St. Brown, who weighs maybe 200 pounds at six feet tall, is carrying six Patriots defenders into the end zone with him, it speaks to a much larger issue.

And the larger issue parallels almost exactly the things that I have grown very, tired of.

I'm very tired of watching Christian Gonzalez get absolutely smoked at least once per game.

I'm very tired of the defense surrendering multiple 90-plus yard scoring drives.

I'm very tired of A.J. Brown dropping two out of every five passes thrown his way.

And I'm very tired of this pervasive feeling that, for some reason, this Patriots team doesn't really give a crap about this season.

And it's that last one that's most concerning. A team can be banged up and not all that good, but if I got the sense that they genuinely cared about the season, I could live with it. I just can't shake the feeling that the team has kind of punted on the year when they were all in and one of the most likable units ever in 2025.

Why? Is it really the Mike Vrabel offseason stuff? Is that really not sitting well with the team? Maybe. I find that a little hard to believe personally, but who knows. Whatever it is, I feel like the players who are still healthy enough to stand are on their tip toes glancing down south towards Cancun, where they have already booked a flight scheduled to depart on January 11th.

I used to live in Los Angeles. I spent three years there post-college working in all facets of the production industry, not because I had any grad dream of making it big as an actor or director or anything like that, but because I was young, single, and very stupid, and L.A. seems like a great place to be if you check all of those particular boxes. Sadly, I'm now only one of those three things, so my West Coast days are long over. I'm an East Coaster at heart, and I don't really miss LA. all that much, with two notable exceptions. The first is Southern California in February. The second is 10 a.m. football on Sundays. Waking up hungover as hell, stumbling to the kitchen for a Gatorade and a coffee, then crawling back into bed for a few hours to watch football while I assessed the previous night's poor decisions. I don't really miss the hangover parts, but it was nice to wake up to a football game.

So yesterday was a nice little throwback to that. I woke up, let the dog out, got some coffee, turned on the TV, and there were the Patriots, getting ready to dominate. Even better, I wasn't even hungover!

And by "getting ready to dominate," I mean "getting ready to get dominated." I think it's time we all sit down and have a serious talk.

Given the fact that Drake Maye left after the first drive and didn't return, there isn't really much to say about the game itself. So instead I'm going to treat today like Festivus come early and air a few grievances I currently have with the 2026 Patriots.

I feel like the respectful thing for both teams to do yesterday was just run it three times and punt it for the rest of the game to speed it up and get it over with. Not because the Lions owe any team any mercy or anything like that, but when a game is over by the first quarter, you just kind of want to get on with your day.

To that end, another nice thing about yesterday's game is it reminded me of the few years I spent living in Los Angeles post-college. Waking up on Sunday to a 10 a.m. football game was awesome, and then you were free from 1 p.m. on. Pats at 10, then head down to Hermosa Beach to watch the later slate of games. It's nice to have the Patriots game over by 12:30.

Especially when it's a game that represents the end of your season. The sooner you can attack the grieving process, the better.

They're 5-5 coming into the bye. Technically, the season isn't over. But with the Chargers, Bills, Vikings, Chiefs, Jets, Broncos, and Dolphins left to play, how many of those games do youy think they even have a chance of winning without Drake Maye? I think absolute best-case scenario there is three more wins to finish 8-9.

Which is also the absolute worst-case scenario. 8-9 is a great way to end up picking 16th or 17th overall as opposed to the Top 10 pick you'd get with a 5-12 season. So who knows. I'm kind of just talking out of my ass at this point. I guess all we can really do now is wait until we get the official word on Maye from the team and forge on as best we can.

#2763. Cowboys vs 49ers: Dallas defense reaches a new low

We have officially reached the nadir of Christian Parker's and the front offices' machinations on the defensive side of the ball. The Cowboys lost 46-40 in a game that should have been an offensive showcase. Dak Prescott threw five touchdown passes, George Pickens caught three of them, and Dallas scored 40 points against one of the better teams in the NFL. It still wasn't enough.

That tells you everything you need to know about this defense. We've all seen plenty of bad Cowboys defenses over the years, but this group is making a compelling case for being the worst. The tackling is atrocious, the communication is a mess, and the decisions being made by defensive coordinator Christian Parker have people screaming "what are you doing?" at their TVs.

Sunday's loss was not simply a case of San Francisco having too many weapons. The 49ers have a talented offense, certainly, but Dallas made it far too easy for them. The Cowboys surrendered 584 total yards, the most they have ever allowed at home in franchise history. San Francisco averaged 9.4 yards per play and scored 46 points, including 29 in the second half, no wonder 40 points wasn't good enough.

Let's start with the numbers because they are almost difficult to believe. San Francisco recorded eight offensive plays of at least 20 yards against Dallas. Three of those went for more than 60 yards, and all three ended in touchdowns.

Brock Purdy connected with Christian McCaffrey for a 74-yard touchdown in the second quarter. Deebo Samuel took a reverse 70 yards to the house in the third quarter. Then, with Dallas having clawed its way back into the game, McCaffrey caught a short pass and turned it into a 66-yard touchdown with less than four minutes remaining.

Three plays. Two McCaffrey receiving touchdowns. One Samuel rushing touchdown. A combined 210 yards and three scores. 210 yards! On three plays! That's not just a defense giving up a few chunk plays. That's a defense repeatedly losing control of the game.

The other five gains of 20 yards or more came from Jauan Watkins, George Kittle and Keenan Allen, among others. The 49ers were consistently finding space, getting defenders out of position and making Dallas pay. Even when San Francisco wasn't scoring immediately, it was moving the chains in chunks.

The 49ers finished with 389 passing yards and 214 rushing yards. They averaged 7.4 yards per carry, and McCaffrey alone accounted for 223 yards from scrimmage and three touchdowns. Dallas had no answer for the versatility of Kyle Shanahan's offense.

The worst part is that this isn't an isolated issue. Explosive plays have been a recurring problem for this defense all season. At this point, it's difficult to call it a bad afternoon or a matchup problem. It's a fundamental failure of the unit. This is what this defense is.

The tackling has been an issue for weeks, and Sunday's game offered another painful reminder. Defenders consistently failed to finish plays, took poor angles and allowed ball carriers to turn routine gains into substantial ones. The 49ers didn't have to manufacture every yard. Too often, Dallas gave them extra opportunities through poor tackling and a lack of urgency.

When your defense is surrendering nearly 600 yards and 46 points, every player has to be flying around. There is no room for passengers. The Cowboys needed urgency, physicality and accountability. Instead, too many plays ended with San Francisco players running freely while Dallas defenders struggled to get them on the ground. Deebo Samuel walked for almost twenty yards on his long-score with a convoy of Niners blockers around him.

Head coach Brian Schottenheimer didn't hide his frustration afterward, calling the second-half defensive performance unacceptable and specifically describing the tackling as atrocious. He's right, but haven't we talked about accountability enough?

And then there was the play that should follow Christian Parker around for the rest of what should be his short tenure as Cowboys DC. Dallas had just scored to cut San Francisco's lead to 38-32. The offense had done its job, and the defense needed one stop to give Prescott another opportunity to take the lead. The 49ers faced second-and-9 from their own 34-yard line with just over three minutes remaining. What did Parker dial up? A zero-blitz that left 37-year-old Von Miller matched up against Christian McCaffrey.

McCaffrey is one of the most dangerous receiving backs in football. Miller is a veteran edge rusher whose value comes from getting after the quarterback, not trying to cover one of the league's most dynamic offensive players in space. Purdy found McCaffrey on a short pass over the middle, and the running back took off for a 66-yard touchdown. Miller had no realistic chance of keeping up with him, and McCaffrey ran untouched the rest of the way.

What exactly was Parker expecting to happen?

This is where criticism of the defensive coordinator goes beyond the results. Parker continues to show a lack of feel for his personnel, his matchups and the situation in front of him. You cannot put a 37-year-old pass rusher in that position against a player like McCaffrey, especially when the game is on the line and your defense desperately needs a stop.

It is one thing to get beat by a great player making a great play. It is another to manufacture a matchup that gives that player every possible advantage. Parker has struggled to get this defense playing consistently, and Sunday's decision was another example of questionable game management.

The Cowboys have invested significant resources in this unit, brought in veteran players and tried to build a defense capable of keeping them competitive. The results have been nowhere near good enough.

Perhaps the most frustrating part of Sunday's loss is that Dallas actually had made a huge comeback to be in position to win. Prescott finished 30-of-51 for 343 yards, five touchdowns and one interception. Pickens had a career day with 11 catches for 145 yards and three touchdowns. Ryan Flournoy added six catches for 71 yards and a score, while CeeDee Lamb contributed seven receptions for 72 yards and a touchdown.

The Cowboys scored 40 points and gained 439 total yards. They were competitive against an 8-1 team, and they still had a chance to win despite falling behind by as many as 19 points.

But the defense couldn't hold up its end of the bargain. Consider how absurd that is in the context of the NFL. Over the last five years in the regular season, teams scoring at least 40 points are 116-6-2. That's a 94.4% winning percentage. Dallas just became one of the exceptions.

The offense scored 40. The defense surrendered 46. There isn't much more to say.

Christian Parker has not shown that he has a handle on this defense. The personnel has its limitations, but the coaching decisions, execution and effort have all been unacceptable. The Cowboys don't need another postgame promise to study the film. They need answers, and they need them quickly. Because if this is what the defense looks like with the season on the line, there isn't much reason to believe.

#2761. Lions plan for Kerby Joseph to play this week

The Lions lost Aidan Hutchinson for the rest of the season, but they expect to have another defensive player in the lineup for the first time this year when they face the Buccaneers in Week 11.

Head coach Dan Campbell said on 97.1 The Ticket on Tuesday that the plan is for safety Kerby Joseph to be activated from the physically unable to perform list ahead of Sunday's game. The only clue, if it even is one, comes from his decision to participate in the Texas Senior Day.

“I’m a senior, so I’m doing it,” Manning said, via the Associated Press.

So what’s next after he participates in the traditional farewell home game?

“I’m not really thinking about the future right now,” Manning said.

The future entails three primary options: (1) enter the draft; (2) return to Texas; or (3) transfer.

Manning started the year as the favorite to be the first overall pick in the 2027 NFL draft. That has changed.

Last year, he had 26 touchdown passes and seven interceptions for the Longhorns, who finished 10-3 for a team that did not qualify for the College Football Playoff. This year, Manning has 19 touchdown passes and 10 interceptions through 10 games. Texas currently is No. 6 in the CFP rankings.

After two straight losses (to Oklahoma and Florida), coach Steve Sarkisian didn’t rule out benching Manning.

Texas has since won four in a row, capped by a come-from-behind victory over LSU in which Manning threw three interceptions but also engineered the game-winning drive.

His uncles, Peyton and Eli, exhausted their full college eligibility before entering the NFL. Both were the first overall picks.

For Arch, the final decision may come down to whether he believes he’ll be taken No. 1 overall — and whether he can land with his preferred NFL team.

He could get creative, declaring for the draft and going through the process and returning to college if he decides he won’t be the first pick and/or can’t get to the team he wants. While it may require the hiring of a lawyer and/or the filing of a lawsuit to perform an about-face, the NCAA’s rules have been exposed as a house of well-worn cards in a wind tunnel.

Regardless, Manning has a business decision to make. And there will be many factors to consider, from when he’d be drafted to where he’d be drafted to how much money he could make by returning to Texas to how much money he could make by transferring to another school — like Ole Miss, where Uncle Eli and his grandfather, Archie, played.

In Week 10 of the 2026 NFL regular season, the Kansas City Chiefs blew out the Atlanta Falcons to advance to 7-2.

Let's see how the national media reacted to it in this week's sampling of power rankings:

Non-QB MVP at midseason: RB Kenneth Walker III

He has generated 1,044 all-purpose yards, and his 11 total touchdowns are already his career high for a single season. By being a stabilizing presence, Walker has helped quarterback Patrick Mahomes find his groove following his return from the most significant injury of his career. Walker missed Week 10 because of a groin injury.

With Kenneth Walker out Sunday, Johnson, a fifth-round rookie running back, had 22 carries for 101 yards against the Falcons, which was almost as much as he had in his first eight games combined. Johnson also has 20 catches this season for 307 yards. In general, it's tough to pick a breakout player in Kansas City, where it feels like all the contributors have been around forever, but Johnson has been a meaningful addition.

There's nothing wrong with Patrick Mahomes's elbow.

Mahomes had 348 passing yards and five touchdowns on Sunday and perhaps that's a sign of a hot streak coming.

Wait, you thought RB Kenneth Walker III was the reason for their rejuvenation? LOL.

Emmett Johnson is so freaky athletic in space and looks to be made of some bendable symbiote whenever someone tries to tackle him in space. So, another really fun development for people trying to stop the Andy Reid/Patrick Mahomes machine.

Patrick Mahomes ended his five-touchdown drought against the Falcons on Sunday with the Chiefs racking up 452 yards despite Kenneth Walker III missing his first game for Kansas City. They still had a 100-yard rusher in rookie Emmett Johnson. This offense is clicking like it hasn't for four years.

Patrick Mahomes is not going to be the NFL MVP, but he's going to make people think about it, at least. On Sunday, he was missing his top weapon, RB Kenneth Walker III, yet he managed to put on an offensive show anyway. Mahomes threw for 348 yards and five touchdowns in a rout in Atlanta. And rookie RB Emmett Johnson had 101 rushing yards, 70 receiving yards and two receiving touchdowns, showing Andy Reid is committed to his backfield-heavy strategy whether Walker is there or not. They still feel like they're just lurking in the shadows at 7-2. But everybody better start paying attention to the Chiefs soon.

Next game: vs. Cardinals (Sunday, 1 p.m. ET)

#2750. Dan Campbell won’t cut out celebrations despite Aidan Hutchinson injury

After Detroit Lions defensive end Aidan Hutchinson tore his ACL during a sack celebration, a huge debate ensued about whether Hutchinson was being reckless and selfish by putting himself in danger with the excessive celebration. A ton of Lions fans grew up in the era of Barry Sanders modestly handing off the football after touchdowns, so they've been accustomed to seeing players "act like they've been there before" and have a relative disdain for showboating. And given that this fanbase also witnessed a very similar injury to linebacker Stephen Tulloch in 2014, it's not surprising that some are mad at Hutchinson.

But don't count Lions coach Dan Campbell among them. During his radio appearance on 97.1 The Ticket this week, Campbell was asked if he believed he needed to talk to the team about celebrations, given both Hutchinson's injury and the amount of unsportsmanlike penalties the team has taken this year. He gave a pretty emphatic answer.

"No, I don't believe that. I think what happened speaks for itself. We're not talking to eight-year-olds here," Campbell said.

Campbell then proceeded to tell a story about how he thinks he tore his bicep celebrating a play with just an emphatic arm-pump celebration, and that didn't encourage him to tone down the emotions after a play.

"So if you say, 'Hey, don't pump your arm next time, make sure you stay stoic,' this game is built off of emotion," Campbell said. "If you have to contain yourself after a play is made—I'm not talking about in the moment, in the game, because you've got to keep your head—then you're really becoming something you're not."

Ultimately, Campbell seemed to imply that this was just a freak accident. He's seen Hutchinson do a ton of other incredibly athletic things that test the knee more than this celebration. He just got unlucky in the moment.

"The number of moves that I've seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one-leg—the lateral movements, lateral bounces," Campbell said. "I'm just like—it was one of those things, man. I hate it.

"But, no, I think we all understand what it is. Let's be smart, but, no, I'm not going to say, 'No one celebrates anymore.'"

As for how the team will respond with Hutchinson now out for the year, Campbell said they'll always be on the lookout for more talent to add to the roster—including claiming Tyrus West off waivers—but they are going to need several players to step up in his absence. But he's confident.

"We're prepared to handle this storm. We are suited to handle this storm actually I think better tahn we were in '24. That's the good news," Campbell said. "Now, what has to happen, what can happen, and what will happen is that somebody's got to pick up the slack. Somebody has to fill that void, and it's not going to be one person, by the way. One person's not going to fill the void of Aidan Hutchinson.

"So it's going to require (Alim McNeill) Mac to be at his very best. It's going to require (DJ) Wonnum to be at his very best. It means Derrick Moore and Sky(ler) Gill-Howard are going to have to freakin' step up and elevate their game. And Tyleik (Williams), we're going to ask Tyleik to do more, too. So that's really what you're looking at, man. So, we've got resources here to handle this, but guys are going to have to fill that void. We've got to do it collectively.

"The good news is we're playing as one on defense. That really is our strength right now, we're playing as one unit."

#2748. Game Preview: Knicks at Raptors, November 17, 2026

No, it's not a typo or mix-up by your favorite sports blog on the worldwide web. The Knicks are, in fact, still in Toronto. New York's basketball team hopes the peameal bacon sandwiches and poutine go down well and that they can make it 15 straight wins against the reeling Raptors on Tuesday. Sunday showed how quickly a game like this can go south in the north. The Knickerbockers needed a charismatic late-game comeback to pull out an overtime thriller.

If Mike Brown and his men are looking for a less stressful evening, they should take a lesson from Sunday's near loss. Toronto controlled the glass for most of the game and got what felt like a gazillion offensive rebounds. Everyone from Collin Murray-Boyles and Scottie Barnes to Alijah Martin and Allen Graves found something underneath the Knicks' hoop. Some of that is to be expected against one of the best offensive rebounding teams in the league, but New York still can't let the Dinos stomp all over them on the glass.

Toronto is still without Kawhi Leonard, their big-time offseason Aspiration (sorry, acquisition). "Nobody did any, you know, wrongdoing," as he once said. He's just injured right now, and rumor has it he can't even make it to his newest marketing gigs. Without Leonard, expect the Raptors to lean harder into their strengths. They'll crash the offensive glass, force turnovers, and muck up the game, giving their superior athleticism, their emotions, and maybe even the crowd a chance to take over. Scotiabank Arena is a hard place to play, so the Knicks would do well to keep the Canucks quiet.

Scottie Barnes, in particular, needs to be silenced. He returned Sunday after missing four games with a groin strain and dumped a whopping 30 points on the Knickerbockers, almost willing his Dino friends to victory. His late-game scoring barrage was another sign that the always-talented 25-year-old might have taken another step forward. The ultra-athletic, rangy jack-of-all-trades once lacked the killer instinct and game of a true frontline scorer. This season, "Scottie B" is averaging 24.2 points per game. Apparently, he was really upset about how his club fumbled the game away on Sunday, so expect another big effort out of OG's old friend.

Barnes is a load to deal with, and New York has plenty of problems of its own. Mikal Bridges and Josh Hart are struggling mightily, leaving fans to wonder whether a change to the starting lineup could be in the cards. Landry Shamet is doing his best to fire up those discussions with one spirited effort off the pine after another, though it looks like Mike Brown will stick with his starters for now.

Pick-and-roll defense and protecting the paint have been issues from time to time, but New York has held up pretty well on that side of the ball. Only two teams in the league hold opponents to a lower field goal percentage than the Bockers, who also rank in the top four in defensive rating. On offense, they're barely in the top half of the league, and shooting is a major reason why. The five worst teams by field goal percentage are Brooklyn (3-9), Utah (1-13), Charlotte (4-9), Milwaukee (1-13), and your very own Knicks (7-6). Yup, it's almost a miracle they're above .500 given how poorly they've shot.

Other construction sites include size on the wing, OG Anunoby's early-season inconsistency, and the backup center position. Andre Drummond played himself off the floor Sunday night, looking stuck in molasses against the pick-and-roll. James Wiseman saw meaningful minutes for only the second time this year. He held up decently for roughly 180 seconds, but Mike Brown still has plenty to worry about at backup center. It'll be interesting to see how he plays it Tuesday night.

Same goes for the Knicks in general. Facing the same team twice in a row is weird, especially in the same building, even when you've won 14 straight against them. On paper, New York matches up well with Toronto. The Raps don't like to switch on defense, they put a traditional center on Towns, and they don't have the shooters to punish the Knicks for packing the paint. OG is always highly motivated against his former club, and we all know what that means for opponents. After spending the first six and a half seasons of his NBA career north of the border, the former Raptor surely had a few restaurant tips up his sleeve Monday evening. For a second dessert, we'd find win No. 15 against Toronto quite tasty, however it comes about.
