#2717. Observations after Sixers shake off slow start, Embiid injury scare to win

Observations after Sixers shake off slow start, Embiid injury scare to win originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers brushed off their blowout loss to the Celtics and got back in the win column Thursday night.

They overcame a first-quarter deficit as high as 17 points and earned a 123-112 home win over the Raptors. The Sixers moved to 10-3 and Toronto fell to 5-7.

Tyrese Maxey had 27 points and eight assists. Joel Embiid added 20 points.

Jaylen Brown tallied 18 points and six rebounds. VJ Edgecombe posted 15 points, eight rebounds and a season-high eight assists. Anfernee Simons scored 17 points and shot 5 for 6 from three-point range.

RJ Barrett recorded 31 points and 14 rebounds. Immanuel Quickley had 26 points and six assists.

The Sixers were without LeBron James (low back/sciatica management), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

The team told reporters Thursday morning that Bona will be re-evaluated in approximately one week and that Barlow participated in shootaround. The 23-year-old forward also went through a pregame workout on the Xfinity Mobile Arena floor. There's no formal timetable for his return.

Toronto's sidelined players included Kawhi Leonard and Scottie Barnes.

The Sixers will face the Pacers on Sunday afternoon in Philadelphia. Here are observations on their victory over the Raptors:

Dean Wade began the game in James' normal spot. The Sixers started slowly, missing their first foul field goals and opening 1 for 8 from three-point range.

He stayed down after setting a ball screen and having an apparent knee-to-knee collision with Ja'Kobe Walter. The Sixers called timeout with 8:30 left in the first quarter and Embiid grabbed at his right knee. He eventually rose with assistance from teammates and Sixers head athletic trainer Kevin Johnson, then limped back to the locker room. Jabari Walker entered.

At the 5:26 mark of the first quarter, Embiid checked back in to cheers from the home fans.

"I knew that he didn't twist it or anything," Maxey said. "I knew it was a knee-to-knee, so I wasn't too worried. He kept saying, 'I just got kneed.' That still hurts, though."

Embiid ended up logging 28 minutes, though he certainly wanted to play more.

"He was very, very motivated to play," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "It was hard to get him out for his scheduled rests tonight, which is a good problem sometimes."

While Embiid's return was a major positive development, the Sixers fell further behind. The Raptors' lead swelled to 32-16 with a Jamison Battle three-pointer and Nurse burned a timeout.

Simons sunk a buzzer-beating three to conclude the first quarter, cutting the Sixers' deficit to nine points.

"Those are big momentum plays that usually have some weight to them and carry you into the next moment," Nurse said.

The Sixers kept rolling against a shorthanded Toronto team playing on the second night of a back-to-back. Simons nailed a triple to kick off the scoring in the second quarter. Brown buried a mid-range jumper and Edgecombe jammed in a crowd-pleasing dunk to give the Sixers a 35-34 edge.

Brown and Edgecombe shined in the second quarter both as individuals and as a duo. The Sixers need Edgecombe to attack a bit more offensively when he shares the floor with Brown and bench players. It doesn't hurt for the second-year guard to have that clarity about his role in those units.

"He's one of our better playmakers," Maxey said of Edgecombe. "He's really good at getting downhill and creating for himself and others."

The Sixers' only three bench rotation players were Walker, Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope (11 points, five assists). In addition to Walker, Wade got center minutes behind Embiid.

Embiid picked up his third foul with 2:23 left in the second quarter and had to take a seat. The teams were tied at 60 apiece through two periods.

The game remained even for much of the third, but Maxey fueled an 11-0 Sixers run late in the quarter.

His speed and the Sixers' collective focus on pushing the pace whenever possible allowed the team to get regular points before Toronto could set its defense. Maxey zoomed in for a layup, then tossed the ball ahead to Brown for an easy bucket. Brown assisted a Simons three. Walker converted a fast-break lay-in and canned a three on the first possession of the fourth quarter. All of a sudden, the Sixers held a 101-87 lead.

Brown, Edgecombe and the bench gave the Sixers more good minutes early in the fourth quarter. They sealed the deal without any late drama. And the fans chanting for a Tacko Fall appearance got their wish with a final-minute entrance by the 7-foot-6 center.

#2716. Jerry Jones on Christian Parker's status: We're talking about that

Cowboys defensive coordinator Christian Parker failed to get a vote of confidence from head coach Brian Schottenheimer on Monday and he didn’t get one from Cowboys owner Jerry Jones on Tuesday either.

Parker’s defense has underperformed all season and they hit another low point during Sunday’s 46-40 loss to the 49ers. The Cowboys gave up 584 yards over the course of the game and the lowlights of the unit’s performance included three touchdowns that covered at least 66 yards.

Schottenheimer said on Monday that the Cowboys were “still in the process of going through all the film and evaluation of where we stand” when asked if there would be a change involving Parker. During an appearance on 105.3 The Fan on Tuesday, Schottenheimer’s comments were brought up and Jones was asked if the team is considering any changes involving Parker’s role or responsibilities heading into this week’s game against the Titans.

“We’re talking about that. I’m not going to talk about it here, but we are talking about those things,” Jones said.

Jones gave a similar answer to a question about simplifying the defense, but having these conversations 10 weeks into the season means that there’s an acknowledgement that the status quo isn’t working. It also means that anything other than a significant shift to the team’s defensive operations is unlikely to result in the kind of change the Cowboys need to salvage their season in the final seven games.

#2715. Week 11 Fantasy Football Waiver Wire Debate: Michael Mayer Vs Chig Okonkwo

Week 10 is behind us, with the exception of Monday Night Football, so it's time to look toward the future. That starts with the waiver wire. There are a ton of valuable pieces available on the waiver wire this week, but you have to decide which ones to prioritize.

There will be some key tight ends you could target this week. Two of those potential studs are Michael Mayer and Chig Okonkwo. Both tight ends could be valuable assets both in the short term and the long term.

Mayer has had a solid season, catching 41 of 49 targets for 404 yards and one touchdown. Surprisingly, while he saw 11 targets in the two games Brock Bowers missed this season, they weren't even close to his best games. However, that does not mean that his value will not rise if Bowers misses time due to his latest ankle injury.

The oft-injured Bowers was forced from Week 10 with an ankle injury after attempting to return to the game, before eventually being ruled out. While this doesn't seem to be an overly serious injury that will land him on injured reserve, he could miss some time. Currently, he is being called day-to-day, but this Raiders' regime has also consistently lied to us about injuries.

We love Mayer's value in Week 11 against the Denver Broncos if Bowers is out. Denver is allowing the third-most fantasy points to tight ends this season.

Even if Bowers plays, Mayer still has value, but his upside is clearly capped when Bowers is available. Bowers' status will have to be monitored heading into Week 11 and perhaps beyond that.

Okonkwo hasn't quite put it all together, but everything seems to be in place for Okonkwo to explode. He's a 27-year-old, impressive athlete who we have seen flash many times before. The Commanders are desperate for viable pass-catching targets, and Okonkwo has seen at least seven targets in three of the past four games, while topping 50 yards in three of the past five games.

Unfortunately, he hasn't found the end zone, which has damaged his fantasy production. Needless to say, he is clearly the lead tight end in Washington and one of Jayden Daniels' top targets.

He holds league-winning upside, and nothing about the situation is going to change. Also, he has a solid matchup this week against the Cincinnati Bengals, who are allowing the ninth-most fantasy points to tight ends this season.

Despite the fact that Mayer is the better start in Week 11 if Bowers is sidelined, we are going with Okonkwo. The only thing that could change our minds on this is if Bowers were to be placed on IR.

Okonkwo will have much more upside for the remainder of the season and could become a legitimate TE1 based on the talent and opportunity. Things are far from a lock to play out that way, but we are willing to roll the dice on the long-term upside.

Observations after Embiid posts season-high 37 points in Sixers' win over Nets originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Sixers head coach Nick Nurse kept an eight-man rotation, only playing Simons, Walker and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope off the bench until the final 18.5 seconds.

The Sixers' collective effort on the glass was a major strength in the first half.

All of the starters besides Maxey recorded at least one offensive rebound. Brown had a double-double before halftime and poured in 19 points over the first two quarters.

The Sixers' focus and intensity slipped in the third quarter.

Nolan Traore's layup cut Brooklyn's deficit to single digits. Michael Porter Jr. hit a long two over Caldwell-Pope to trim the Sixers' lead to six points.

The Sixers concluded the third period on a high note. Walker sunk a buzzer-beating three to lift his team to a 105-95 edge. Again, the 24-year-old provided much more than intangible energy and made meaningful, momentum-shifting plays.

The action stopped with 9:02 left in the fourth quarter after Brown was fouled by Randle. The Sixers' star forward appeared to have his forehead collide with Randle's face. He was down for several minutes before going to the bench.

Randle needed stitches in his mouth before re-entering with 5:01 to go, Amy Fadool reported on the NBC Sports Philadelphia broadcast. Brown subbed back in with 6:02 remaining and a bandage on the cut above his left eye. However, he exited at the 1:44 mark and did not return.

Nurse told reporters in Brooklyn that Brown was out at the end of the game because of "tightness," according to PhillyVoice's Adam Aaronson.

After their shaky third quarter, the Sixers were ultimately fine in the fourth and reached a dozen wins.

Egor Demin's jumper pulled the Nets to within 125-121, but Embiid then iced the game by swishing a corner three.

That may be important moving forward, as Walker has been one of the top utilized backs in the league so far in 2026.

#2712. NFL Panic Meter: Should Steelers be worried after embarrassing blowout loss?

One bad game shouldn't eliminate eight games of solid football.

But that was a seriously bad game for the Pittsburgh Steelers.

Get out-gained 337-40 in a half and there will be some reasons for concern. The Steelers were absolutely blown out by the Cincinnati Bengals, and it didn't help that it happened on a big Sunday night stage. The Steelers were 6-2 going into the game and while there was skepticism over their staying power, nobody expected them to get destroyed like that.

The Steelers don't seem too worried about it ruining their season. Aaron Rodgers handled a question about whether the loss would change the trajectory of the season with, "That's very dramatic," and head coach Mike McCarthy took a similar tack.

"It's one loss and it will be dealt with as such," McCarthy said, via the team's site. "I'm more focused on the details of the fundamentals and the things we needed to do. We're not going to drive down the drama road. It's a waste of time. It's a waste of energy. We're going to pour our energy into ourselves, look in the mirror and stay focused on the things that we can control."

Cincinnati Bengals inside linebacker Oren Burks brings down Pittsburgh Steelers running back Jaylen Warren.

That's a fine approach, because there's nothing else McCarthy can say. But the Steelers have some real concerns. They came into the season tied for the oldest roster in the NFL, with many older veterans starting with 42-year-old quarterback Aaron Rodgers. It's a long season and the Steelers have to worry about injury and fatigue setting in. The Steelers have gotten very little out of their receivers this season, which limits the explosiveness of the offense, and while the defense has been good it was picked apart by Joe Burrow.

But it's not time to get too worried. The Steelers had shown significant improvement before having a horrendous night in Cincinnati. That isn't all erased, nor are six wins in what is becoming a very soft AFC wild-card race. Rodgers was good enough with the limitations at receiver through eight games and while Sunday night started with him throwing incomplete on his first seven passes for the first time in his career, there haven't been signs of a steep decline before Sunday night. It could happen in the second half of the season but there's no clear and imminent danger of it.

Good teams can have bad games. The Bengals are hot. It happens. As McCarthy said, there's no reason to go down a drama road. The Steelers should be fine. But that's a little less certain after a truly miserable night in Cincinnati.

Panic meter: Let's not give up on the Steelers yet, but remember Sunday night as a turning point if things do go south.

When Drake Maye was sacked by Aidan Hutchinson, Patriots and Lions fans had no idea both teams' seasons were changing on that play.

The Patriots are concerned that Maye fractured his ankle, and we'll get to Hutchinson in a bit. It appears Maye is either going to miss significant time or the rest of the season. That's terrible news for the 5-5 Patriots less than a week after the trade deadline passed. Jimmy Garoppolo's name has been thrown around but realistically, it's hard to find any quarterback who is still unsigned in mid-November to save the season.

Former Patriots quarterback Matt Cassel, who is 44 years old, had some jokes.

It might not be that much worse than Tommy DeVito, who was awful in relief of Maye. He had 81 yards on 24 attempts. The Patriots had 91 yards on 11 possessions after Maye's injury. It was unwatchable football. And that's likely to be the case for the rest of the season.

Panic meter: Hey Patriots fans, at least the Celtics and Bruins are good.

Aidan Hutchinson was excited for a third-down sack on Drake Maye, so he jumped up and the landing might have ruined the Lions' Super Bowl hopes.

Hutchinson tore his ACL on the celebration, a brutal injury for Hutchinson and Detroit as well. Detroit was already without standout linebacker Jack Campbell. Hutchinson's knee injury removes the two best players from a defense that wasn't very good with them.

The Lions are 7-2 and Detroit fans will point to 2024, when the team went 15-2 after Hutchinson broke his leg. Perhaps they can get on a roll while being shorthanded. But realistically and unfortunately, the ceiling for the Lions was lowered with the injury.

Panic meter: The Lions shouldn't fall apart, but that celebration was a turning point in their season.

Everyone will have issues with the Vikings' blitz-happy defense this season. The Packers certainly have. They allowed eight sacks on Sunday and 12 in two meetings against the Vikings. While that's certainly too many, Green Bay has allowed only 13 sacks in their other eight games.

The problem is that the next opponent, after the bye, is Myles Garrett, Aaron Donald and the Rams. There's a lot of work to be done before that game the day before Thanksgiving.

The Packers lost 30-10 to the Vikings and they obviously haven't matched up well with Minnesota this season. But Green Bay was playing very well before Sunday's game and are still in fine shape in the NFC wild-card race.

Panic meter: As long as Brian Flores isn't on the other sideline, Green Bay should be fine.

The Texans' season can be viewed in an optimistic way, if you wish. Five of their losses this season have come on a drive by the opponent in the final minutes of regulation or in overtime. Close games like that should be close to 50/50 propositions, so with just a little more luck Houston would be right in the playoff picture.

But maybe it's not just luck. The Texans' defense, which was supposed to be good this season, has failed repeatedly when asked to close out a game. And regardless of how the Texans got to 3-6, that's where they are. And they have lost a key tiebreaker to the Browns, who are currently the No. 7 seed in the AFC.

Houston's playoff odds are 34.4% via DVOA, which seems high for a team three games under .500. That's because the AFC's wild-card race isn't very good. But, maybe the Texans aren't very good either.

Panic meter: The talent is still there, but the season is slipping away fast with bad losses.

#2709. 4 Downs: Raiders' Second-half collapse leads to frustrating Loss against seahawks

On Sunday, the Las Vegas Raiders had the opportunity to prove themselves against the Seattle Seahawks. The game was held in Las Vegas; the team's rookie Fernando Menodoza started at quarterback. It was his first NFL start. The Raiders went into the locker room at halftime up by three points. For the first two quarters, it seemed as though the Raiders would finally deliver the performance they were looking for.

Instead, the Raiders collapsed in the second half, giving the Seahawks a chance to pull off a 21-13 win. After several drives stalled, the Raiders also allowed a couple of big gains by the Seahawks, and their ground attack continues to struggle to create opportunities. With the defeat, the Raiders dropped to 4-6, and head coach Klint Kubiak is looking for solutions.

The top pick quarterback was not expected to fix every one of the Raiders' offensive problems in his first NFL start. However, he provided some hope for fans to look forward to, particularly in the first half against the Seattle Seahawks.

Mendoza helped Las Vegas get an early start when they had a long 11-play, 46-yard scoring drive that ended with Mendoza running for 10 yards into the end zone. Mendoza seemed very relaxed outside the pocket. He also didn't seem intimidated by the situation. Both are good signs for a young quarterback being forced into a tough situation.

He completed 17 of 29 passes for 143 yards with zero interceptions and ran for 22 yards. While he did not turn over the ball, Seattle made it very difficult for him after halftime by sacking him six times and limiting his time to complete his progressions.

While Mendoza may need to focus on identifying pressure and throwing the ball away, his supporting cast didn't help him much. The offensive line was weak, the receivers couldn't get open consistently, and the rushing attack didn't help.

Whatever the Raiders did well on offense in the first half, they could not maintain that in the second half. At halftime, the Raiders were up 10-7 and seemed to have a strategy for dealing with the Seahawks' defense. However, as soon as the Seahawks began to put increased pressure on the Raiders, their strategy fell apart. The result was just 27 yards from scrimmage and made five consecutive three-and-outs.

Perhaps the biggest blow to the offense was Brock Bowers going out with an ankle injury. Bowers had just two catches for 18 yards before departing. After the All-Pro tight end left, it was hard for the Raiders' passing attack to get going.

For most of the first half, the Raiders limited Smith-Njigba to one catch for 11 yards before the half. However, this didn't hold up.

Smith-Njigba dominated in the second half with 160 receiving yards. The biggest moment came when Drew Lock escaped the defensive line and threw a 71-yard touchdown pass to Smith-Njigba. This score put Seattle up 21-10, completely shifting the game's momentum.

The All-Pro wide receiver finished with 8 receptions for 171 receiving yards and 1 touchdown. On the other hand, Lock completed 14 of 17 passes for 240 yards and 2 touchdowns.

Jeanty had another subdued performance, running for only 18 yards on nine attempts. While adding 46 yards on four catches.

As a team, the Raiders rushed for 65 yards on 24 attempts, averaging 2.7 yards. This production isn't enough for an offense trying to support a young quarterback. The Seahawks controlled the line of scrimmage, giving Jeanty and others fewer opportunities to run.

The offensive line is still a big issue for this team. Along with allowing six sacks and 12 QB hits, Seattles defenders consistently got into the backfield before the play could develop. Jeanty has had trouble finding good running lanes, and the issues in front of him make it hard to tell what he can do.

The hardest thing about Sunday's loss was not that it was by eight points. It was the way the Raiders went from controlling the game to being completely outplayed. At halftime, Las Vegas led 10-7, had forced two turnovers, and had the opportunity to defeat the current Super Bowl Champion in their own stadium.

Seattle outplayed Las Vegas in total yards (382-163) and was much stronger in the second half. Las Vegas had been successful on third downs early in the game, converting on seven of their first ten third-down opportunities; however, they did not convert on any third-down opportunity in the second half. The RRaiders'offense could not stay on the field, which the defense paid for in the end.

Embiid and James listed as questionable for Sixers vs. Raptors originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

#2707. Los Angeles Chargers news: Bolts’ commitment to Omarion Hampton apparent in 1st win

The Chargers began the season with Omarion Hampton as their lead back, but the second-year runner lost two fumbles in as many games to start the campaign and that quickly led the Bolts to a three-headed committee for the next month-plus.

In recent weeks, Hampton's been garnering a larger share of the backfield touches, but Monday night's performance was much more of what we all expected to be the norm week-in and week-out. Hampton carried the football more than 20 times and finished with 105 yards and a score, his first 100-yard game since Week 4 of last year. He looked strong, locked-in, and overall more comfortable as the game went on the more he touched the football.

This success on the ground or Hampton also coincided with a shift in the run game, going away from zone and more into gap and power schemes. Per James Koh of 97.1 The Fan in Los Angeles, the Chargers ran zone 53 percent of the time from Weeks One through Nine, but that number dropped to just 38 percent against the Ravens.

The shift away from zone for Hampton has been discussed at length this season, noting his success in gap schemes dating back to his time at North Carolina. On one of his best runs of the night, the Bolts pulled left tackle Joe Alt across the formation to kick out a defender in the hole and Hampton cut right off his backside for a gain of 19 to begin the second half.

Overall the Chargers ran for 164 yards on 34 carries, aided by Justin Herbert's 51 yards off scrambles, to end the night averaging a strong 4.7 yards per tote.

Is this the new recipe moving forward for the Chargers? I wouldn't be shocked in the slightest, if so.

#2706. ‘Hard to win': Simons, Walker carry Portland lessons into Sixers roles

'Hard to win': Simons, Walker carry Portland lessons into Sixers roles originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Anfernee Simons and Jabari Walker shared the floor for 128 games in Portland. The Trail Blazers won 43 of them.

They sat beside one another in the Sixers' interview room Thursday night after a victory and discussed what the other 85 taught them.

"Whether you've got a big role or a small role, it's hard to win in the NBA," Simons said. "We've seen it firsthand."

Their roles were considerably different then.

Simons was an established starter entrusted with creating and scoring. Walker, a 2022 second-round pick, was trying to establish himself in a rotation. During their three overlapping seasons, Portland went 33-49, 21-61 and 36-46. The Blazers missed the playoffs each year.

Both are now reserves on a Sixers team constructed to contend. In nine games they've both appeared in this season, Philadelphia is 7-2.

They provided 26 points, 11 rebounds and six three-pointers in the Sixers' 123-112 win over the Raptors at Xfinity Mobile Arena. Simons scored 17 points and shot 5 for 6 from three-point range. Walker recorded nine points and eight rebounds, including four on the offensive glass.

The Sixers outscored Toronto by 24 points during Simons' 23 minutes. Neither former Blazer committed a turnover.

Their production helped Philadelphia overcome a 17-point first-quarter deficit and improve to 10-3 without LeBron James.

"You learn once you come off the bench to kind of read the game before you get in there," Simons said. "Obviously, we started off kind of slow. You just want to go in, bring some energy, get some stops, get out and run. I think everybody who got off the bench brought energy."

Simons supplied an immediate lift with a deep three-pointer at the first-quarter buzzer. His shot ended an 8-0 Sixers run and trimmed Toronto's lead to nine.

He remained involved during an 11-0 burst late in the third quarter, knocking down a three and setting up Walker for a basket. Walker then made a three early in the fourth before Simons scored on the Sixers' next possession.

When Toronto pulled within six with less than five minutes remaining, Simons answered with his fifth three-pointer.

Making those shots is the most obvious way Simons can help a roster already featuring James, Joel Embiid, Tyrese Maxey and Jaylen Brown. He said the Sixers have not made him feel as though every opportunity must go in.

"They encourage us to keep shooting the ball," Simons said. "Knowing that a team has that much confidence in us to keep shooting makes the game that much easier. Whether you miss or make, as long as we get the right shots in the right moments, I think everybody can live with the misses and makes."

Simons spent seven seasons in Portland before playing last year with Boston and Chicago. His move to Philadelphia reunited him with Walker and Rayan Rupert, another former Blazer who joined the Sixers on a two-way contract during the offseason.

Knowing familiar faces awaited him helped Simons enter another new locker room.

"It made the transition seamless," he said. "Just being able to have some comfortability with guys that I played with for multiple years. It's been fun, kind of feeling like a little bit of old times. Me, Bari and Rups have been through a lot."

Walker's path to Philadelphia began a year earlier and required an additional step.

After playing 188 games over three seasons with Portland, Walker signed a two-way contract with the Sixers in July 2025. He earned a standard contract in February and has since pushed his way into the backup-center conversation despite standing 6-foot-7.

Walker believes some of the work he did on losing teams was easily missed.

"When I first got to the league, I was part of a rebuilding process," he said. "I just felt like some of the little things I did didn't always get noticed when you're losing. I told myself, if I ever got to be part of a winning organization, then I'd go out and expand my abilities."

Rebounding has made Walker difficult for Nick Nurse to overlook.

Walker credited his timing and strong hands to lessons passed down by his father, former NBA center Samaki Walker. Those instincts have become especially useful while the Sixers piece together their frontcourt behind Embiid.

"Rebounding is a skill I kind of honed in on," Walker said. "My dad was a good rebounder. He was known for his rebounding and his timing. I think it's just a gift that he transferred to me."

Walker has made it clear that he expected to earn a role this season.

"I don't know if I was delusional, but I always felt like I was going to play and I was going to be a part," he said. "I read something where they said it's a surprise, but if you were around me this summer, it wouldn't be a surprise. This is expected, and I just want to continue to get better."

The freedom Simons once had in Portland is no longer necessary. Walker does not need a high volume of touches, either. Both understand that playing beside the Sixers' stars requires shooting when open, moving the ball and handling the less glamorous work.

"You've got to find ways to impact the game multiple ways, even if you're not scoring," Simons said. "Whether it's cutting, making the right read and pass to Joel, being the right swing pass to VJ, boxing out on defense or just being in the right position."

Their opportunities will fluctuate. Simons may take nine shots one night and only a few the next. Walker's minutes will depend on matchups and the health of Philadelphia's frontcourt.

Winning has changed what they are being asked to do. Their three years together in Portland taught them why the adjustment is worthwhile.

"You feel appreciative of the times where you go into a situation where your role might be a little bit less, but you're winning," Simons said. "That's something you can't take for granted."

#2705. Green Bay Packers News: Packers recovering from a meltdown

You never want to overstate things if you don't have to, but it's hard to avoid hyperbolic language after a full collapse like we saw on Sunday.

The offensive line was never really together to begin with, but even in its decimated state, it was especially bad against the relentless Minnesota Vikings.

And even accounting for the offensive line weakness, the passing game was nothing to write home about. The Packers' dynamic duo of wide receivers was largely neutralized, and Jordan Love missed badly on a red zone throw, resulting in an interception.

Meanwhile, on defense, the Packers were largely solid — except on third downs. Two massive conversions on third and long helped propel the Vikings toward their eventual victory, and the Packers were more or less just passive observers.

But aside from that, things were…well, they were still bad. A win, no matter how ugly or undeserved, was the only worthwhile outcome in this game. When you're scratching and clawing for divisional progress, there are no moral victories, and the gap between 7-3 and 6-4 in the win/loss column is massive, especially with the Rams and Bills looming after the Packers' bye week.

It was a terrible, horrible, no good, very bad weekend for your Green Bay Packers, and there's a lot of work to be done to get back on the right track.

#2704. Arthur Fils comments ahead of facing Alexander Zverev at the ATP Finals: ‘I am not afraid’

Arthur Fils defeated Rafael Jodar, 4-6, 6-2, 6-2, at the ATP Finals.

The win kept Fils in the hunt for an ATP Finals semi-final spot, following his defeat to Ben Shelton in his opening match. The Frenchman will return to play the world number one, Alexander Zverev, in his final group stage match later this week.

After picking up his maiden ATP Finals win, Fils delivered his verdict on the match itself before looking ahead to his eighth career meeting with Zverev.

First, Fils shared his thoughts on the win against Jodar. The 22-year-old dropped the first set, but battled back to secure the win in a decider, taking advantage of his opponent's physical struggles.

"It means a lot to me after a tough start. I played a great match against Ben [Shelton], actually; just unlucky. The first set here was a bit so-so. I was serving with a bit of frustration but I am very happy with the way I handled the second and the serve and some relief now," said Fils.

Arthur Fils vs Rafael Jodar – Match stats

Fils then looked ahead to Thursday's match against Zverev, who won his opening match against Jodar in straight sets and is set to play Shelton in Tuesday's evening session at the Inalpi Arena.

"It is going to be tough, of course. But I know that I can battle; I can go deep in the battle, and I don't care about long matches. I am not afraid. It is going to be a nice match against the number one in the world; I am just going to enjoy myself. I don't have anything to lose," he said.

Fils doesn't think he has anything to lose, but he certainly has a lot to gain. A win against Zverev could qualify him for the ATP Finals' last four. He currently sits top of the 'John McEnroe Group', although he will drop to second after Zverev and Shelton play their match.

No Frenchman has won the ATP Finals singles title since the event began in 1970. To change that, Fils will need to be at his best against Zverev, an opponent he has struggled to beat.

Through seven matches, Zverev leads his head-to-head with Fils, 5-2. The German won their first two meetings in Germany before Fils won the Hamburg Open final in 2024.

2023 Hamburg Open SF – Alexander Zverev defeated Arthur Fils, 6-2, 6-4

2024 Halle Open QF – Alexander Zverev defeated Arthur Fils, 6-7, 6-3, 6-4

2024 Hamburg Open F – Arthur Fils defeated Alexander Zverev, 6-3, 3-6, 7-6

2024 Paris Masters 3R – Alexander Zverev defeated Arthur Fils, 6-4, 3-6, 6-3

2025 Miami Open 4R – Arthur Fils defeated Alexander Zverev, 3-6, 6-3, 6-4

2025 Italian Open 4R – Alexander Zverev defeated Arthur Fils, 7-6, 6-1

2026 Indian Wells QF – Alexander Zverev defeated Arthur Fils, 6-2, 6-3

Zverev then won in Paris, and Fils won in Miami as the pair continued to contest high-quality three-set matches. The world number one has taken control recently, though, beating Fils in straight sets at the 2025 Italian Open and at Indian Wells this year. Only time will tell if Fils will fare better when they play at the year-end championships.

#2703. Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid

Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Jaylen Brown-Tyrese Maxey duo starred, LeBron James reached another milestone, and the Sixers earned a win without Joel Embiid on Sunday afternoon.

They improved to 11-3 on the season with a 131-124 victory over the Pacers at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Brown scored 31 points and Maxey posted 27.

LeBron James had 15 points and 11 assists. He passed Jason Kidd on Sunday for third in NBA history with 12,092 assists.

Pascal Siakam scored 31 points for the 5-9 Pacers.

On the first day of a back-to-back, the Sixers were down Embiid (right foot plantar fascia injury management), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pacers' injured players included Tyrese Haliburton and Andrew Nembhard.

The Sixers will face the Nets on Monday night in Brooklyn. Here are observations on their win over the Pacers:

Jabari Walker started for the first time this season, opening the game at center. He's been in the Sixers' rotation the past five games since Bona's injury.

Walker set up the Sixers' first basket, swiping a steal and then tossing the ball ahead to Maxey for a fast-break dunk. Maxey and Brown both began well and combined to score all of the Sixers' first 15 points.

Though he conceded five inches to 7-foot Pacers center Ivica Zubac, Walker was solid in his first stint. He got an early post stop on Zubac and then snatched a strong defensive rebound.

Dean Wade was next up for the Sixers at the five. He sunk a three-pointer on his first shot to give the team a 20-16 lead.

Walker ended up playing 25 minutes and posting 11 points and seven rebounds. Going back to the preseason, his stock has steadily risen.

"Here we are again after another game saying Jabari played very well," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He plays hard, he rebounds, he's got another level of confidence with his shooting this year.

"It's kind of wash, rinse and repeat again with him (today). He was great. … Jabari went in there and battled (Zubac). I thought he certainly matched him, if not outplayed him, which is a heck of a job."

Wade had 12 points and seven boards in 35 minutes. Overall, the Sixers' lack of healthy conventional centers is still a clear deficiency. Of course, everything should be simpler once Embiid is back in action.

The Sixers' defense did not have a sparkling start. The Pacers tallied 35 points in the first quarter and shot 62.5 percent from the field.

Brown helped the Sixers avoid a substantial deficit by draining all sorts of jumpers. He rarely appears bothered by close contests. Brown moved to 7 for 9 from the floor when he knocked down a three early in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope's triple knotted the contest up at 37-all.

Nurse needed to stray from his Plan A rotation when VJ Edgecombe picked up his third foul with 9:56 left in the second quarter. Labaron Philon Jr., who got a game with the G League's Delaware Blue Coats on Friday night, subbed in.

For a rookie guard, Philon has looked comfortable and confident on offense in most of his NBA appearances. He swished a long-range jumper in the second quarter and saw a floater rim out.

Brown reached the 20-point mark and put the Sixers up one with a slam assisted by James late in the second quarter.

He ran into serious foul trouble in the third quarter. Brown was whistled for his fourth foul with 9:53 left in the period for an off-arm shove on Aaron Nesmith. Wade entered and Brown exited.

The Sixers' slim lead didn't slip away. Walker nailed threes on consecutive possessions and Maxey jammed in a dunk.

Soon after, Tacko Fall came in for his first meaningful minutes as a Sixer. The 7-foot-6 two-way contract player grabbed an offensive board off a missed Edgecombe free throw, which drew one of the day's loudest cheers. With Fall on the floor, Maxey and James connected on an alley-oop that extended the Sixers' lead to 82-73.

When the Sixers tried to sit in a zone, Fall was called for a defensive three-second violation. Whatever the scheme, most of Fall's defensive work will come around the paint. He certainly has exceptional physical traits as a rim protector and is not nimble on the perimeter.

Fall logged five scoreless third-quarter minutes and recorded three rebounds. The Sixers outscored Indiana by a point with Fall on the court. Given Fall is occupying a (two-way) roster slot and the Sixers are so depleted at center, it made sense to see a bit of what he can do outside of garbage time. While five minutes isn't enough for any grand conclusions, it's very safe to say Fall is a unique player.

"I'm 7-6, so I can change the game in so many ways," Fall said. "Offensively, setting screens, making sure guys get open, being an easy target for lobs. Defensively, I feel like I've always been an elite rim protector and I've worked on my body a lot to be able to move a lot better."

Nurse praised Fall's outing, saying he "gave us some great minutes and a nice lift."

Fall is appreciative of his first NBA opportunity since the 2021-22 season.

"I've worked very hard," he said. "God has brought me back here and I'm thankful. And I'm ready. Whenever my name is called, I'm going to go out there and do my job."

The Sixers built their advantage to 102-87 with a Brown three early in the fourth quarter. They couldn't cruise to a blowout, though.

Siakam kept hurting the Sixers as a driver and had a highly efficient fourth quarter, scoring 19 points on 7-for-9 shooting. Quenton Jackson's layup trimmed Indiana's deficit to 119-115.

The Sixers ultimately held off the Pacers' push.

Caldwell-Pope canned a three, Wade threw down an and-one dunk, and James checked off his milestone assist with a late dish to Edgecombe for a layup.

#2701. Vikes Views: Nincompoop of the Week: Week 10 – Vikings at Packers

The Minnesota Vikings dismantled the Green Bay Packers, 30-10. Even in a 20-point blowout at Lambeau Field, somebody still earns the crown. Let's take a look at who was a Nincompoop this week.

The Vikings defense is going to make a lot of offensive lines look bad. The Packers offensive line was also missing a few key players. However, there are no excuses for what happened on Sunday. There were plays where four or five Vikings were in the backfield almost immediately. Jordan Love had no chance on seemingly half of his dropbacks. The eight sacks allowed were the most surrendered by any team in the NFL this season.

One of the reasons the Vikings were able to generate so much pressure was the Packers' game plan. The Vikings defense is susceptible to the run game, yet Green Bay never seriously tested them. Maybe that's because the Packers have one of the worst rushing attacks in football. They had some success when they ran the ball, but they abandoned it way too early. When your quarterback is getting battered and you keep asking him to throw deep, the results shouldn't be surprising.

If it wasn't for his 24-yard reception, Felton would have contributed negative yards to the offense. Two pre-snap penalties from a receiver already struggling to find his rhythm didn't help matters. Tai has just 99 yards in 26 career games. Felton didn't do anything obviously wrong, he'll just need to do a lot more if he wants to remain a regular part of the offense.

An early missed field goal, numerous penalties, and a late fumble on special teams didn't help the Packers' chances on Sunday. They were sloppy from start to finish. On a day when the Vikings' special teams unit was nearly perfect, Green Bay's special teams played a major role in the team's downfall.

Not a name I ever expected to put on this list. Darrisaw allowed another two sacks on Sunday, bringing his total to a career-high eight sacks allowed this season. The Vikings finally have competent quarterback play again, and the last thing they need is their franchise left tackle allowing defenders free shots at Kirk Cousins. Darrisaw is struggling to regain his form after a few injury-plagued seasons.

My vote goes to Matt LaFleur. I'd probably go LaFleur, Pack OL, Darrisaw, Pack Special Teams, and Tai Felton if I had to rank them.

#2700. Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn

Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers will arrive in Brooklyn with one victory already secured on their back-to-back and a different combination of stars likely available for the second.

Philadelphia improved to 11-3 with a 131-124 win over the Pacers on Sunday. Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 31 points, Tyrese Maxey added 27 and LeBron James recorded 11 assists to move past Jason Kidd for third in NBA history.

James has not played both ends of a back-to-back this season, making it reasonable to expect him to sit Monday night. Joel Embiid, meanwhile, rested Sunday and is expected to return against Brooklyn. The Sixers had not submitted their official injury report as of Monday morning.

The Nets enter at 3-9, but this is not the same roster that finished 20-62 last season. Before the Sixers attempt to win twice in two nights, here is a closer look at what has changed in Brooklyn and where Monday's matchup could turn.

Nic Claxton is now in Chicago. Julius Randle arrived from Minnesota as the central piece of a four-team trade. Keon Ellis and Moe Wagner joined in free agency, while Michael Porter Jr. remains one of Brooklyn's primary scoring options.

Porter is available after missing Saturday's game for personal reasons. He will give the Nets another shooter after Brooklyn struggled badly without him in a 129-110 loss to Memphis.

The Nets' rotation also includes a large group of young players. Egor Dëmin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr. have all received minutes in the backcourt, while Noah Clowney, Danny Wolf and Day'Ron Sharpe have played in the frontcourt.

The combination has produced uneven results. Brooklyn averages 110.2 points and allows 116.4 per game. Its minus-6.3 net rating ranks 23rd in the league.

Brooklyn has shown its offensive ceiling. The Nets scored 145 points in a 34-point win over New Orleans last week. Three nights later, they fell behind Memphis by 26 and lost by 19.

Jordi Fernandez has been direct about the difference between the team he sees in practice and the one that has appeared in games.

"We practice better than we play," the Nets head coach said after Saturday's loss. "It's a frustrating sign because you believe you're better."

The Nets did not merely miss shots against Memphis. Their problems began with their approach.

"We didn't punch first," Randle said. "Just organization. I think maybe just trying too hard, playing a little unorganized, stuff like that. And then just attention to detail. I think we've got a lapse of focus sometimes."

Fernandez was even more direct when discussing his young guards.

"They've got to grow fast," he said. "Being 20 is not an excuse."

That inexperience has appeared in Brooklyn's ball control. The Nets' offensive turnover rate ranks 28th in the league.

They have been much more disruptive on the other end. Brooklyn has forced turnovers on 13.9 percent of its opponents' possessions, tied for seventh.

The Sixers forced 10 Indiana turnovers and converted them into 19 points Sunday. Philadelphia committed 13 total turnovers while playing at the quick tempo it has emphasized since training camp.

Brooklyn has enough length and athleticism to disrupt the Sixers. It has not consistently maintained its own organization after creating that pressure.

Brooklyn has allowed 14.2 three-pointers per game, the eighth-most in the NBA. Opponents have posted a 55.2 effective field-goal percentage against the Nets.

That presents another opportunity for a Sixers team receiving strong outside shooting from several role players.

Anfernee Simons ranks third in the league with 33 three-pointers off the bench and is shooting 41.8 percent from deep. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope has made 46.5 percent of his threes, while Dean Wade is at 41.4 percent.

Maxey made five threes against Indiana and Brown added three. Jabari Walker also continued his strong start by making three of his six attempts from deep.

Those contributions matter on the second night of a back-to-back, especially if James sits. Philadelphia should not need Maxey and Brown to create every possession from a standstill if its shooters continue punishing open space.

Embiid's expected return would change the shape of Philadelphia's offense after James served as its primary organizer Sunday.

Brooklyn no longer has Claxton to use against Embiid. Sharpe has moved into a larger role and is coming off 16 points and 11 rebounds in Memphis. Wagner, Clowney and Randle give Fernandez several other bodies to try, but none presents the same defensive profile as Claxton.

Sharpe's work on the glass deserves attention. The Sixers have survived significant rebounding deficits this season, but allowing Brooklyn extra possessions would give Porter and Randle more chances to settle into the game.

On the other end, Embiid should have opportunities to force Brooklyn's frontcourt into difficult decisions. Sending help would create openings for Philadelphia's shooters. Defending him individually would leave Sharpe, Wagner and Clowney with a demanding assignment.

The Sixers have already won three games without James. Monday could require them to do it again, this time with Embiid returning as the center of the offense.

Brooklyn's record suggests a favorable matchup. Its shooting and young talent make it less comfortable than the standings indicate.

The Sixers handled the first half of their back-to-back by producing a season-high 131 points. Finishing the sweep will require them to adjust to a different available roster while preventing an inconsistent Nets team from finding one of its explosive nights.

#2699. Through 10 weeks, the AFC playoff field is basically set

The 2021 expansion of the regular season to 17 games has given the bad teams more time to separate from the good teams. Which makes it easier for the eventual playoff teams to declare themselves.

This year, the seven AFC playoff teams have essentially declared themselves after only 10 weeks of games.

The most important developments in that regard were these: (1) the Browns beating the Texans; (2) the Ravens losing to the Chargers; and (3) the Patriots losing badly to the Lions — and losing quarterback Drake Maye, possibly for the rest of the season.

Already, we know that the Bills, Jaguars, Bengals, Broncos, and Chiefs are essentially locks to win tickets to the postseason party. The Steelers, despite being outclassed by the Bengals, have enough winnable games remaining to land a wild-card berth.

The only spot up for grabs is the seven seed. And the Browns are now in the driver's seat.

Already, four AFC teams are dead in the water: the 2-8 Jets, the bill1-8 Dolphins, the 1-8 Titans, and the 1-8 Chargers. The 3-7 Colts barely have a pulse, and it won't last long. That's five of 16 teams, with a fork already stuck in a spot where forks aren't supposed to go. And the 3-6 Texans aren't far behind.

The remaining contenders for the last spot in the playoff tree are the 6-4 Browns, the 5-5 Patriots, the 4-6 Raiders, and the 4-6 Ravens. New England could be in the process of imploding. The Raiders are 1-6 over their last seven games, and they're now starting a rookie quarterback. The Ravens have lost five in a row.

As a result, there's currently not much drama as to who the seven AFC playoff teams will be.

The good news is that the top of the seven-team hierarchy remains undecided — and it could stay that way for a while. The Bills, Jaguars, and Bengals stand apart from the rest. The Broncos and Chiefs remain in the mix. (The Steelers seem to be destined for another one-and-done postseason appearance, with a road game looming to Buffalo, Jacksonville, Cincinnati, or Denver/Kansas City.)

The bad news, and the inescapable reality, is that the have-nots have become crystal clear. With eight weeks of the season left.

For a league that loves parity, there isn't much of it when it comes to the line separating the playoff teams from the wait-til-next-year franchises.

#2698. 'God damnit ChatGPT': Bilal Hasan reacts to UFC 334 upset loss

Bilal Hasan appears to be taking his first-career loss in stride.

The biggest betting favorite of the night, Hasan (10-1 MMA, 1-1 UFC) lost a split decision to Luis Gurule (12-4 MMA, 2-4 UFC) in this past Saturday's UFC 334 main card opener at Madison Square Garden in New York.

During the pre-fight press conference, Hasan played ChatGPT's prediction for their fight out loud, which read first-round knockout for "The IndoNinja." While Hasan was in a flow state early, Gurule smothered him on the ground to take the win.

Hasan let ChatGPT know that it failed him.

The 25-year-old, who had finished his past nine opponents in a row, then released an extensive statement reflecting on the loss.

"Alhamdulillah, what an absolute privilege it was to perform in the world's most famous arena 🙏🏽

Not the result we wanted, but my goal as a fighter was never to be undefeated. It was to compete at the highest level, perform to the best of my abilities, and continue to grow as a martial artist.

Thank you to the @ufc for giving me the platform to fully express my art. And a massive thank you to my coaches and teammates @charliescombatclub and @ryantheliondiaz for your time, dedication, and sacrifice. I truly wouldn't be here without you guys. 🫡🫶🏽

Can't wait to get back in the lab 🤼 and see what's next!

I'm truly living the dream.Thank you for all the love and support! Aku cinta kalian semua ❤️🇮🇩🥷."

#2697. Sixers, Brown have a ‘humbling' night in loss to Celtics

Sixers, Brown have a 'humbling' night in loss to Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Whatever Jaylen Brown and the Sixers dreamed up for their first matchup against the Celtics soon flew out the window on Tuesday night.

Brown was whistled for two early fouls, the Sixers fell behind and their early-season flaws were on full display in a 123-101 loss at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

"We've just got to get better," Brown said. "Tonight was humbling. We got outplayed, out-executed. So we go back to the drawing board and get ready for the next one."

Over 26 minutes, Brown had 13 points on 4-for-9 shooting and five rebounds against the team that traded him away this summer.

As for the endless dissection of his relationship with former co-star Jayson Tatum, Brown didn't have much to add Tuesday. There was no public reconciliation, no obvious on-court animosity and generally nothing to satisfy fans hoping for some kind of fireworks on that front.

"I'm just focused on doing what's best for my team," Brown said. "Everything else … to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson; he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics. They executed, they shot the hell out of the ball and they were the better team tonight."

Tatum scored 21 points, two of which came on a third-quarter slam over LeBron James. Paul George continued his fantastic start as a Celtic by dropping 36 points and draining six three-pointers.

The Celtics dominated from beyond the arc, shooting 22 for 49 (44.9 percent). The Sixers went just 11 for 37 (29.7 percent) on another night of underwhelming offense. The team sits 22nd in the NBA in offensive rating, 24th in three-point makes per game and 21st in three-point percentage.

"I think that we've got some guys that are obviously shooting much lower percentages than their last season or their career average — almost a handful of guys," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "And I don't think it's because they're shooting fallaway, totally contested ones. I think they're shots we want them to take. So I think they'll continue to take 'em and I'm pretty sure those will even out into their career averages."

Joel Embiid is at 37 percent from the floor and 4 for 33 from three-point range (12.1 percent).

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center got through an injury scare when he rolled his ankle late in the second quarter and hit a three early in the third. It was his first long-range make since the Sixers' home opener back on Oct. 22.

"It's a work in progress," Embiid said. "I think the biggest thing is just not thinking. I'm probably overthinking it. The rhythm of the game is different. I'm so used to having to create for myself, so it's a little bit different.

"But I think I'm slowly getting better. You could see as soon as I made a three, the next shot in the short corner … I didn't even think about it. I just shot it and let it fly, and I made it. … I think it's just about taking what's in front of you. Make or miss, keep working on your game. It's going to get there at some point."

Even with the Sixers second in the Eastern Conference standings at 9-3, Embiid believes the team's defense has "slacked off a little bit" in recent games.

"I don't really look at the record," he said. "I just look at the process of, 'Are we getting better every day?' We can always do a better job at all phases of the game."

"They're one of the top teams in the league and they've been together for a while," Brown said. "We've got a lot to work on over here, so we've got to get to work."

The Sixers will face the Raptors on Thursday night in Philadelphia.

They'll have to wait until Jan. 21 in Boston to see the Celtics again.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said. "I was looking forward to playing well, but s— happens. Just get ready for the next one."

#2696. Hertha BSC’s attacking numbers underline impressive start to 2. Bundesliga season

The Berliners have scored 25 goals so far, the joint-third highest total in the division, while their underlying numbers suggest their attacking success is built on more than simply clinical finishing.

According to Bundesliga Match Facts powered by AWS, Hertha have accumulated an expected goals (xGoals) rating of 22.9, the second-highest figure in the division. Only Heidenheim have generated a higher total, highlighting the quality of opportunities the capital club have consistently created.

Their shot efficiency rating of 2.1 also places them sixth in the division. Although several teams have been more clinical in front of goal, Hertha’s combination of regular chance creation and effective finishing has established them as one of the 2. Bundesliga’s most dangerous attacking sides.

One of the encouraging aspects of Hertha’s attacking development has been the contribution of different players throughout the squad, having relied heavily on individuals in years gone by.

Sebastian Grønning has provided an important focal point in the final third, while other members of the squad such as Oluwaseun Adewumi, Josip Brekalo and Mads Pedersen have also demonstrated their ability to contribute in attacking areas.

Rather than depending entirely on one goalscorer, Hertha have demonstrated an ability to find contributions from different areas of the pitch.

Their 5-3 victory over Eintracht Braunschweig provided an excellent example of that attacking depth. Despite falling two goals behind, Hertha responded with an impressive comeback, demonstrating their ability to create opportunities and punish opposition mistakes.

Their 2-1 victory over Wolfsburg before the break was another significant result, with Adewumi and Deyovaisio Zeefuik finding the net in front of a packed Olympiastadion. Against one of their promotion rivals, Hertha showed they could translate their attacking quality into important points.

The relationship between Hertha’s expected goals and actual goals scored is particularly interesting.

Their 22.9 xGoals figure indicates that they have consistently created opportunities worth more than 22 expected goals, while their 25 goals demonstrate that they have also managed to convert those chances effectively.

Although the difference suggests a degree of overperformance in front of goal, the gap is relatively modest compared to the overall volume of opportunities created.

This is important when assessing whether their current position is sustainable. Hertha are not simply benefiting from an unusually clinical spell; their underlying chance creation suggests they have the attacking foundations to remain competitive throughout the campaign.

Ranking second for xGoals, joint-third for goals scored and sixth for shot efficiency, they are performing strongly across all three attacking indicators.

For a club that has endured several frustrating years since dropping out of the Bundesliga in 2023, the current campaign represents a welcome change in fortunes.

Hertha have not only produced the results required to reach the summit but have also developed an attacking profile that compares favourably with the division’s strongest sides.

There is still a considerable amount of football to be played, and maintaining their current standards over an entire campaign will be the real challenge.

However, the numbers provide plenty of encouragement for supporters in the capital. With consistent chance creation, contributions from across the squad and an increasingly convincing attacking record, Hertha have established themselves as genuine contenders for promotion.

Their position at the top of 2. Bundesliga is no longer simply a reflection of a good run of results. The underlying statistics suggest there are solid reasons to believe their resurgence can continue.
